- 2026-09-06 (lane B, idea 72 ew-all-broad-as-the-simple-book) — **ANSWERED / KILL of the proposition: the simplest 4b-passing book is NOT the book to promote. It is the third of four candidates, it does not clear cross-universe 4b at PROTOCOL's own 10 bps, and it fails idea 65's cadence bar outright. No new KEEP, no RULES change; RULES.md, scan.py, bot.py and baseline.py untouched.** 180 grid points — 2 panels x 5 books x 3 cadences {D,W,M} x 2 gross conventions {LIT, MATCH} x 3 cost rungs {5,10,25} — **every one printed**; exactly two tuned dimensions (cadence, convention), with panel, book and cost reported at every value and never selected on. The four candidate books are imported verbatim from idea 89's audit module rather than re-implemented, and four gates passed before any new number was read: the analytic-cost identity vs `engine.backtest` at **0.000e+00**, idea 2's `u56/top20` at **12.660% / 1.0921 / -18.308%**, idea 72's `B136/EWall` at **10.716% / 1.0261 / -17.688%**, idea 57's `B136/ew-band3` at **11.106% / 1.0624 / -16.801%** and the live book at 6.453% / 0.6642 / -13.828%. **(1) THE ANSWER.** Cross-universe 4b — both panels passing at the SAME cadence, convention and cost rung, out of 18 — is `ew-band3` **8/18** > `frac085` 6/18 > **`EWall` 2/18** > `top20` 1/18 > v1 0/18, and **both of EWall's cells are at 5 bps**: at PROTOCOL's 10 bps it passes on B136 (10.72% / 1.026 / -17.69%, halves 1.146/0.914, OOS 1.019) and fails on u56 (10.40% / 1.049 / -15.87%, halves 1.068/1.034, OOS 1.112) on the **CAGR floor**, 10.40% against the 10.66% bar, short by 0.26pp. That is idea 82's 'EWall breaks even at no cost at all' reproduced on a second axis. **(2) Paired over all 36 (panel, cadence, convention, cost) cells, EWall LOSES to idea 57's band book on every axis** — dSharpe **-0.0681** (wins 12/36), dOOS Sharpe **-0.0827** (12/36), dCAGR -0.70pp, dMaxDD -0.66pp — while BEATING idea 2's standing KEEP on every risk-adjusted axis (dSharpe +0.0265, dOOS +0.0642, MaxDD shallower in **33/36**) at the price of 2.30pp of CAGR. Against `frac085` it is a wash (+0.0050 / +0.0112). **(3) Idea 65's bar is the cleanest kill.** SWING = max-min Sharpe over {D,W,M} within a cell: `ew-band3` **0.0363** (worst 0.0623) < `EWall` 0.1926 (worst 0.4112) < `frac085` 0.2384 < `top20` 0.3436 < v1 0.7139 — idea 3's ordering reproduces and extends to a second panel and three cost rungs — and cells passing 4b at ALL THREE cadences are `ew-band3` 4/12, `frac085` 2/12, `top20` 1/12, **`EWall` 0/12**. Idea 72's book passes 4b only on a cadence dial, which is exactly what idea 65's proposed bar was written to catch. **(4) Idea 81's gross correction, applied one day after it was established, is small here but never in the book's favour**: `top20` is the only one of the five under-grossed (mean realised **0.717 u56 / 0.739 B136** vs 0.749-0.751 for the rest — far milder than the 0.492 idea 73 found on STK20), so MATCH levers only it, by 1.015-1.046; the 4b verdict differs in **4 of 90** (panel, book, cadence, cost) cells and **all four are `top20` losing a pass on the DD cap** (u56 monthly at 5/10/25 bps, B136 weekly at 5). Sharpe invariance holds as an identity at **4.44e-16** (idea 66), so the entire effect lands on 4b's two ABSOLUTE bars — the half of 4b a gross mismatch corrupts. **(5) Rule 8** (book AND cadence chosen on 2009-2016 only, 2017-2026 read ONCE): the IS chooser picks `EWall` in **0 of 12** cells and picks the **MONTHLY** cadence in **12 of 12**. u56 -> `frac085/M`, OOS 14.32% / **1.236** / -18.84% against the do-nothing `EWall/W` control's 11.33% / 1.112 / -15.87% (regret -0.124); B136 -> `top20/M`, OOS 15.67% / 1.007 / **-26.10%** against the control's 10.58% / 1.019 / -17.69% (regret **+0.012** — the control wins). The control beats the pick in 4 of 12, mean regret -0.0704: selection beats doing nothing on average and pays 8.4pp of extra OOS drawdown on the broad list for it, which is idea 64's monthly-drawdown warning arriving through the selector. SPY OOS 0.882; v1 OOS 0.747 (u56) / 0.576 (B136). **(6) Idea 155's rankability correction is third-decimal on both panels** — B136/EWall 1.0261 -> **1.0253** (its published pair, exactly), B136/ew-band3 1.0624 -> 1.0617, u56/EWall 1.0490 -> 1.0491, u56/ew-band3 1.1348 -> 1.1356 — so the EWall-vs-ranked gap measured here is ranking, not coverage. **(7) KEEP paths over all 180 points**: 4a 68/180, 4b 66/180; by book 4b `ew-band3` 22/36 > `frac085` 18/36 > `EWall` 14/36 > `top20` 10/36 > v1 2/36; first-failing bar **CAGR 76**, H2 56, DD 54, H1 50, OOS 50 — it is the CAGR floor, not drawdown, that kills the un-ranked book. All 6 pre-registered predictions hit. **Nothing is promoted**: `ew-band3` finishes first on every axis in this run but is already idea 57's standing candidate, so no new KEEP is claimed. Caveats carried: current-constituent survivorship (idea 54) flatters `EWall` MORE than the ranked books — it holds exactly the beaten-down cohort a delisting-aware panel would remove — so this KILL is conservative and is not manufactured by the bias; MaxDD is a single realised extremum (idea 117); MATCH re-levers with a full-sample constant and is a diagnostic convention, not a tradable rule; costs are flat linear bps, not spread-and-impact (idea 126); the small panel is excluded (ideas 39/49/136). Result memo: `research/backtests/2026-09-06_ew-all-broad-as-the-simple-book_B.result.md`. Script: `research/backtests/2026-09-06_ew-all-broad-as-the-simple-book_B.py`.
- 2026-09-06 (lane B, idea 83 dispersion-as-a-survivorship-detector) — **ANSWERED, and the QUEUE's hypothesis is REFUTED: the premise holds, the inference does not. KILL of the 'dispersion is a survivorship thermometer' reading. No RULES change, no new KEEP-candidate, no memo; nothing in RULES.md, scan.py, bot.py or baseline.py touched.** Idea 78's seeds, draws, panel, gate, gross, cadence and costs were IMPORTED unchanged so the analysis sits on the table it analyses: all 150 sub-panels of B136 re-drawn from `SEED_B + k` and **all 450 books re-run** (150 CAND-5, 150 CAND-20, 150 EWall), every one reported. **Reproduction gate first**: U56/CAND20 at 12.6597% / 1.09214 / -18.3083% (halves 1.08828/1.10155), U56/RULES v1 at 6.4531% / 0.66418 / -13.8278%, and all 17 numeric columns of idea 78's committed `gridB.csv` over 300 rows at **8.3e-17 - 7.1e-15** with f4a/f4b verdict strings identical in **300/300**. Exactly two tuned parameters (k, n), both idea 78's; **no parameter of this run is tuned**. The control `W` is the mean ANNUALISED log return of a draw's own constituent names — name returns only, no book, no gate, no ranking, no costs — annualised over each name's OWN listed history, because 12 of B136's 136 columns list after the window opens (ABBV ANET AVGO META NOW PANW PLTR TSLA UBER XLC XLRE ZTS, plus the empty MMC) and they hold the panel's largest winners (PLTR +0.491/yr is the panel's best name); a common-window W silently drops all 13, i.e. under-measures winner-content on exactly the names that manufacture it. The common-window variant is reported alongside and agrees. **(1) THE PREMISE IS TRUE.** A wider draw is a more winning draw in every cell: Spearman(sd, W) pooled **+0.473** (k20 +0.495, k40 +0.442, k80 +0.661), Pearson **+0.567**, Spearman(sd, W_max) +0.522, and W on its own predicts draw Sharpe at R2 **0.155, t +5.22**. Nothing in the queue's mechanism is in dispute. **(2) THE INFERENCE IS FALSE, and the pre-registered bar (kill < 1/3 AND |t| < 2, written before the number was read) is missed on BOTH legs in EVERY cell.** n=20 pooled over 150 draws, CAND-20 Sharpe: R2(sd) 0.3062 (t +8.08) -> **partial R2(sd | W) 0.1899 (t +5.87)**, kill **0.620**; EWall 0.3437 (t +8.80) -> **0.2129 (t +6.30)**, kill 0.619. With the candidate count held EXACTLY at k, sd survives at |t|>2 in **3 of 3** k-cells for CAND-20 and **3 of 3** for EWall (2 of 3 for CAND-5), and `kill` never falls below 0.36 for a book Sharpe. Adding the draw's single best name W_max as a second control makes sd **STRONGER** (t +6.92, not weaker), and the common-window control gives the same answer (0.2147, t +6.34). Roughly **38%** of dispersion's univariate R2 is winner-content; 62% is not. **(3) THE CONDITIONING RUNS THE OTHER WAY.** With sd in the regression, W's own t falls from +5.22 to **+1.43** pooled and drops under 2 in 6 of the 8 CAND cells: dispersion explains winner-content better than winner-content explains dispersion. **(4) The queue's other leg — the RANKING PREMIUM — was already near-nil** and stays so: R2 0.0620 (t -3.13) -> 0.0295 (t -2.11); nothing there was contaminated because nothing was there. **(5) RULE 8 TESTS THE THERMOMETER'S OWN PREDICTION AND IT FAILS** (selectors fitted on 2009-2016 only, 2017-2026 read once; do-nothing = full B136 CAND-20 at OOS 12.49% / **0.892** / -20.05%; SPY OOS 0.882). If dispersion were a thermometer, the residualised column would keep the raw column's skill. **S5 RESID-DISP** (sd orthogonalised on W_IS) lands at 5.99% / **0.956** / -12.7%, regret **+0.064** and **z -0.31 BELOW its own cell mean** — it keeps **22%** of **S2 DISPERSION**'s +0.292 (9.03% / 1.184 / -9.4%, z +1.66). And **S6 WINNERNESS**, the pure survivorship proxy pointed straight at the thing the thermometer is supposed to be measuring, is the **only selector in the table that LOSES to doing nothing**: 6.06% / **0.775** / -12.0%, regret **-0.117**, z -1.86. Input skill vs OOS Sharpe over the 150 draws: IS Sharpe +0.217, IS sd **+0.194**, IS sd|W_IS +0.232, IS W_IS **+0.074**, IS n_elig -0.262. **(6) NOTHING IS PROMOTABLE, stated against the run's own best number.** S2's +0.292 is inside its cell's noise (cell sd 0.116) and S1, S2, S4, S5 and S6 all **fail 4b on the CAGR floor**; the only 4b pass is S3's, which idea 78 already showed is its cell's 46% base rate. **BOTH KEEP PATHS on all 450 books: 4a 276/450** (CAND20 146, CAND5 32, EWall 98), **4b 87/450** (40/2/45), failing-bar census H2 233, DD 203, CAGR 194, OOS 176, H1 121 — every pass is a random sub-panel already inside idea 78's published grid, and a random k=80 sub-panel clearing 4b at a **46%** base rate is a fact about the bar, not the book. The 4b verdict itself separates on **sd** (Welch t +4.14) about twice as strongly as on **W** (+2.28), the same asymmetry at the verdict level. **What the record must now carry: idea 78's test C is CLEAN on this axis** and its dispersion columns may be read as published; idea 54's panel-level survivorship concern is untouched by this and stands. **Survivorship is the premise under test, not a caveat to it** — `universe_broad.json` is current constituents, so W is the realised return of names already known to have survived, which under-states true winner-content and makes the control conservative; that cuts AGAINST a surviving sd coefficient, so the limitation is stated rather than argued around. Other caveats: W is one scalar per draw (a name-by-name fixed-effect design would be stronger and is not run); 150 draws on one panel; ideas 39/49's inverted gate and idea 128's shallow IS drawdown window bias every selector in (5) identically. Ideas 252-253 queued. Script `research/backtests/2026-09-06_dispersion-as-a-survivorship-detector_B.py`; result `.result.md`; console, draws, regressions, mechanism, cells and walk-forward CSVs alongside. Rules unchanged.
- 2026-09-06 (lane C, idea 251 does-reach-belong-beside-every-instrument-price) — **ANSWERED: reach belongs beside every instrument price, and back-filling it moves published claims. No RULES change, no new KEEP; nothing in RULES.md, scan.py, bot.py or baseline.py touched.** Idea 245's module (base books, simulator, instruments) and through it idea 94's `run_stop` were IMPORTED, not re-implemented: 2 panels (u56, broad136) x 2 instrument-free base books (EWALL0, CAND20, gross 0.75) x 6 families x an EXTENDED strength ladder x 2 cost rungs = **512 backtests, all reported**, exactly TWO tuned dimensions (family, level). Harness first: no-instrument == `engine.backtest` **0.000e+00**, stop == idea 94's `run_stop` **0.000e+00**, and all 288 points shared with idea 74's COMMITTED grid reproduce at **1.8e-15** (its per-cell `max_bought` likewise). **(1) The column.** Best-cell reach at 10 bps, idea 74's published ladder -> extended: 200d **7.02 -> 7.02**, band **6.87 -> 6.87**, abs **5.05 -> 6.56**, ddctl **9.42 -> 10.93**, stop **2.73 -> 6.91**, de-gross 14.86 -> 25.68 (unbounded by construction — g=0 is cash). **(2) Idea 74's stop reach was a grid edge**: its maximum sat at the shallowest level swept (0.08); at stop=0.02 the stop buys 5.44-6.91pp in 4/4 cells, so 'the stop cannot buy 4pp at any depth' is REFUTED. The gates and the band are not grid edges — ~7pp is a real ceiling. **(3) The census.** Of the record's only price list quoted AT budgets (idea 74's menu.csv, 240 entries), **136 (56.7%) were quoted at a budget the instrument could not reach**; extending the ladders rescues only 19, leaving **117 (48.8%)** — the exposure is mostly a genuine instrument limit, not a grid artefact. In prose: 110 price-claim lines in 34 files -> 238 (claim, instrument) pairs, of which only **48 (20.2%) state the depth they were quoted at** (3 beyond the published reach, 0 beyond the extended one). The prose finding is the absence: across those pairs a budget drawn uniformly from PROTOCOL's own T grid {2,4,6,8,10}pp lands outside the named instrument's reach **22.4%** of the time and no published price says so. **(4) The back-fill moves 4 of 40 published menu picks**, all at the deep budgets and all the same way — idea 40's ddctl displaces de-gross (broad/EWALL0 T=10pp dg 0.58 -> ddctl 0.43; u56/EWALL0 T=8pp dg 0.61 -> ddctl 0.45; both repeat at 25 bps). The shallow-budget ordering is unchanged and the stop, now reachable at T=4, is still dominated (0.68 vs dg 0.62): idea 74's reach LABELS were wrong for the stop, its price CONCLUSION survives. **(5) Rule 8**: reach ORDERS the instruments the same way out of sample (Spearman 0.743 PUB / 0.700 EXT across the six families) but its LEVEL does not transfer — IS reach-argmax == OOS argmax in only 22/48 (PUB) and 18/48 (EXT), and **OOS reach exceeds IS reach in 47 of 48 cells** (idea 117's shallow-IS-window mechanism; a max_bought read off 2009-2016 would have called the stop unable to buy 1pp). So the column is publishable **ranked and window-stamped, never as a bare number**. KEEP paths, all 504 arms: 4a 209/504, 4b 57/504; the best 4b arm is idea 74's own PARK by-product re-derived (u56/EWALL0 + 12% band 14.09%/1.233/-19.4%, halves 1.268/1.211, OOS 15.22%/1.272/-19.4%, 1.9x turnover) against RULES v1 6.45%/0.664/-13.8% (OOS 7.73%/0.747/-13.8%) and SPY 15.23%/0.889/-33.7% (OOS 15.45%/0.882/-33.7%) — no new candidate. Survivorship (current constituents) is NOT cancelled here because reach is an absolute quantity, so every number is an under-statement. PROTOCOL rule 6 respected: the schema recommendation is proposed in the result file, not applied. `research/backtests/2026-09-06_does-reach-belong-beside-every-instrument-price_C.py`
- 2026-09-06 (cloud, idea 74 drawdown-instrument-exchange-rate) — **ANSWERED: the ranked insurance menu the queue asked for is delivered, its ORDERING is stable, and the three published pairwise numbers it replaces were wrong in ORDERING and not merely in level. As a per-cell SELECTOR the same menu is a KILL under rule 8. No RULES change, no new KEEP; one PARK by-product; nothing in RULES.md, scan.py, bot.py or baseline.py touched.** Idea 245's module (base books, simulator, instruments) and through it idea 94's `run_stop` were IMPORTED, not re-implemented: 2 panels (u56, broad136) x 2 INSTRUMENT-FREE base books (EWALL0, CAND20, both gross 0.75) x 6 instrument families x 6 strength levels x 2 cost rungs = **296 backtests, all reported**, exactly TWO tuned dimensions (family, strength), with the budget grid T in {2,4,6,8,10} pp a reporting axis printed reachable or not. Harness first: no-instrument == `engine.backtest` at **0.000e+00**, stop == idea 94's published `run_stop` at **0.000e+00** at every depth, and every published rate recomputed from the committed grid CSV at **0.000e+00**. **(1) The menu**, median pp of CAGR surrendered per pp of MaxDD bought over the 4 panel x book cells at 10 bps, cheapest first at T=2pp: MA re-entry band **-0.06**, idea 40's book DD control **0.29**, absolute momentum **0.41**, 200d-type gate **0.44**, **de-gross 0.63 (the reference lever)**, per-name trailing stop **0.89**. At T=4: band 0.23, ddctl 0.40, 200d 0.42, abs 0.53, dg 0.62, stop UNREACHABLE. **(2) The published ordering reverses.** Idea 9 priced the stop at 0.17-0.53 and idea 57 the 200d gate at 4.45, i.e. the stop looked ~10x cheaper; on one matched book at one cost the stop is **the dearest instrument the project owns** and the only family that loses to simply holding less (beats de-gross in 1/3 comparable cells, median gap **+0.28**), while every other family beats de-gross where comparable — 200d **7/7**, band **5/5**, abs **4/4**, ddctl **13/15** (median gaps -0.20, -0.47, -0.17, -0.17). Those three numbers were never comparable and should not be quoted again. **(3) REACH is half the menu and has never been published.** The stop cannot buy 4pp of drawdown at any depth in any cell; abs tops out at ~5pp, the gates at ~7pp, ddctl at ~9.4pp, and **de-gross alone reaches an arbitrary budget** with a rate flat in the budget (0.63 -> 0.61 from T=2 to T=10) because it is an exact lever. The correct reading is two-column: below ~6pp use the cheap gates, above it there is no alternative to holding less. At 25 bps every rate rises and every reach shrinks (stop 1.63 then unreachable). **(4) Rule 8 rejects the menu as a selector.** Family AND level chosen on the 2009-2016 rate only, 2017-2026 read once: the IS-cheapest family stays cheapest OOS in **3/25** cells, mean rate regret **+0.291**, median +0.226. The IS window picks de-gross **21/25**; OOS the cheapest is ddctl 12 and band 9, de-gross only 2. The mechanism is idea 117's crisis-depth-as-denominator restated on this axis: the IS controls draw only -11.3%..-15.3% against OOS -19.2%..-25.7%, so a shallow window makes every instrument look dear (dg's own rate 0.97-1.33 IS vs 0.59-0.82 OOS) and de-gross wins IS purely on reach. **The exchange rate is therefore a valid REPORTING column and an invalid SELECTOR**: RULES may quote the ordering when a drawdown budget is set, and must not fit family or level per window. **(5) Both KEEP paths, all 144 arms at 10 bps:** 4a 35/144, 4b 33/144, all four no-instrument controls failing BOTH paths on DRAWDOWN. **PARK by-product:** `EWALL0 + 12% MA re-entry band` on u56 at **14.1% / 1.233 / -19.4%**, halves 1.268/1.211, **OOS 1.272**, turnover **1.9x/yr**, exchange rate **-0.26** (it buys 3.1pp of drawdown while ADDING 0.8pp of CAGR), surviving 25 bps at 13.8% / 1.207 / -19.5% (OOS 1.245) — the best 4b arm in the run, against SPY 15.2% / 0.889 / -33.7% (halves 0.957/0.834, OOS 0.882) and RULES v1 6.5% / 0.664 / -13.8%. It is PARK and NOT KEEP for two stated reasons: 12% is the WIDEST point of the ladder with u56 Sharpe monotone in width (1.130 -> 1.233) while turnover falls 6.4x -> 1.9x, i.e. a grid edge rather than a located optimum (idea 240's exact lesson), AND it fails 4b on `broad` at both cost rungs on DRAWDOWN (-21.7% against the -20.2% cap), so it is not cross-universe. Two queue ideas added (250 locate-the-band-width-ceiling, 251 does-reach-belong-beside-every-instrument-price). SURVIVORSHIP: both panels are current constituents and a survivor panel has shallower crashes, so every instrument here is priced in a world with less drawdown to buy; the rate is a ratio of two within-cell differences against the same control, which cancels most but not all of that. Script `research/backtests/2026-09-06_drawdown-instrument-exchange-rate_cloud.py`, console + 3 CSVs committed.
- 2026-09-06 (cloud, idea 245 arm-the-cheap-instruments-in-crashes-instead) — **KILL of the queue's premise, generalised: NO slow instrument is cheaper in crashes, and idea 75's sign reversal turns out to be a property of ARMING ANYTHING on a regime rather than a property of the stop. No RULES change, no new book, no KEEP candidate; nothing in RULES.md, scan.py, bot.py or baseline.py touched.** 2 panels (u56 56 names, broad 136) x 2 INSTRUMENT-FREE base books (EWALL0 equal-weight-all-no-gates, CAND20 top-20-by-composite-no-gates, both gross 0.75) x 6 instrument families x 4 strength levels x 2 cost rungs = **200 backtests, all reported**; exactly TWO tuned dimensions (family, strength), with panel/book/cost reported at every value and never selected on. Harness gates first: idea 94's module is IMPORTED, `run()` with no instrument equals `engine.backtest` at max|diff| **0.000e+00** on returns AND turnover, and `run(stop=S)` reproduces idea 94's published `run_stop` at **0.000e+00** over all four depths. One gate was demoted from an assertion to a reported number and the reason is published: weight-scaling a book by g is NOT idea 66's static lever in this engine (the drift step re-normalises the book against its cash line), so `dg` is priced with the lever convention throughout and the two conventions are quoted side by side (0.0002 CAGR / 0.0016 Sharpe apart at g=0.60). **(1) The pre-registered split answers NO for every conditional instrument.** (arm − its own control), annualised inside SPY<200dMA vs outside, 16 cells per family: 200d gate cheaper-in-crashes in **2/16** (median gap **−4.87** pp/yr), 3% band **0/16** (−4.44), absolute momentum **0/16** (−4.23), idea 40's book DD control **2/16** (−3.76), and idea 75's stop **1/16** (−2.21), reproducing its published sign. The only 16/16 YES is **constant de-gross** (+11.54 pp/yr median), which is a YES by arithmetic and not by timing — holding less is mechanically profitable when the market falls — and it cannot be armed, because arming it IS the ddctl row, which is dearer in crashes in 14/16. **(2) The mechanism is flip rate, exactly as ideas 55/57/4 predicted:** gap magnitude orders with turnover across the whole family set (200d 10.8x/yr → −4.87; band 7.3x → −4.44; ddctl 5.6x → −3.76; dg 2.4x → +11.54). Robustness, never selected on: under idea 75's `breadth20` regime (8.1–8.6% of days vs spy200's 17.1%) the gaps shrink but no ordering changes (200d −1.78, abs −0.59, ddctl −3.70, stop −2.16, band +0.03 at 8/16 — the one family reaching a coin flip, dg +14.80); at 25 bps every gate gets WORSE (200d −5.69, band −4.99, abs −4.64, ddctl −3.83, stop −2.60). **(3) Both KEEP paths evaluated, nothing withheld:** 4a 23/96 and 4b 25/96 instrument arms at 10 bps, with all four no-instrument controls failing BOTH paths on DRAWDOWN — the instruments genuinely do buy drawdown. The best 4b arms are the project's own known books rediscovered rather than new candidates (u56/EWALL0/band3% **12.3% / 1.168 / −17.6%**, halves 1.217/1.136, OOS 1.211, 4.3x turnover — this is idea 57's `ew-band3`; u56/CAND20/ddctl12% 14.7% / 1.164 / −17.8%, halves 1.279/1.071, OOS 1.138), against SPY 15.2% / 0.889 / −33.7% (halves 0.957/0.834, OOS 0.882) and RULES v1 6.5% / 0.664 / −13.8%. **No new KEEP is proposed, because rule 8 does not pick any of them.** **(4) Rule 8 gives another do-nothing win.** (family, strength) chosen on 2009–2016 IS Sharpe and 2017–2026 read once, 8 cells: mean OOS regret against the no-instrument control **−0.0368**, median −0.0178, **wins 1/8**, with the two worst cells at −0.129 (u56/CAND20) and −0.126 (u56/EWALL0) at 10 bps; the best available OOS arm was ddctl/0.08 or band/0.08 in 6/8 and never what the IS chooser took. Restricting the chooser to the queue's slow set alone reproduces the table exactly (the stop is never picked). OOS benchmarks: RULES v1 7.7%/0.747/−13.8% (u56) and 5.9%/0.576/−21.2% (broad), SPY 15.5%/0.882/−33.7%. **Standing consequence for the queue:** any future "arm X only in crashes" proposal is presumed dead on arrival unless X is unconditional de-grossing, and that case is already priced here. SURVIVORSHIP: both panels are current constituents, so every LEVEL is biased upward; the verdict statistic is a within-cell difference (arm minus its own control on the same panel, book and cost rung), which the bias largely cancels out of. Script `research/backtests/2026-09-06_arm-the-cheap-instruments-in-crashes-instead_cloud.py`, console + 5 CSVs committed.
- 2026-09-06 (lane B, idea 75 stop-only-in-crashes) — **KILL, and the queue's premise is REFUTED with the sign reversed. The per-name trailing stop's damage is NOT whipsaw in trending markets: it is concentrated in exactly the crash regime the proposed arming keeps.** 144 stop arms + 12 matched no-stop controls, all reported; exactly TWO tuned parameters (stop depth S in {10,15,20,25}% and arming regime ARM in {always, spy200, breadth20}), with panel {u56, broad}, book {v1, top20, ew-band3}, cost {10,25} bps and cooldown (0) reported at every value and never selected on. Idea 94's module is imported, not re-implemented: with stop=None the simulator equals `engine.backtest` at max|diff| = 0.0, and with ARM=always it equals idea 94's `run_stop` at max|diff| = 0.0 with identical firing counts, asserted before any result was read. **(1) The premise, measured.** Annualised (stop minus its own control) split by SPY's 200d regime: median **-1.234 / -1.163 / -1.027 / -0.664 pp/yr in the CRASH regime** at S = 10/15/20/25% against **-0.929 / -0.336 / -0.038 / -0.005 pp/yr in the TREND regime** — negative in 69 of 72 crash cells, and 3.5x / 27x / 133x larger in magnitude than the trend column at S = 15/20/25%. At the depths that matter the trending-market damage the queue wanted to remove is a rounding error. Idea 9's own year cut said the same and was read backwards: 2020, the deepest crash in the sample, is the stop's worst year in 12 of 12 rows (-1.1 to -1.9 pp). Mechanism is idea 96's section 3 restated — a trailing stop fires into a short-term reversal, and crashes are where reversals are largest. **(2) H1 fails on magnitude.** The always-on stop gives up a median 0.0108 of Sharpe against its no-stop control; spy200 recovers **+0.0007** (beating always-on in 30/48 paired cells while keeping 47.8% of firings) and breadth20 **+0.0006** (25/48, 34.4%) — about 6% of the loss bought back by cutting half to two thirds of the trades. The armed stop still loses to not stopping in 46 of 48 cells. **(3) H2 fails outright.** Median dMaxDD of armed vs always-on is 0.000 pp; priced against idea 66's static-gross lever, arms cheaper than the lever are 12/22 (always), 13/20 (spy200), 12/21 (breadth20) — arming does not make the instrument cheaper, it makes it smaller. **(4) KEEP paths: 0 of 144 arms passes a path its own control does not already pass** (4a 27/72 and 4b 29/72 at 10 bps, every one inherited from idea 2's top20 or idea 57's ew-band3, 3 of 6 controls passing 4b unaided); the best armed arm (u56/ew-band3 S=10% spy200, 10.7% / 1.123 / -14.8%, OOS 1.228) sits below its own control on Sharpe, CAGR and OOS Sharpe. **(5) Rule 8** (S, ARM fixed on 2009-2016, 2017-2026 read once, 12 cells): the selector takes a stop in 10/12 and an ARMED stop in 4/12, mean OOS regret **-0.0083** (median -0.0094, negative in 8/12), and the best available OOS arm is the no-stop control in 8/12 — the **15th instance in the record of a dial rule losing to doing nothing**. OOS benchmarks: RULES v1 7.7% / 0.747 / -13.8% (u56) and 5.9% / 0.576 / -21.2% (broad), SPY 15.5% / 0.882 / -33.7%. **The carry-forward finding is the corrected mechanism, not the KILL:** conditioning a fast instrument on a crash regime concentrates it into its own worst regime, which is a prior against every 'arm it only when it matters' proposal — queued as ideas 245 and 246. Stated limits: current-constituent survivorship on both panels runs WITH this conclusion (it makes the measured loss an upper bound on the stop's value); breadth20's look-ahead-free expanding threshold arms 3.1% of IS days against 13.1% of OOS days, which weakens the rule-8 evidence for that regime specifically but not the full-sample premise test, which splits on spy200 in every row; the depth grid, book definitions and IS/OOS split are inherited from idea 94 on overlapping data, so the walk-forward validates the ARMING comparison, not those constants. No RULES change, no book promoted, and no file outside `research/` was modified.
- 2026-09-06 (cloud, idea 76 holding-period-as-the-hidden-variable) — **ANSWERED: idea 76's thesis is a KILL as stated, and the run says why on its own pre-registered prediction.** 13 books (NOGATE, EWALL, BAND3, ABS, V1, V1C20, CAND5/10/20/30/40/60, MOM20) x 4 panels (U56, ETF36, B136, SMALL) x 4 cost rungs {0,10,20,30} bps x 3 cadences {D,W,M} = **624 backtests, all reported**; primary cell 10 bps / weekly / next-day execution; common window 2011-01-13 -> 2026-09-04. Harness: idea 9's three published anchors reproduce almost exactly (**V1 15.7d / 0.146** vs its 16d/0.144, **CAND20 39.6d / 0.226** vs 39d/0.228, **BAND3 168.8d / 0.193** vs 170d/0.189), so the episode-length measurement is the same instrument idea 9 used. **(1) P1 — cost sensitivity is an ARITHMETIC IDENTITY, not an empirical property holding period could explain.** `engine.backtest` charges `- turnover_t * bps/1e4` on a turnover path that does not depend on the cost rung, so `dSharpe/10bps` is fixed by turnover and book vol before any book runs. Measured against the prediction over 52 book-cells: **Spearman(turnover/sigma, dSharpe/10bps) = -1.000, R2 = 1.0000, mean |measured - predicted| Sharpe 0.00049 (max 0.00103)**; the CAGR residual is 0.098pp mean and is compounding (CAGR is geometric, the charge is linear). Held-name vol20 alone is uninformative (rho +0.041) — it enters only through sigma. `1/L + held-vol` alone reach R2 0.8626 but add **+0.0000** on top of `turnover/sigma`. Measured range -0.281 .. -0.007 Sharpe per 10 bps, wider on both sides than idea 68's published -0.068 .. -0.099 because this corpus spans NOGATE (0.65x/yr) to SMALL/V1 (33.3x/yr). **(2) P2 — L and turnover are the SAME DIAL, so the 'turnover is only its shadow' framing is a re-parameterisation, not a discovery.** **Spearman(L, annual turnover) = -0.989 (N=52)**, Spearman(1/L, turnover) +0.989, and OLS `turnover ~ 2*gross/L` gives **R2 = 0.9812** (mean abs error 1.82x/yr, concentrated in the NOGATE corner where L is the whole sample). No test on this corpus can show either variable dominating. **(3) Cadence is the one channel that is not turnover arithmetic, and L very nearly fails to take it.** Mean cadence Sharpe RANGE falls monotonically by L quartile — **0.321 (L~17d) -> 0.256 -> 0.215 -> 0.093 (L~1215d)** — which is idea 65's cadence-insensitivity bar restated and confirmed. But R2(range ~ turnover) = 0.3578 beats R2(range ~ 1/L) = 0.3297, and 1/L's incremental contribution over turnover is **+0.0179**. The practically useful form is the **cost x cadence interaction: at 30 bps there are 8 4b passes at monthly cadence and ZERO at weekly and ZERO at daily** — slowing the cadence buys cost resilience, and it does so entirely through turnover (U56/BAND3 4.68x/yr weekly -> 3.06x/yr monthly). **(4) Rule 8 (choosers fixed on 2009-2016, 2017-2026 read once, pooled equal-weight over 4 panels): LONGEST and LOWTURN are byte-identical in 4 of 4 panels — both pick NOGATE everywhere — at 0.937 pooled OOS Sharpe, against NOTHING (U56/CAND20) at 1.131.** ISSHARPE 0.882, RANDOM 0.781, LOWVOL 0.584 (the worst rule in the run, so held-name vol is not a selector either); SPY OOS 15.5%/0.882/-33.7%, RULES v1 7.7%/0.747/-13.8%. Both rival design variables beat a coin flip (+0.156) and the incumbent IS-Sharpe selector (+0.055) but lose to doing nothing by **-0.194** — the **14th** such instance in the record. **KEEP paths: 4a 89/624, 4b 91/624; primary cell W/10bps 4a 11/52, 4b 14/52. Nothing new is promoted** — every 4b pass is a book already in the record. Notable coordinates: **U56/BAND3 (idea 57's book) W/10bps 10.9% / 1.120 / -15.1%, halves 1.015/1.217, OOS 1.232 at 4.68x/yr**, better than the standing candidate on Sharpe, MaxDD AND OOS at less than half its turnover, passing 4b at 0/10/20 bps weekly and at all four rungs monthly — but **failing 4b on ETF36 and SMALL at every rung**, so idea 58's cross-universe cost wall is CONFIRMED (30 bps weekly, 20 bps daily, 2 of 4 panels at any rung), not closed. **B136/BAND3 passes BOTH 4a and 4b** (10.8% / 1.064 / -16.8%, OOS 1.071), as do B136/EWALL, B136/ABS, B136/CAND40, B136/CAND60. Best 30-bps row anywhere is **U56/CAND20 MONTHLY 14.0% / 1.130 / -19.6%, halves 1.082/1.179, OOS 1.233, 4.72x/yr** — the standing candidate surviving triple costs by being re-decided monthly, at 1.3pp more drawdown than weekly (inside the 20.2% cap, but idea 3's monthly drawdown tax again). **Recommendation for the record: publish turnover, not holding period, as the cost-sensitivity variable and quote it as the formula `dSharpe/10bps = -turnover*0.0010/sigma` (max residual 0.001) rather than measuring it per book; keep L only as a cadence-insensitivity proxy, where it is slightly WORSE than turnover; use neither as a book selector.** SURVIVORSHIP: all panels are current constituents, one-directional, hardest on B136/SMALL; holding-period statistics are especially exposed because a delisted name's episode is truncated in reality and absent here, biasing measured L UP. NOGATE's L is the sample length by construction (censored share 1.000) and is the corpus endpoint, not a book. Script `research/backtests/2026-09-06_holding-period-as-the-hidden-variable_cloud.py`, memo `..._cloud.result.md`.
- 2026-09-06 (cloud, idea 242 quote-every-n-argmax-with-its-saturation-share) — **ANSWERED: the back-fill is done, the column is REAL BUT NARROW, and as a decision rule it is a KILL.** Every committed CSV in research/backtests/ (648) was scanned mechanically for a position-count sweep: 28 files qualify, giving **184 argmax cells, 175 on a mappable panel**, with 114 candidate columns rejected under a logged reason (`.census_rejects.csv`) so the denominator is auditable rather than asserted. Each cell was back-filled with its panel's mean eligible count, its under-fill share `u = P(n_elig < n)` at the quoted `n`, and the panel's saturation point `n_sat50`. **(1) 10 of 175 (5.7%) published argmaxes are at or past saturation, and all 10 are on the two panels with fewer than 25 tradable names** (ETF24 6/8, STK20 4/8); B136, BSTK100, SMALL, U56 and ETF36 contribute zero. So the column earns its place on narrow panels and is a near-zero constant everywhere else. **(2) The larger defect in the record is the grid edge, not saturation: 55 of 175 argmaxes (31.4%) sit at their own grid top** — 5.5x more common. **(3) Idea 240's panel-size law does not generalise.** Spearman(panel mean eligible count, published n argmax) is **+0.926 on idea 240's own 7 cells and +0.132 over the record's 175**; idea 240's cells were one sweep sharing one grid, the record's 175 carry 20 different grids, and the grid is what mostly sets the argmax. That number should not be quoted as a record-wide fact. **(4) Saturation duplicates books, and duplicated books inflate pass counts.** Under the NORM convention a cell the panel cannot fill is byte-identical to the widest one it can: **6 of 84 CAND points are exact duplicates of a narrower n (all 6 saturated) and 3 of 24 CAND 4b passes are duplicates** — STK20 NORM n=30/40/60 are all the n=20 book at Sharpe 1.341. Collapsing them takes NORM from **13/42 raw to 10/36 distinct books**; FIXED is unaffected (11/42) because there a saturated cell de-grosses instead of duplicating. Any 4b pass count quoted over a saturating grid is inflated. **(5) Rule 8 (IS 2009-2016, OOS 2017-2026 read once, 7 panels, both conventions, pooled equal-weight): the saturation cap NEVER BINDS.** `SATARGMAX` (IS argmax restricted to n with IS under-fill <= 0.50) minus `ISARGMAX` is **+0.0000 in 14 of 14 cells and the picks differ in 0 of 7 panels on both conventions** — every live IS argmax was already unsaturated (max under-fill at a pick 0.205). Pooled OOS Sharpe: **NOTHING (incumbent U56/n=20) 1.168 FIXED / 1.131 NORM**, WIDEST 1.012/0.916, RANDOM 0.927/0.868, ISARGMAX = SATARGMAX 0.885/0.891, NARROWEST 0.761/0.763, against SPY OOS 15.5%/0.882/-33.7% and RULES v1 7.7%/0.747/-13.8%. That is the **13th** instance in the record of a dial rule losing to doing nothing. **(6) The predictive claim behind the column is a null with the wrong sign**: Spearman(under-fill at the IS pick, OOS regret) = **+0.724 (N=14)**, i.e. the more under-filled picks had the *smaller* regret; with 0 saturated picks in the sample there is no test of the actual hypothesis. **KEEP paths, all 98 points: 4a 28/98 (CAND FIXED 23/42, NORM 4/42), 4b 28/98 (CAND FIXED 11/42, NORM 13/42 raw -> 10/36 distinct); nothing promoted** — the best rows are the already-published U56/CAND20 FIXED (12.5% / 1.092 / -18.3%, halves 1.059/1.127, OOS 1.168) and STK20/CAND10 NORM (21.6% / 1.344 / -20.1%, OOS 1.410) on the 20-mega-cap panel idea 10 already flagged as selection. Harness reproduces idea 2's KEEP and live v1 to the third decimal; the small panel drops the 44 tickers with `max_1d_move >= 1.0`. **Recommendation for the record: publish mean eligible count and under-fill share beside any count argmax on a panel under ~25 tradable names, collapse duplicated books before quoting a pass count, and do NOT promote saturation to a chooser constraint.** SURVIVORSHIP: all panels are current constituents, one-directional, hardest on STK20/BSTK100/SMALL; the census inherits the bias of every parent script it reads. Script `research/backtests/2026-09-06_quote-every-n-argmax-with-its-saturation-share_cloud.py`, memo `..._cloud.result.md`.

RULE 8 IS UNAMBIGUOUS: with the share pinned at 0.53 so that only the count rule is chosen, the IS chooser **re-picks FIX in 8 of 8 large-cap cells and adds exactly +0.0000 OOS Sharpe**; allowed to choose the share too it averages **-0.0445** against doing nothing and picks `ADAPT|m0.10` on broad@10bps for **-0.2904** — the ninth consecutive selection-loses-to-doing-nothing instance. **THE MECHANISM IS CASH, NOT BREADTH**, and the run was designed to separate them: under the incumbent's own GROSS/n weighting a fixed count silently de-grosses whenever the eligible list is shorter than n, so in 2022 the incumbent held **45.7% of capital in cash on u56** (invested 0.5430) against the adaptive book's 0.7411 — and with cash held equal (weights renormalised to full gross) the two rules' mean |dSharpe| on the large-cap cells is only **0.0422**, i.e. breadth-timing per se carries almost nothing; under the live construction the adaptive book's drawdown is deeper in **26 of 28** large-cap cells (mean -4.50pp). **IDEA 46 IS HALF RIGHT and the half matters:** the adaptive count beats the fixed one in 2022 in **12 of 12** cells (+0.92 to +5.31pp; u56 -5.80% vs -9.02%) and in 2020 in only **4 of 12** (u56 alone; it loses 7.19pp on broad), and since it holds LESS cash the 2022 win is selectivity — holding the top half of a halved eligible list — which helped in a slow grind and hurt in a V-shaped recovery; across all calendar years it is 8/18 on u56, 8/18 on broad (mean -0.28pp) and 9/16 on the small panel. TWO BY-PRODUCTS, NEITHER PROPOSED: **ADAPT63** is the only arm that beats the incumbent's broad 4b pass count (6 of 28 vs 5) and holds 4b at m=0.53 and m=0.75 (11.01%/1.0409/-16.39%, OOS 1.1072, 4a AND 4b) — rejected because rule 8 never picks it, it dies at 25 bps, and its 63-bar window is an unswept third dial of exactly the kind idea 223 has just finished pricing; and **FIXIS** is the first audit of the fact that idea 153's published Ebar is a FULL-SAMPLE mean, so the incumbent's u56 n=20 reads the future — removing it gives n=19 and costs 0.031 of Sharpe and 0.040 of OOS Sharpe (broad is unchanged at n=48). A CONTROL CAUGHT MY OWN PRE-REGISTERED PREMISE: I predicted ADAPT is unconditionally fully invested because n_t <= E_t; false, because the composite cannot score a name whose 252-day window is still NaN, so the rankable count is below E_t on 6.2% / 8.4% / 58.6% of bars and the adaptive book de-grosses there too — the pre-registration is left verbatim with a labelled correction and every invested-gross figure is measured. CAVEATS: survivorship on all three current-constituent panels (idea 54), the gate is inverted on the small panel (ideas 39/49), idea 223's trade-date band applies to a weekly schedule's 5 anchors and was NOT re-measured here, and costs are a flat linear bps charge.

THE THREE BANDS THE IDEA ASKED FOR. (1) mean |dSharpe|: **0.0945+-0.0178 (u56), 0.0514+-0.0136 (broad), 0.1049+-0.0197 (small)**, with the five real keys' mean at the 92nd, 100th and **51st** percentile of the null band — on the small panel the real keys are INDISTINGUISHABLE from noise, and R6 on u56 (0.0565) sits BELOW the null mean. THE CORRECTION: idea 158 published S3 = 0.7240 and read it as 0.001 ahead of S1; that value is that draw's, at the **56th percentile** of a distribution whose sd is FOUR TIMES S1's spread, so the honest statement is not 'the null selector ties the real one' but 'the null selector is a coin flip whose expectation is 0.019 below the real one and **0.109 below doing nothing**' — S3 beats do-nothing in 2/100 draws and SPY in 1/100, the seventh consecutive selection-loses-to-doing-nothing instance, now with an error bar. THE CLAIMS THAT GOT STRONGER: the null key beats the LIVE /sqrt(vol20) scaler in **27.06+-1.41 of 28** large-cap cells tilted NEG and **26.56+-1.63** tilted POS, 100 of 100 draws win at least half, and **0 of 100 draws are worse than the live scaler on mean signed dSharpe** (null -0.0682/-0.0461 vs the scaler's -0.1931) — so the eighth delete-the-scaler finding is a property of the null FAMILY, and idea 158's own draw was an understatement at the 23rd and 2nd percentiles. Cross-universe 4b combinations **3.19+-1.03**, at least one in 100 of 100 draws, so a cross-universe pass is not evidence a key is real; and rule 8's selector picks a null arm on broad@10bps in **26 of 100** draws (41 of 100 somewhere among the 6 cells), which is common but not the norm idea 158's single draw implied. Offered to PROTOCOL and NOT adopted: a null-key control quoted as a point estimate must carry its draw count and its family's sd. CAVEATS: survivorship on all three current-constituent panels (small drops max_1d_move >= 1.0, 439 of 483 names remain); the gate is inverted on the small panel (ideas 39/49); and **100 draws bound the SEED, not the model** — every null key here is a geometric random walk with R6's functional form, and a different null family is a different question.

The Q95 band's sd falls **0.0242 -> 0.0153 -> 0.0115 -> 0.0076 = 1/3.18 against sqrt(200/20) = 3.16** — textbook 1/sqrt(K), which is the property that makes a draw count mean anything. Measured on 20 blocks rather than 3, idea 201's headline is understated: **31.7% of configurations flip at K=20**, not 17.8%. **(3) THE UNDETERMINED ZONE.** 95th percentile of |margin| among flippers: MAX 0.1248 / 0.0787 / 0.1068 / 0.0141, Q95 **0.0921 / 0.0327 / 0.0097 / 0.0163**, with 72.8% / 41.1% / **16.1%** / 25.0% of the corpus inside the Q95 zone. Idea 201's `read |margin| < 0.05 as undetermined` is correct at K=20 and an order of magnitude too loose at Q95/K=100. **(4) THE NUMBER, in the form the queue asked for: fix the statistic to a 95th percentile, then use 100 draws, and publish the band's own sd (0.0115, 17.6% of the band) beside every verdict.** At Q95/K=100 the pairwise flip rate is 2.5% against 7.7% at K=20 and the undetermined zone is |margin| < 0.0097; K=200 doubles the cost for no measured improvement on this corpus. **(5) RULE 8 — TWELFTH consecutive do-nothing win, and the third failure of this clause as a gate (after 181/186/192).** Clause read on <= 2016-12-31 only, 2017-2026 read ONCE, 18 cells: S0 do-nothing **0.7766** mean OOS Sharpe; ORACLE-OOS 0.8197 (+0.0431, t +3.86); S1 IS-Sharpe argmax 0.7405 (-0.0361, t -1.68); and **all EIGHT clause-gated selectors below the control at every K and both statistics** — best MAX K=200 at -0.0145 (t -1.89, 0W/4L) and best only because it abstains in 14 of 18 cells, worst Q95 K=50 at -0.0652. Benchmarks over the same window: RULES v1 OOS 7.73%/0.7471/-13.83% (u56), 5.94%/0.5762/-21.19% (broad), 7.88%/0.6617/-32.37% (small); **SPY 15.45%/0.8820/-33.72%**. **(6) BOTH KEEP PATHS on all 180 real rows: 4a 37/180, 4b 28/180** (U56 1/19 @10bps and 6/8 @25, BROAD136 6/1 and 23/0, **SMALL439 0/0 and 1/0** = the twelfth reproduction of idea 136), and **all 28 of the 28 4b passes are INSIDE their own 200-draw band** — idea 186 found 18 of 18 inside at K=20 and a tenfold increase in draws rescues none. **4 of 6 pre-registered predictions hit.** The two misses are informative rather than adverse: P4 (MAX flip rate still above 5% at K=200) fails at 2.8% because the test has emptied, not stabilised, and P5 (Q95 monotone and below MAX at every K from 50) fails only at K=200, where each configuration contributes ONE block pair and the estimator is at its noisiest — the quantity free of that confound, the band's own sd, is monotone for Q95 and flat for MAX exactly as claimed. Caveats carried, not buried: current-constituent survivorship on all three panels (idea 54) is inherited identically by real and rotated draws so the clause reading is unaffected and every level is not a tradable estimate; only J-1 distinct rotations exist per configuration (J ~670 on U56/BROAD136, ~600 on SMALL439) so a 400-draw pool is 60-67% of the whole rotation population, blocks are disjoint in offset but not independent, the K=200 flip rate is a LOWER bound, and the MAX band at large K approaches the population maximum — which is why its drift flattens at the top and must not be read as convergence to a critical value; BUDGET-skip's turnover mismatch (idea 186: 25.4%, idea 191: 1782.7%) is inherited and stated, not fixed (idea 203's subject); 2 of 180 rows (SMALL439 / BUDGET tau=0.05 / skip) have an undefined IS Sharpe, are carried as non-clearing with the surviving n printed, and nothing is imputed; idea 38's calendar-day index and idea 126's t+1-only execution carry over. Memo `research/backtests/2026-09-05_how-many-draws-does-clause-11b-need_cloud.memo.md` carries the proposed clause 11b wording (report-only, never a gate). Ideas 214-216 queued. Script `research/backtests/2026-09-05_how-many-draws-does-clause-11b-need_cloud.py`; artefacts `.result.md`, `.memo.md`, `.console.txt`, `.band.csv`, `.flip.csv`, `.zone.csv`, `.walkforward.csv`, `.keep.csv`.
- 2026-09-05 (lane B, idea 190 is-the-conditional-sleeve-anything-at-all) — **ANSWERED, and it splits three ways: the published clause STATISTIC is a KILL, the static sleeve's separation is REAL under the right statistic, and that separation is HINDSIGHT and unavailable to an out-of-sample chooser. KILL of the static sleeve as an asset choice. No new book, no KEEP candidate, RULES.md / scan.py / bot.py / baseline.py untouched.** Idea 186 priced the CONDITIONAL sleeve against a rotation null and left the STATIC one open; rotation is degenerate at on-share 1.0 (every rotation of s ≡ 1 returns the identical book), so the matched null for an always-on overlay is a random **SUBSTITUTION** of its assets — same construction (idea 100/104's momentum-vote × risk-parity), same share f, same cadence, same gross rescale, same name count, different tickers. Corpus: 2 panels (U56, BROAD136) × base n {10,20,40} × cost {10,25} × f {0.10,0.20,0.50} × set {S3=TLT/GLD/UUP, S4=+DBC} = 72 real rows, 12 R20 controls, 36 CASH controls and **10,344 null rows = 5232 genuine backtests in 538 s**; exactly 2 tuned parameters (f, asset set), panel/n/cost carried and never selected on. The **DIV** pool (the 12 non-crypto `bonds_fx_commod` less the real names) is **ENUMERATED WHOLE** — C(9,3)=84 and C(8,4)=70 — so the percentile is exact with no seed and no draw band, which is idea 208's proposal 11b applied at the first opportunity after it was written; the **ALL** pool uses 200 draws from the int-literal seed 1902026 (idea 208's proposal 5). **Reproduction 5/5 before any new number was read:** fast_backtest vs engine **1.39e-17**, the cost identity **1.39e-17**, this run's blend vs the committed idea-134 `book_weights` **0.000e+00**, **24 of 24** committed control-arm rows at max|d| **5.0e-16**, and the null's three exact invariants (name count, disjointness, realised gross identical on every rebalance date) PASS — with the two quantities it does NOT match measured rather than assumed (sleeve on-share real 0.9395 vs null [0.9036,0.9456] — the momentum vote can zero the leg, so 'always-on' is 94%, not 100%; turnover ratio [0.937,0.969]). **(1) The published statistic is wrong for this construction.** Ideas 181/186 clear a clause when |dSharpe(real)| exceeds every draw; the DIV null mean dSharpe is negative in **100.0%** of the 72 points (real **+0.0493** vs null **−0.0549**), so a substitute that DESTROYS 0.27 of Sharpe outranks the real +0.10 and blocks it. Two-sided clear **21/72 = 29.2%**; signed strict argmax **72/72**. **(2) But the signed reading is exactly what hindsight produces:** TLT/GLD/UUP is the argmax of all 84 diversifier triples **72/72 full-sample and 48/72 on 2017–2026, but only 8/72 on the IS window ≤2016** — a 2016 chooser using this project's own IS-Sharpe rule would not have picked it. **(3) The drawdown half is a cash carve-out with three tickers on it:** cash at the same f reproduces **98.4%** of the sleeve's MaxDD gain (median; 93.7/94.8/104.6% at f=0.10/0.20/0.50) while contributing **−0.0017** of Sharpe against the sleeve's +0.0493 — de-grossing (ideas 66/184) for the risk, assets for the return. **(4) Rule 8, 12 cells, 2017–2026 read once.** S0 do-nothing **0.9805 / 14.75% / −24.28%**; S1 the IS-argmax sleeve with the **assets given** **+0.0699 (t +6.85), 12/12** — the first arm in twelve runs to beat do-nothing on OOS Sharpe — but bought with **5.5 pp of CAGR**, failing 4b's CAGR floor in 11 of 12 cells (**OOS 4b: S0 0/12, S1 1/12, S2 2/12, S4 0/12, substitutes 0.0%**); S2 the clause-as-a-gate +0.0112 (3/12), abstaining in 9 of 12 — the fourth run after ideas 181/186/194 in which the null clause fails as a gate; S3 mean substitute −0.1694 (0/12); S4 cash −0.0036 (0/12); and the decisive arm **S5, the IS-argmax SUBSTITUTE, −0.2509 (t −17.29), 0/12**, i.e. **S5 − S1 = −0.3208 (t −19.09), 0 of 12** — the IS rule picks `IEF+LQD+UNG` / `IEF+LQD+SLV` in 12 of 12 cells. **Once the sleeve's assets must be CHOSEN rather than handed over, doing nothing wins by a mile: the thirteenth consecutive do-nothing result.** Benchmarks on the same window: SPY **0.8820 / 15.45% / −33.72%**; RULES v1 @10bps u56 0.7471, broad 0.5763. **(5) Both KEEP paths on all 10,464 rows:** real 4a 41/72 and 4b **23/72**, R20 controls 0/12 on 4b, CASH 8/36, null draws 4a 32.6% and 4b **12.3%** — the real sleeve reaches 4b MORE often than a substituted one (31.9% vs 12.3%), the opposite direction to idea 186's rotation result, and (2) is the reason rather than skill; of the 23 passes only **7 clear the two-sided DIV null on Sharpe and 2 on drawdown**, and the same construction passes OOS 4b once in twelve. **Products, both PROTOCOL proposals for Sunday review (rule 6), nothing applied here: 11c** — read a null clause on the SIGNED percentile, not |dSharpe|, and where the population is enumerable enumerate it instead of drawing a band; **11d** — any instrument whose ASSET IDENTITIES were human-chosen must publish an IS-chosen-substitute arm, or its separation is undated hindsight. Reporting note for the 527 committed sleeve rows: a sleeve row quoting MaxDD must quote the matched-f cash control beside it. **Predictions 5 of 6 pre-registered hit** (P5 MISS: S1 beat S0 — informatively, since (2) and S5 explain why that is not what it looks like), plus P7 HIT, declared POST-HOC along with S5 and the signed reading, both added after the first pass and labelled as such in the script. Caveats carried: current-constituent survivorship on both panels (idea 54) inherited identically by real and null so the comparison is unaffected and the levels are not; the DIV pool is itself a survivor list, so the enumeration is exact FOR THAT POOL; SMALL439 out of scope because joining diversifier ETFs would put them in the small panel's ranking pool and change the base book (ideas 136/186: 0 of 36 there anyway); 12 cells share 2 price panels so every t-stat is optimistic and the enumerated percentile is the inference leaned on; ideas 38 and 126 carry.
