No RULES change; no new book; one KEEP-candidate memo'd and NOT proposed.** 11 cells (idea 159's 294-book share sweep and idea 168's 352-book exponent sweep, both at 10 and 25 bps, plus idea 165's own cell reproduced), 1003 book-rows, 66 arm-rows, exactly two tuned parameters (gross convention x screen coefficients) with all four arms reported in every cell. **Reproduction 4 of 4, and two of them total rather than sampled:** idea 159's committed grid reproduces **294 of 294 rows at 2.22e-16**, idea 168's **352 of 352 at 2.22e-16**, and idea 165's `W_4bIS` u56@10bps walk-forward cell reproduces with the **pick identical** and max |diff| **2.78e-17** on OOS CAGR/Sharpe/MaxDD. **(1) The count idea 165's single cell needed:** the screen changed the pick in **3 of 11** cells under idea 165's own convention and **4 of 11** under the published bars, and the changed pick cleared the OOS-window 4b in **2 of 3** and **3 of 4** respectively, against **0 of 11** for the do-nothing control. **(2) It abstains far more often than it fires, and it has a hard boundary:** empty in 7 of 11 cells, and **0 of 20 arm-cells at 25 bps against 16 of 24 at 10 bps**; the small panel is empty at both rungs and has **0 of 98** books clearing the OOS window at any point (agrees with ideas 164/170). **(3) AUDIT by-product, a real defect that moves no verdict:** an AST sweep of **61 `margins_at` call sites** finds **53 published order, 5 three-bar variant, 3 SWAPPED** — idea 165 lines 626/641 and idea 168 line 515 pass `(0.60, 0.70)` into `(phi, delta)`, i.e. a CAGR floor of 60% of SPY instead of 70% and a drawdown cap of 70% of SPY's instead of 60%. **The swap costs idea 165 rather than flattering it:** the published bars admit FEWER books (85 vs 126 across 11 cells) yet change the pick MORE often (4 vs 3) and clear MORE often (3 vs 2), and idea 165's printed headline is exactly right. **(4) MECHANISM — this answers idea 163 directly:** the screen pays through **DRAWDOWN, by DE-CONCENTRATING**. All **14 of 14** changed arm-cells move the pick to a larger book (n 4->6, 4->20, 14->25, 9->18, 9->25); **OOS |MaxDD| falls in 14 of 14** (-1.22 to -4.36pp), OOS Sharpe rises in 10 of 14 (+0.066..+0.117) and falls in 4, OOS CAGR falls in 10 of 14 (-7.25..+0.24pp). The IS drawdown cap is what does the work: it excludes the concentrated top-of-IS-Sharpe books. **(5) Rule 8:** +0.0287 paired mean OOS Sharpe, t +1.92, **3 wins / 0 losses / 8 ties** (idea 165's convention); +0.0172, t +1.18, 3-1-7 (published). This is the **first selector in the project's record that does not lose to doing nothing** after 110/132/151/166/171/174/175/186 — and it is **not significant**, with 8 of 11 cells exact ties because the screen empties and falls back. The gross convention is inert: STATIC and CF_IS differ in **1 arm-cell of 22**, a sixth confirmation that gross is not a choice variable. **(6)** Both KEEP paths over all 1003 book-rows: 4a **302**, 4b **162**, and **every 4b pass is at 10 bps** (u56 93, broad 69, small 0) — all of them rows idea 159 or 168 already published, so no new book is discovered. **2 of 5 pre-registered predictions HIT** (P2, P3 and P4 all missed in the same direction: when this screen fires it has so far always been right, and the published bars are the stronger version). **KEEP-candidate, memo'd, NOT proposed:** the u56 top-20 book ranked by idea 81's composite times the 12-1 momentum percentile (`MOM`, m=0.53), weekly, 10 bps, g=0.75 — 12.32%/1.0296/-18.88%, halves 1.1097/0.9711, **OOS 13.18%/1.0355/-18.88%** against SPY 15.45%/0.8820/-33.72% and RULES v1 7.73%/0.7471/-13.83%, turnover 10.17x/yr — **selected out of sample** by the rule-8 screen at the published bars. Blockers: one cost rung, one panel, survivorship, and **no random-selector control** (idea 151's warning), which is the single most important missing arm. Ideas 198-200 queued; RULES.md, scan.py, bot.py and baseline.py untouched.
- 2026-09-05 (lane B, idea 158 does-share-price-any-key-or-only-vol) — **ANSWERED, and it SPLITS a load-bearing published claim: the SIGN of idea 153's mechanism is arithmetic and universal, its STRENGTH is not. KILL of the R2 reading. No RULES change, no new book, no KEEP-candidate.** Idea 153 concluded that HOW MUCH OF THE PANEL YOU HOLD prices a cross-sectional tilt better than HOW STRONG THE KEY IS, on R2 0.43-0.60 for overlap against the key's own slope-t at 0.01-0.11 — measured on ONE key (vol20), where 'overlap prices the tilt' and 'vol20 in particular is weak on high-overlap panels' are the same regression. This run swaps the key and keeps everything else identical. **546 backtests** (3 panels x 7 book shares x 13 books: 1 no-tilt control + 6 keys x 2 tilt directions), each run ONCE at 0 bps with the 10 and 25 bps rungs derived from the engine's own identity `r_c = r_0 - turnover*c/1e4`, so gross and net legs of every comparison are the SAME book; weekly, t+1, 75% gross. **Exactly two tuned parameters** (the share m over 7 values and the tilt direction over 3), every grid point printed; the KEY is a reported axis, never selected on. **Reproduction 8 of 8 exact before any new number was read:** idea 153's mean weekly eligible counts (37.50 / 91.46 / 141.23), its published INV-vs-NONE held-set overlap anchors (u56 **69.4%**, broad **42.5%**), the shared control book `NONE / n=20 / u56 @10bps` at **12.65974% / 1.09214 / -18.30835%, halves 1.08828 / 1.10155** to every printed digit, RULES v1 at 6.45305% / 0.66418 / -13.82780%, and the cost identity at **0.00e+00**. The six keys are vol20 in idea 153's verbatim level form (`VOL/NEG` **is** RULES v1's live scaler), vol20 on a rank footing, the composite's own three legs (12-1, 6m, 3m), and **RAND** — the 126-day change of a seeded synthetic random walk per name, built with R6's exact functional form so it matches the real keys' persistence and turnover and carries zero information by construction. **(1) THE DECISIVE TEST HELD.** Within every key, with m the only mover, `Spearman(overlap, |dSharpe|)` is negative in **36 of 36** (panel, key, cost) cells and in **24 of 24** large-cap cells on the m<=0.53 subgrid — RAND included (-0.636, -0.673, -0.055, -0.455). A book that holds more of its panel expresses less of ANY key. **(2) THE R2 DID NOT.** Re-run key by key on m<=0.53: VOL 0.429, VOLR 0.469, MOM 0.551, R6 0.578, R3 0.454, against **RAND 0.070 — 14% of the real keys' mean 0.496**, far under the pre-registered half bar (on |dCAGR|: RAND 0.228 vs 0.596). RAND's slope is still negative with t -2.09, so the direction is arithmetic and the tightness is not: **idea 153's R2 is a joint product of arithmetic AND the key being real.** The project keeps 'a book holding half its panel cannot express a ranking'; it may NOT keep 'share prices a tilt better than key strength does' on the strength of one key. Key strength and share both carry some of it — across the six keys Spearman(|rank IC|, mean|dSharpe|) is **+0.488** against overlap's **-0.362**, and with six keys neither is decisive. **(3) THE NULL CONTROL THE PROJECT DID NOT HAVE, and it is the run's sharpest by-product.** RAND's own rank IC vs the forward 21d return inside the gate is **-0.0233 (t -3.87)** on u56 and +0.0006 (t +0.16) on broad, against VOL +0.0912 (t +8.74) and MOM +0.0613 (t +5.68) — and it still moves the book by as much: mean |dSharpe| on u56@10bps **RAND 0.0794** vs MOM 0.0723, R3 0.0745, R6 0.0409, VOL 0.1077. **So the SIZE of a tilt's effect on Sharpe is not evidence that the key is real**, and any past reading of the form 'the tilt moved Sharpe by X, so the key does something' is unsupported unless a null key was run alongside it. **(4) EIGHTH DELETE-THE-SCALER FINDING, first stated against noise rather than against the unscaled composite.** Mean signed dSharpe over 28 large-cap (panel, share, cost) cells: **VOLR/NEG -0.3306 (positive in 0 of 28), VOL/NEG — the LIVE `/sqrt(vol20)` scaler — -0.1931 (0 of 28)**, R3/NEG -0.1059, MOM/NEG -0.0978, R6/NEG -0.0906, **RAND/POS -0.0531, RAND/NEG -0.0321**, R3/POS -0.0336, MOM/POS +0.0066, R6/POS +0.0102, VOLR/POS +0.0136, VOL/POS +0.0293. **A random key beats the live scaler in 27 of 28 cells tilted NEG and 23 of 28 tilted POS.** At the incumbent's own point (u56, m=0.53, n=20, 10 bps) all twelve tilts are negative and the live one is third-worst: VOL/NEG -0.0963 dSharpe / -2.76pp CAGR against RAND/NEG -0.0222 / -0.81pp. **(5) BOTH KEEP PATHS on all 546 books: 4a 164, 4b full-sample 54, 4b on the OOS window alone 98; every pass is 10 bps and large-cap and 0 of 182 small-panel books passes anything** (seventh reproduction of idea 136). **19 (m, key, dir) combinations pass 4b on two panels and FOUR of them are the RANDOM key**; RAND holds **11 of the 54** full-sample passes, more than VOL's 5 or VOLR's 5 and more than any single real key's. Nothing is proposed: every passing book is idea 153's already-published family perturbed by a tilt the RAND column says is indistinguishable from noise, and that a zero-information book clears the cross-universe 4b at the same rate as the real ones is a fact about **the bar**, not the book. **(6) RULE 8** ((m, key, dir) chosen on 2009-2016 only, read ONCE on 2017-01-01..2026, 6 panel x cost cells): S1 IS-Sharpe argmax over all 91 arms **0.7230** (10.57% / -22.81%, 0 of 6 OOS-4b), **S3 IS-Sharpe argmax over the RANDOM key ALONE 0.7240** (9.17% / -22.67%, 0 of 6), S4 untilted-only 0.7808, and the do-nothing control at the incumbent m=0.53 **0.8149** (9.61% / -21.50%, **3 of 6 OOS-4b passes**), against RULES v1 0.4514 (4.86% / -25.30%) and SPY 0.8820 (15.45% / -33.72%); large-cap only S1 0.9434 / S2 1.0430 / S3 0.9523 / S4 1.0027. **A selector allowed to fit only a synthetic key is 0.001 AHEAD of one allowed to fit all 91 arms**, and on broad@10bps S1's argmax IS a random-key book (`RAND/POS, m=0.20`, OOS 11.44% / 0.876 / -17.2% against the control's 12.00% / 1.038 / -18.9%), so S1 and S3 are byte-identical there. Do-nothing beats S1 in 4 of 6 cells. Sixth instance after ideas 110/151/132/166/168/170 that an IS-fitted selector does not beat the constant it was meant to improve on — and the first in which the selector is shown to be picking NOISE rather than merely picking badly. **Predictions: 3 of 6 held** — P1 (reproduction) and P3 (decisive) held, P5 held weakly; P2 failed harmlessly (overlap monotone in 34 of 36 cells, both misses under 0.01 at n<=4), **P4 failed on the R2 half** and P6 failed on the cross-universe count — both failures are the result, not noise around it. **Offered to PROTOCOL and explicitly NOT adopted:** a reporting clause that any claim a ranking key does something must carry a null-key control run on the same grid, since |dSharpe|, the 4b pass count and rule 8's own pick are all reachable with zero information. Caveats carried: survivorship on all three current-constituent panels (idea 54); the gate is inverted on the small panel (ideas 39/49) so its numbers describe a gate that does not work there; ideas 38 and 126; idea 128's shallow IS drawdown window, which biases every selector here identically; m is a sample-average share (idea 157 owns the time-varying variant); MOM/R6/R3 are legs of the composite itself, declared before the result, which is why the decisive statistic is the within-key one; and **RAND is ONE draw of a random key on one realised path**, so the 27-of-28 and 4-of-19 counts are properties of that draw — queued as idea 180, with idea 181 pricing the proposed clause against the existing record. Script `research/backtests/2026-09-05_does-share-price-any-key-or-only-vol_B.py`; result `.result.md`; console, grid, overlap, delta, regression, IC and walk-forward CSVs alongside.
- 2026-09-05 (lane B, idea 168 the-sign-is-the-parameter-not-the-share) — **ANSWERED, and it is a KILL of the idea's own hypothesis: the tilt exponent has NO interior optimum, so `k = 0` is not a measured argmax and the project's five delete-the-scaler findings are three points read off a monotone slope. No RULES change, no new book, no new KEEP-candidate.** The reproduction gate passed **10 of 10** before any new number was read — idea 153's mean weekly eligible counts (37.50 / 91.46 / 141.23), its share->n map (u56 m=0.53 -> n=20, m=0.20 -> n=7; broad m=0.53 -> n=48), its matched-share dCAGR(POS-NONE) at m=0.20, 10 bps (u56 +2.828%/yr, broad +2.749%/yr), idea 159's signed dCAGR(INV-NONE) at m=0.53, 0 bps on u56 (-0.026806), and the cost-derivation identity `r_c = r_0 - turnover*c/1e4` against a fresh 10 bps engine run at **0.00e+00**, which is what makes every gross/net pair below the *same book* rather than two runs. **162 books** (a 9-point ladder in k over {-1.00..+1.00 step 0.25} x 3 book shares x 2 gross constructions x 3 panels), each run once at 0 bps with the 10 and 25 bps rungs derived exactly, weekly, t+1, no shorting, no leverage; two tuned parameters (k and the share m), every grid point reported. The book is idea 81/153/159's verbatim with the exponent generalised — `score_k = composite x clip(vol20, 0.08)^k`, so **k = -0.5 IS RULES v1's live score exactly**, k = 0 is the unscaled composite and k = +0.5 idea 159's POS arm. **THE CURVE IS MONOTONE.** Spearman(k, dCAGR) = **+0.93 to +1.00 in 12 of 12** large-cap (construction, share, cost) cells at 10 bps; the beneficial band's upper endpoint is **open at the top grid edge in 12 of 12**; the CAGR argmax sits at k >= +0.50 in 12 of 12 and at the grid edge (+0.75/+1.00) in **10 of 12**. On u56 lit m=0.53 at 10 bps the ladder runs 7.67% / 8.17% / **9.90% (k=-0.5, LIVE)** / 11.34% / **12.66% (k=0)** / 13.21% / 13.15% / 13.19% / 13.51% of CAGR against Sharpes 0.909 / 0.913 / 0.996 / 1.046 / 1.092 / 1.102 / 1.082 / 1.079 / 1.095 — i.e. CAGR keeps rising to the edge while **Sharpe is flat to within +-0.02 over k in [-0.25, +1.00]**, so the higher exponent buys return and vol at par. The band's LOWER endpoint is pinned at 0 by construction (k = 0 is the control, dCAGR(0) = 0 identically) and is reported as definitional, never quoted as a crossing. **The one non-definitional crossing in k is PROTOCOL 4b's, and the live tilt is on the wrong side of it:** at 10 bps the lowest k passing 4b (full sample AND OOS window) is **-0.25 in 8 of 12** large-cap cells and >= -0.25 in 12 of 12, while **k = -0.5 passes in 1 of 12** (u56 norm m=0.85 only). u56 lit m=0.53 (n=20) at 10 bps: k=-0.5 gives 9.90%/0.996/-17.4%, halves 1.054/0.950, and **fails 4b on the CAGR floor**; one rung up at k=-0.25 it passes; at k=0 it is **12.66%/1.092/-18.3%, halves 1.088/1.102, OOS 14.36%/1.168/-18.3%** against RULES v1 on the same panel and cost (6.45%/0.664/-13.8%, halves 0.641/0.688, OOS 7.73%/0.747) and SPY (15.23%/0.889/-33.7%, halves 0.957/0.834, OOS 15.45%/0.882). **At 25 bps nothing passes 4b full-sample at any k on any panel** — the H1 Sharpe bar binds. Both KEEP paths were evaluated at every book: 4a passes **0 of 54** u56 books at 10 bps (v1's own -13.8% MaxDD is shallower than every k-book's) and 44 of 54 on broad, the asymmetry PROTOCOL 4b was added on Sep 4 to correct. **Rule 8 (k chosen on 2009-2016 only, read once on 2017-2026):** mean large-cap OOS Sharpe **S_POS (k=+0.5) 1.0195 > S_ZERO (k=0) 1.0002 > S_IS 0.9979 > S_INV (k=-0.5, the live tilt) 0.8378**, and paired, **S_IS beats the do-nothing constant in 18 of 36 cells** — a coin flip, and the **fifth** instance after ideas 110/151/132/166 that an IS-fitted selector does not beat the constant it was meant to improve on (IS argmax == OOS argmax in 30.6% of cells, Spearman +0.179). What DOES transfer is the **sign**: k = -0.5 is last of four selectors in every large-cap cell, by 0.16 of Sharpe and 4.0 pp/yr of OOS CAGR. **The small panel reverses every sign** (Spearman(k, dCAGR) = -0.80 to -0.98 in 18 of 18 cells; S_POS 0.233 is the worst arm there while S_ZERO 0.323, S_IS 0.321 and S_INV 0.320 are indistinguishable), so whatever k prices is not the same object on the two panel types — idea 49/39's inverted gate carries, reported and not traded. Predictions written before any number was read: P1 reproduction CONFIRMED, P2 monotone on k<=0 CONFIRMED, **P3 Sharpe argmax >= 0 at the incumbent share FALSIFIED** (broad's is -0.25; the Sharpe curve is flat and its argmax is noise, spanning the whole 2.00-wide grid across cells), P4 argmax not sharply identified CONFIRMED, P5 IS chooser does not beat k=0 CONFIRMED, **P6 no large-cap 4b passer FALSIFIED** — 26 of 54 u56 and 22 of 54 broad books pass 4b full-sample at 10 bps, but every one is a **re-exponenting of idea 153's existing candidate** (n ~ 0.53 x mean eligible, tilt-free) and is reported, **NOT PROPOSED** (idea 144 convention, ideas 164/166 precedent). Memo written with exact RULES wording for the Sunday review, conditional on idea 153's candidate being adopted: the scaler is **deleted, not tuned** — rank on the composite alone, keep vol20 in the eligibility test only — and the positive exponent the CAGR curve points at is explicitly NOT adopted, being a grid-edge argmax that is Sharpe-neutral and sign-reversed on small caps. Caveats carried: current-constituent survivorship on all three panels (idea 54); vol20 is clipped at 0.08 before exponentiation and the gate already removes vol20 >= 0.60, so the ladder prices the slope INSIDE the gate, not a vol factor at large; at m = 0.85 all k hold most of the eligible set so dCAGR -> 0 mechanically; ideas 38 and 126 carry; one realised path, measured once. Script `research/backtests/2026-09-05_the-sign-is-the-parameter-not-the-share_B.py`, outputs `.grid.csv` / `.curve.csv` / `.band.csv` / `.argmax.csv` / `.walkforward.csv` / `.console.txt` / `.result.md` / `.memo.md`.
- 2026-09-05 — Research C (idea 171, do-gross-choice-rules-lose-to-constants-in-general): **ANSWERED — the project's general 'IS-fitting never pays' claim survives, but at 1 of 5 dials, not 5 of 5.** Ideas 110/151/132/166 each found, on ONE dial, that a parameter chosen in-sample does not beat its inherited constant. This run tested the SET: five dials (GROSS, N, BAND, CADENCE, SLEEVE), each swept over a ladder that CONTAINS its own incumbent constant with the other four held fixed, on 53 books (5 fixed panels + 48 idea-78-style sub-panels of B136, k in {20,40,80} x 16 draws), 36 ladder points, **1908 ladder rows**, 10 bps, t+1, paired book-by-book with an exact binomial sign test. Two tuned parameters (selector x ladder point); three controls (CONST, RANDOM, ORACLE). **Headline: the incumbent selector (IS-Sharpe argmax) is ahead on 3/5 dials and significant on 2/5 — CADENCE and SLEEVE — and on 0/5 when the OOS score is the 4b margin instead of raw Sharpe.** **SLEEVE is not a win, and two controls prove it:** the selector picks the ladder ENDPOINT f=0.30 in 53/53 books, byte-identical to the ORACLE (agreement 100.0%), on a ladder monotone in f (Spearman +1.000) and truncated by choice; and the RANDOM control also beats the constant there (+0.1000, t +11.2), banking 55.5% of the oracle's gain. A dial where coin-flipping wins is a dial whose grid slopes, not a dial where fitting has skill. **CADENCE is the only survivor**: paired +0.0642 OOS Sharpe (t +4.54, 40 win / 11 loss, sign p 0.0001), capture **67.1%** of the oracle against RANDOM's **-33.0%**, oracle at an INTERIOR point in 96.2% of books, selector agreeing with it 75.5% (picks M in 44/53, oracle M in 48/53) — an interior optimum driven by a mechanism (turnover cost) that transfers. **GROSS reproduces idea 166 on a fresh corpus exactly as pre-registered (P2 HIT):** mean OOS Sharpe is flat to 0.001 across the entire 0.20-1.00 ladder — gross is a near-pure SCALE lever, so its Sharpe argmax is noise — the selector consequently picks g=1.00 in 50/53 books and LOSES (mean d -0.0006, sign p 0.0008), while the 4b-margin ladder is NOT flat and peaks at g=0.80 (-0.158) with 0.75 at -0.161 and 0.70 at -0.179, i.e. idea 166's 0.70-0.80 plateau, reproduced. N loses (capture -23.9% of a +0.0900 oracle gain); BAND is not significant on a ladder whose whole mean spread is 0.018 Sharpe. **The mechanism behind the Sharpe-vs-4b sign flip:** every arm that raises mean OOS Sharpe lowers the OOS 4b pass count, because Sharpe is bought by surrendering CAGR (SLEEVE: mean OOS CAGR 9.96% -> 8.82%, Sharpe 0.964 -> 1.144, MaxDD -17.2% -> -12.6%), and the CAGR floor is 4b's most-violated bar (**1223 of 1908 rows**, vs H2 689, DD 447, OOS 405, H1 312). Rule 8 throughout: every selector read <=2016-12-31 only and 2017-> was read once. Harness controls asserted BEFORE any new number: a vectorised backtester reproduces products/backtester/engine.backtest to **6.2e-17** on returns and 1.2e-15 on turnover at all four cadences (20x faster, hence 1908 rows in 142s), and at BAND=0 the CAND-20 weights equal idea 78's `weights_cand` **exactly** (max |dw| = 0.000e+00 on U56/B136/BSTK100). 4 of 7 pre-registered predictions hit; the two informative misses are P3 (fitting wins on <=1 of 5 — it is ahead on 3) and P7 (no 4b KEEP). 4a 1507/1908, 4b 250/1908 (214 on sub-panels, which are a corpus device and not tradable; 36 on the five fixed panels, every one a re-parameterisation of an existing book per idea 144). **By-product 4b KEEP-candidate, memo'd but NOT proposed:** the standing top-20 EW book on universe.json rebalanced MONTHLY instead of weekly — full sample 14.8% / **1.208** / -19.6%, halves 1.219/1.206, OOS Sharpe **1.287** vs the incumbent weekly's 1.177 and SPY's 0.882, turnover **4.28x/yr vs 9.45x**, all five 4b bars passing; both IS selectors pick M on U56 from 2009-2016 alone and M is also the oracle. Two blockers are stated on the face of the memo: the drawdown margin is **0.6pp** (19.6% against a 20.23% cap), and the identical move **fails 4b on universe_broad by 5.9pp of drawdown** (-26.1%). PROTOCOL rule 6 reserves the change for Sunday review and idea 107 (open) owns cadence. **What PROTOCOL can now say:** an IS-fitted argmax loses on a dial that is a SCALE lever (gross) or FLAT (band), is not a choice at all on a dial whose ladder is MONOTONE and TRUNCATED (sleeve), and pays only on a dial with an INTERIOR optimum driven by a transferring mechanism — of five, only cadence — and even there it buys Sharpe with the CAGR that 4b grades on. Survivorship (idea 54), idea 38's calendar-day index and idea 126's t+1-only execution carry over. Ideas 173-175 queued: the endpoint-artefact audit across every ladder the project has fitted, the (phi, delta) satisfiability map for 4b's two bars, and a second-corpus replication of the cadence result for idea 107.
- 2026-09-05 (lane B, idea 159 the-share-at-which-ranking-stops-paying) — **ANSWERED, and it is a KILL of the question rather than an answer to it: there is no book share at which ranking stops paying, because the VALUE of a cross-sectional tilt and the COST of running it are the same function of share. No RULES change, no new book, no KEEP-candidate.** The reproduction gate passed **8 of 8** before any new number was read — ideas 81/153's INV-vs-NONE name overlap at n=20 (u56 69.4%, broad 42.5%), idea 153's matched-share dCAGR(POS-NONE) at m=0.20 (u56 +2.83%/yr, broad +2.75%/yr), its mean weekly eligible counts (37.5 / 91.5 / 141.2), and the cost-derivation identity `r_c = r_0 - turnover*c/1e4` against a fresh 10 bps engine run at **0.00e+00**, which is what makes the gross and net legs of every comparison below the *same book* rather than two runs. **180 books** (3 panels x 10 book shares x 3 tilts x 2 weighting constructions), each run once at 0 bps with the 10 and 25 bps rungs derived exactly, weekly, t+1, no shorting, no leverage; two tuned parameters (the share m and the tilt), every grid point reported. The idea asked for the share m* at which the tilt's realised magnitude g(m) = |dCAGR at 0 bps| falls below the 10 bps cost c(m) of running it, with a bootstrap interval. Three cost bars were pre-registered and all three reported: the exact incremental cost of holding the tilted book over its own no-tilt control (primary), the cost of *expressing* the tilt (10 bps x annualised sum |w_tilt - w_NONE| over rebalances, an upper bound), and a flat 0.10 pp/yr floor. **Only 10 of 36 (panel, construction, tilt, bar) cells produce a finite empirical m* at all, and 6 of those 10 sit at the grid's lower endpoint m = 0.05** — 'already noise at the smallest share tested', not a threshold. The fitted (log-linear) estimator returns -16.72, +15.82 and +86.90, which is the numerical signature of two near-parallel lines rather than a crossing. The bootstrap (circular block, block 21 d, 2000 replicates, seed 159, tilt and control resampled on the *same* block index) censors **27.5% to 83.2%** of replicates on the large-cap panels, and every interval that is not censored spans essentially the whole share axis (u56 INV [0.050, 0.934] at 83.2% censored; broad POS [0.050, 0.977] at 56.0%). Prediction P4 — that the interval would be at least 0.25 wide — held for the wrong reason: the interval is wide because the parameter does not exist, not because it is imprecisely estimated. **The diagnosis, added post-hoc after P3 failed and labelled as such throughout:** a crossing exists only if g decays in share *faster* than c does, i.e. only if d = slope(log g) - slope(log c) < 0. Bootstrapped on the same scheme, **d straddles zero in 6 of 6 (panel, tilt) cells** — u56 INV **+0.032** [-1.186, +1.097] with P(d<0) = 47.4%, broad INV +0.173 [-0.781, +1.274] at 38.5%, broad POS -0.590 [-2.116, +0.688] at 80.0%. The mechanism is arithmetic and follows directly from idea 153: both the value and the cost of a tilt are proportional to how many names it moves, and how many names it moves is exactly what book share governs, so share scales numerator and denominator alike. The ratio is flat — **g/c = 1.95 / 1.83 / 2.20 at m = 0.05 / 0.53 / 1.00 on u56** (Spearman(m, ratio) -0.224) and 1.50 / 1.47 / 1.58 on broad (+0.358). **On the honest cost bar the tilt is never expensive at all**: g/c runs **16 to 4254 on u56 and 9 to 193 on broad, at every share including the endpoints.** At the incumbent's own share (u56, m = 0.53, n = 20) the live INV tilt has **2.68 pp/yr** of gross magnitude available against **0.083 pp/yr** of true incremental cost and 1.46 pp/yr on the upper-bound bar — affordable by a factor of 32 — and it spends that magnitude as **-2.68 pp/yr**. INV's signed dCAGR is **negative at all 10 shares on u56 (-13.49 to -0.61 pp/yr) and all 10 on broad (-9.57 to -0.36)**, while POS is positive at 19 of those 20 points. **So the live vol scaler is not too expensive to run; it is wrong-signed everywhere.** That is the fifth independent delete-the-scaler finding (after ideas 72, 82, 141, 160 and 162) and the first that separates the two candidate reasons — cost and direction — and finds the cost reason absent. It also sharpens idea 82: ranking does not subtract value by costing too much, it subtracts value by pointing the wrong way; and it removes this axis as a source for idea 124's book-size floor, which must now come from a different argument. **Rule 8 (12 panel x construction x cost cells, m* re-estimated on 2009-2016 only, picks read once on 2017->):** the m*-gated selector returns mean OOS Sharpe **0.7212** against **0.8044** for doing nothing (NONE at m = 0.53), winning **1 of 12**; it beats the plain IS-Sharpe chooser by only +0.021 (4 of 12), and RULES v1 returns 0.4514. **All four selectors lose to SPY's 0.882.** The IS gate is itself unstable — 1.01 (gate nothing) in every 10 bps cell and 0.05 (gate everything) in every 25 bps cell — which is the non-existence of m* reappearing inside the selector. **Both KEEP paths, all 180 books:** 4a 22/90 literal and 22/90 gross-normalised; 4b **17/90 literal and 24/90 gross-normalised at 10 bps** and **0/90 at 25 bps under both constructions** (idea 137's wall, confirmed again). No new book: the passes are idea 153's already-published set re-measured on a finer share grid (u56 and broad, m in [0.30, 1.00], tilt NONE or POS; the incumbent u56 m=0.53/NONE/n=20 at 12.66%/1.092/-18.31%, halves 1.088/1.102, OOS 1.168), with 19 cross-universe arms all inside idea 153's interval. **INV — the live tilt — passes 4b 2 of 60 times**, both at m >= 0.85 where all three tilts hold nearly the same names. Nothing is proposed to the Sunday review. **3 of 6 predictions held.** P1 (reproduction) and P5 held; P2 failed (g is monotone in share for INV, Spearman -0.988 on u56 and -1.000 on broad, but not for POS at -0.515, nor on the small panel); **P3 is the informative failure** (no crossing strictly inside [0.15, 0.85] on either large-cap panel); P4 held for the wrong reason; P6 half-failed (the gate loses to doing nothing as predicted but edges plain IS-Sharpe by +0.021). One PROTOCOL rule-9 *reporting* clause is offered in the memo and nothing else: a claim that an instrument stops paying above some level must report the ratio of its realised magnitude to its cost across that level rather than the two curves separately, and a ratio whose log-slope difference straddles zero has no threshold and none may be quoted from it. Caveats carried: survivorship on all three current-constituent panels (idea 54); the eligibility gate is inverted on the small panel (ideas 39/49) so its numbers describe a gate that does not work there; ideas 38 and 126 carry over; idea 128 (the IS window's SPY drawdown is shallower than the OOS window's); the m -> 1.00 endpoint forces all three tilts onto the same eligible set so g -> 0 mechanically, which is why the fitted estimator is also reported on m <= 0.70 and a crossing at 1.00 is treated as no crossing; a block bootstrap on one realised path measures sampling error around that path, not uncertainty across worlds; m is a sample-average share and idea 157 (open) tests the time-varying n_t. Script `research/backtests/2026-09-05_the-share-at-which-ranking-stops-paying_B.py`; result `.result.md`; memo `.memo.md`; console, grid, curve, crossing, ratio, dslope, bootstrap and walk-forward CSVs alongside. Ideas 167 (is the value-cost parallelism general across idea 74's whole insurance menu) and 168 (sweep the tilt EXPONENT, since the sign and not the share is the live dial) queued.
- 2026-09-05 (lane C, idea 166 does-the-ceiling-beat-a-chosen-gross) — **ANSWERED, KILL of idea 148's `g = min(g_req, 1.00)` clause as a gross-CHOICE rule: out of sample it is the WORST of four rules, worse than doing nothing. No RULES change, no new book, no KEEP-candidate.** Idea 78's 300 sub-panel books (B136, k in {20,40,80} x 50 seeded draws x n in {5,20}) reproduce **EXACTLY** (max abs diff 2.2e-16 on CAGR/Sharpe/MaxDD/H1/H2/Sharpe_OOS; 4b failing-bar string identical in 100.0%), and idea 156's headline reproduces to the digit (g_req > 1.00 in **55.0%**, median 1.032; k=20/n=20 full-sample 4b pass **0.00 -> 0.12**). Four IS-only, rule-2-legal gross rules were raced on those books over a 10-point ladder (3000 ladder rows, 1200 book-arm rows, all reported): STATIC 0.75, CEILING `min(g_req,1.00)`, MAXMARG (IS 4b-margin argmax) and idea 152's interval MIDPOINT. **OOS-window 4b passes: STATIC 33, MIDPOINT 32, MAXMARG 28, CEILING 9 of 300**; paired, MIDPOINT beats CEILING **24 win / 1 lose** (+0.085 mean OOS margin, t 10.9) and the do-nothing constant beats it 32-8. The mechanism is the one idea 156 identified, pointing the other way: CEILING parks **55.0% of the books at exactly 1.00**, which cuts CAGR-bar failures 145 -> **71** and adds **101 drawdown failures (144 -> 245 of 300)** against a bar that permits only 60% of SPY's drawdown, while the three Sharpe bars are gross-invariant (H1 159/159/160/160, idea 66 confirmed). **Idea 156's `0.00 -> 0.12 -> 0.00` is one of six cells**: it is the only cell where the ceiling wins (by 0.02), and in the two richest cells it is last by 7x (k=40/n=20: 0.30 vs 0.04) and by 0.32 to **0.00** (k=80/n=20). By-product, unpredicted and stronger than the question asked for: **the incumbent constant `g = 0.75` is the ladder's own out-of-sample argmax** (OOS-4b pass 0.110 at 0.75 vs 0.027 at 1.00, 4.1x) on a 0.70-0.75 plateau, and **no IS-fitted chooser beats it** (MIDPOINT 13-14 paired) — a fourth instance of ideas 110/151/132's do-nothing result. Oracle control: only **60 of 300** books have ANY passing gross point, so gross is not the binding problem. Rule 8 (gross AND sub-panel chosen on <= 2016-12-31, read once on 2017->, 24 picks): mean OOS Sharpe **STATIC 0.9620 > MIDPOINT 0.9521 ~ MAXMARG 0.9519 > CEILING 0.9372**, 4a passes 5/6/6/**2**, every pick above RULES v1's OOS 0.576; idea 156's own pick reproduces (k=20 at g=1.00, OOS 11.16%/1.103/-13.93%) and the same book at g=0.90 gives 10.04%/1.104/**-12.58%**. Both KEEP paths reported: **4a 646/1200, 4b 135/1200 full-sample and 102/1200 OOS-window — every one a re-grossing of a book already in the record, none proposed** (idea 144 convention, idea 164 precedent). 6 of 6 pre-registered predictions hit, with the ceiling's loss to the constant recorded as unpredicted. What survives of ideas 148/156 is the **diagnostic** `g_req` (idea 165, open), not a rule that sets gross from it. Ideas 170-172 queued.
- 2026-09-05 (cloud, idea 156 the-CAGR-floor-is-what-kills-small-pools) — **ANSWERED, KILL of the artefact hypothesis as posed: 4b's CAGR floor IS gross-sensitive, but it is NOT separable from the drawdown cap, and removing it by re-grossing simply relocates every failure onto the cap. No RULES change, no new book, no KEEP-candidate.** Idea 78's 300 sub-panel books (B136, k in {20,40,80} x 50 seeded draws x n in {5,20}) were re-run from the same `default_rng(78_500 + k)` stream and reproduce its committed `gridB.csv` **EXACTLY** (max abs diff 2.2e-16 on every metric; failing-bar string identical in 100.0% of 300 books; k=20/n=20 fails on CAGR in 50 of 50, k=80/n=20 passes 46.0%). Scaling each book to SPY's 17.72% realised vol lands within **0.14%** of target and leaves mean Sharpe unchanged to four decimals (0.8996 -> 0.9000 -> 0.8994 across a 0.75 -> 1.13 mean-gross move), so the test is clean. **The mechanical half of the premise is confirmed: uncapped vol matching removes 106 of the 119 CAGR-bar failures.** The conclusion does not follow: **DD-bar failures rise 146 -> 297 of 300 and corpus 4b passes fall 42 -> 2**, with **0** of the 38 books whose ONLY failure was the CAGR floor becoming a pass and **37 of 38 becoming DD-only failures**. The reason is structural and belongs to 4b rather than to these books: 4b demands 100% of SPY's Sharpe in both halves and out of sample but only **60%** of its drawdown, so a book run at SPY's volatility cannot clear the cap. **Idea 148's no-leverage ceiling is confirmed and strengthened**: 55.0% of the 300 books need g > 1.00 to reach SPY's vol, the driver is the book's cash weight rather than the pool size (k=20/n=20 runs 6.94% vol and needs a median **1.82x**, 100% of its draws levered; k=80/n=5 needs 0.77x), and `g = min(g_req, 1.00)` is the ONLY setting under which the small-pool cell passes 4b at all (0.00 -> 0.12 -> 0.00 as the ceiling is applied then lifted). Rule 8 makes it concrete: idea 78's own k=20 pick, which failed 4b on the CAGR floor, **passes** re-grossed to the ceiling (OOS 11.16%/1.103/-13.93% vs SPY OOS 15.45%/0.882/-33.72% and RULES v1 B136 OOS 5.94%/0.576/-21.19%) and **fails again on drawdown at g = 1.66** — its 4b verdict is a statement about where g = 1.00 sits, not about the signal. Across all 1500 book-arm rows, 4b passes requiring leverage: **0**. Predictions scored 3.5 of 6, with P3 ("most CAGR-only failures flip to a pass uncapped") a clean miss recorded as one. Three clauses proposed to the Sunday review: state 4b's CAGR floor and DD cap as ONE joint risk-budget constraint; record each book's required vol-matching gross beside its 4b verdict; strengthen idea 148's ceiling clause.
- 2026-09-05 (cloud, idea 152 price-the-broad-POS-near-miss) — **ANSWERED: the near-miss is real, is priced exactly, and is NOT capital-worthy. PARK the broad-only book; KILL the cross-universe KEEP claim. No RULES change, no new live book.** Idea 81 left two books one bar short on `universe_broad.json` @10bps, n=20: `POS` (composite x sqrt(vol20)) failing 4b on the DRAWDOWN cap alone by 1.14pp, and `NONE` failing on H2 alone by 0.023. Those two failures are not the same kind of failure, and that is what this run establishes on one harness. Both premises **reproduce exactly** (POS 16.0%/1.052/-21.4%, halves 1.197/0.927, OOS 1.005, to published precision; `INV/n=5/w=0.15` equals `baseline.rules_v1_weights` to 0.000e+00 on all three panels). Idea 66's **exact Sharpe-neutral lever is confirmed near-exactly** — over a 25-point gross ladder spanning a 7x range, the max Sharpe spread in any of the 18 (panel, cost, scaler) cells is **0.0112** — so H1/H2/OOS are effectively gross-invariant while DD and CAGR are not. Consequently **POS's DD failure closes and NONE's H2 failure cannot**: POS has an unlevered 4b interval at **m in [0.55, 0.70]** (4 of 25 points; m=0.75 sits one step above the DD ceiling; the best point, m=0.70, gives 14.91%/1.051/-20.04% with a **+0.02pp** DD margin, having bought 1.14pp of drawdown with 1.07pp of CAGR), while NONE's H2 moves **0.8067 -> 0.8154** against a 0.8340 bar across the entire ladder and is empty at every m. But the interval does not survive its own preconditions: **cross-universe 4b is 0 of 25 m-points** (u56 passes only at m>=0.65; the sub-$2B panel fails all five bars at every gross, exactly as idea 81's panel-signed tilt predicts), and **0 of 150 unlevered points pass 4b at 25 bps** on any panel with any scaler, so idea 82's proposed breakeven bar kills the family outright. Rule 8 adds one clean datum for the standing selector question: on broad@10bps plain IS-Sharpe picks m=1.00 and **fails** the OOS-window 4b on drawdown (OOS 20.86%/1.007/-27.80%) while the 4b-aware IS screen picks POS/m=0.60 and **passes** it (12.56%/1.003/-17.34%, vs SPY OOS 15.45%/0.882/-33.72% and RULES v1 broad OOS 5.94%/0.576/-21.19%) — the screen earns its complexity here, through the DD bar specifically. Census over all 450 grid points: 4a 140, 4b 13, all unlevered, all @10bps, none on small. **6 of 6 pre-registered predictions hit.** Two reporting clauses proposed to the Sunday review (classify every 4b near-miss by which bar it fails, since DD/CAGR failures are closable on the gross ladder and Sharpe-bar failures are not; and record any de-grossed 4b pass with its interval and cross-universe count in the same row). Memo: `research/backtests/2026-09-05_price-the-broad-POS-near-miss_cloud.memo.md`.
- 2026-09-05 (lane B, idea 160 selectivity-as-the-book-parameter) — **ANSWERED, KILL of the proposal: a book should keep fixing its NAME COUNT n. Fixing the SHARE q of the eligible set is a correctly-built instrument that buys nothing and costs 1.9 turns a year. Rules unchanged, no book, no promotion.** Two tuned parameters (q in {0.10, 0.20, 0.30, 0.40, 0.53, 0.65, 0.80, 1.00} x the count rule in {FIXQ, FIXN}) = **16 grid points per cell, every one printed**, on 3 panels x 2 cost rungs = **96 backtests**, weekly, t+1, 75% gross, full-gross rebuild on BOTH arms so the comparison is not confounded by idea 81's silent de-grossing. The control is matched: for each q the FIXN arm uses n = round(q x mean E_t) taken over the **IS window 2009-2016 only**, so the two arms hold the same number of names on average and differ only in whether that number tracks E_t. Three checks passed before any new number was read: the trading-day index is clean on all three panels (idea 38, max 253 rows/yr); idea 2's published KEEP comes back at **12.65974% / 1.09214 / -18.30835%, halves 1.08828 / 1.10155** under its own dg convention, matching the CHANGELOG's re-derivable value to every printed digit; and the live RULES v1 row at 6.45305% / 0.66418 / -13.82780%. **(1) A pre-registered premise FAILED before any book was run, and it predicted the answer.** Idea 78's Spearman(q, spread) = -0.975 is a CROSS-CELL statistic; the time-series version a share-tracking rule would have to harvest is **-0.069 on u56 (919 weeks) and -0.013 on broad (920 weeks)**, with non-monotone quintile spreads on both panels. There was never anything there. **(2) THE ANSWER: 32 primary comparisons (2 large-cap panels x 2 costs x 8 q), paired on daily returns — mean dSharpe -0.0257 (FIXQ wins 9/32), dCAGR -0.048pp (11/32), dMaxDD -4.40pp (FIXQ shallower 8/32), dOOS_Sharpe -0.0394 (9/32), turnover +1.86 x/yr. Paired daily |t| >= 2 in 2 of 32 and BOTH are negative.** The secondary small panel is worse (dSharpe -0.055, FIXQ wins 2/16). **(3) The instrument is not broken — it is inert.** FIXQ cuts the realised selectivity sd from 0.09-0.20 to 0.003-0.044, a **12x to 22x** stabilisation, and moves Sharpe by -0.026. **(4) What it does buy is a slow-bear tilt priced in fast-crash drawdown, which is idea 46's adaptive count recovered on a new instrument with the sign of its cost now visible: 2022 mean +2.35pp (better in 26/32), 2020 mean -3.69pp (better in 5/32)** — and 2020 is where the drawdown is, which is why FIXQ is deeper in 24 of 32. **(5) Both KEEP paths on all 96 books: 4a 22 (FIXQ 7 / FIXN 15), 4b full-sample 15 (5 / 10), 4b on the OOS window 23 (7 / 16).** Every 4b pass is 10 bps and large-cap; **0 of 32 small-panel books passes anything**, a sixth independent reproduction of idea 136. **(6) One 4b pass is recorded and explicitly NOT proposed: `u56 @10bps, FIXQ q = 0.53` reads 13.17% / 1.0572 / -19.36%, halves 0.9822 / 1.1270, OOS 15.54% / 1.1813 / -19.36% — the highest OOS Sharpe of all 96 books** — but its matched control `FIXN n = 19` also passes 4b (1.0390, OOS 1.1072) so the instrument is not what earns it, it fails 4b on broad (DD cap) and at 25 bps (H1, DD) so it clears neither a second panel nor idea 82's proposed 25 bps bar, and its H1 margin over SPY is 0.025. **(7) Rule 8** (parameters read 2009-2016 only, 2017-2026 read once, 4 primary cells): **this idea's own selector is LAST of five and loses to a RANDOM pick by 0.256 of OOS Sharpe** — S0 do-nothing at the published n=20 **0.9356** (12.36% / -19.2%), S4 random **0.9580**, S3 IS-argmax over FIXN 0.9322, S1 IS-argmax over all 16 arms 0.8000, **S2 IS-argmax over FIXQ 0.7019 at -32.9% drawdown**; all 6 cells 0.754 / 0.744 / 0.774 / 0.686 / **0.557**. Against SPY OOS 0.882 and RULES v1 OOS 0.662. The IS window sends every informed selector to q = 0.10 (5 of 6 S1 picks, 6 of 6 S2 picks) and the concentration does not survive; the do-nothing control beats SPY and three of four selectors — ideas 132/141/149/151 on a seventh bar. **(8) THE TRANSFERABLE FINDING is the LEVEL of q, not whether it is pinned.** Sharpe is near-monotone increasing in q on both large-cap panels and both arms (**Spearman +0.571 to +0.976**, argmax q = 1.00 in 3 of 4 primary cells), the Sharpe range across q is **0.117-0.230 per cell against a 0.026 FIXQ-vs-FIXN gap** — the level is **5-9x more consequential** than pinning it, and the winning level is hold-everything-eligible. That is a **fourth independent derivation of ideas 72 / 82 / 141's 'the ranking subtracts value'**, reached from the selectivity axis. It inverts on the small panel (argmax q = 0.10, Spearman -0.524), which is ideas 39/49's universe clause again. **Not reproduced and reported as such:** the queue's 'B136: n_elig 3 to 127, so q swings 16x week to week' — the eligible count does range 3 to 127 (42x) but a top-20 book's q is capped at 1.0 and swings **6.35x**; the ~16x swing (14.45x) belongs to the **small** panel, not to B136. Caveats carried: survivorship on all three current-constituent panels (idea 54), which runs AGAINST the arm this run rejects since FIXQ holds MORE names in bad weeks — exactly where delisted names would sit; idea 128's shallow IS drawdown window, which biases every rule-8 selector identically; idea 126 (t+1 only, no spread or impact model); no IWM in the cache so the small panel is judged against SPY, stated not adjusted; and q = 0.53 is imported from idea 153 and is therefore not free of that run's own fitting. Script: `research/backtests/2026-09-05_selectivity-as-the-book-parameter_B.py`; result: `.result.md`; memo: `.memo.md`.
- 2026-09-05 (cloud, idea 81 vol20-as-the-hidden-ranking-key) — **KILL of the inversion, and a third independent CONFIRM of the deletion: RULES v1's `/sqrt(vol20)` is signed against its own panel and should GO, but the fix is to stop dividing, not to start multiplying. No RULES change from a research script; 0 of 36 books is a cross-universe KEEP.** Idea 80's `fama_macbeth` and its book constructor were IMPORTED and called verbatim, so the premise under test is literally idea 80's own measurement, and three checks passed before any new number was read: `INV / n=5 / w=0.15` equals `baseline.rules_v1_weights` at **max|diff| 0.000e+00** on all three panels (the INV arm IS the live book, not a lookalike); `NONE / n=20` is **identical** to idea 80's committed `CAND/COMP/n=20` path (12.65974% / 1.09214 / -18.30835%, halves 1.08828 / 1.10155); and idea 80's published bivariate vol20 slopes come back **+0.00450 (t +3.90)** on u56 and **+0.00294 (t +3.19)** on broad with low-vol rank ICs **-0.0428 / -0.0332** against its published -0.0426 / -0.0329. **Not fully reproduced and reported as such:** idea 2's published KEEP row reads Sharpe **1.093** and halves 1.088 / **1.103**; this run and idea 80's own committed code both give **1.09214 / 1.08828 / 1.10155** — CAGR and MaxDD match exactly, the last published digit of Sharpe and H2 is not re-derivable from any committed script, and the 0.001 gap is recorded rather than hidden. Exactly two tuned parameters (the scaler in {INV `/sqrt(v)`, NONE `x1`, POS `x sqrt(v)`} and n in {5, 20}) = **6 grid points, every one reported**, on 3 panels x 2 cost rungs = **36 books**, weekly, t+1, equal-weight at 0.75/n so count and gross are not one dial. **(1) THE ANSWER: the first dose is the whole effect.** Paired daily t-tests on the same panel/cost/n: deleting the scaler (**NONE - INV**) is worth **+5.08%/yr, mean t +2.37**, and is significant in **8 of 8 large-cap cells** (u56 t +3.33..+3.79, broad t +2.82..+3.48); inverting it (**POS - NONE**) is worth **-0.05%/yr, mean t +0.64**, significant on **exactly one of three panels** (broad +2.5..+5.2pp, t +2.02..+3.15), indistinguishable on u56 (+0.5..+1.6pp, t +0.74..+1.14) and negative on small (-1.6..-8.6pp). Mean dSharpe **+0.145** then **-0.034**. P2 (POS > NONE > INV) held in **4 of 12** cells and needed 8 of 8. **(2) The sign is real, the dose is not.** The Sharpe peak is **NONE on all four u56 cells, POS on all four broad cells, INV on three of four small cells** — and the winning tilt's sign matches its panel's own vol premium sign in **3 of 3 panels**. The small panel is a new number idea 80 never ran and it **REVERSES**: bivariate slope **-0.00084 (t -0.95)**, low-vol rank IC **+0.0195 (t +3.84)** — inside the gate the sub-$2B panel pays LOW vol. Any vol wording in RULES needs the same large-cap universe clause ideas 39/49 demanded of the trend gate. **(3) Mechanism, and it is NOT the slope's t.** u56 has the LARGER slope and gets nothing; broad has the smaller slope and gets +2.5..+5.2pp/yr. What separates them is book share: at n=20 the INV and NONE books overlap **69.4%** of their union on u56 (20 names from a 56-name list, ~37.5 eligible) against **42.5%** on broad (20 from 136). A cross-sectional key only pays where the book is a small slice of the cross-section — queued as idea 153. **(4) What POS costs:** MaxDD deeper than NONE's in **12 of 12** cells, held-name vol20 0.175 (INV) -> 0.321 (NONE) -> 0.383 (POS), turnover 23.2 -> 18.8 -> 22.7 x/yr (INV is also the most expensive arm). 10 of POS's 11 4b failures include the drawdown cap. **(5) One documented near-miss, stated precisely: `broad @10bps, POS, n=20` reads 16.0% / 1.052 / -21.4%, halves 1.197 / 0.927, OOS Sharpe 1.005, and fails 4b on the DRAWDOWN CAP ALONE by 1.2pp** — while the same book without the tilt fails on **H2 alone** (0.811 vs 0.834). The tilt fixes broad's H2 failure and breaks its drawdown: a swap of binding bars, not an improvement. Queued as idea 152. **(6) Both KEEP paths on all 36 books: 4a 6, 4b full-sample 2, 4b on the OOS window 4, cross-universe 4b 0.** Both 4b passes are u56 @10bps at n=20 (NONE, the standing idea-2 candidate recovered independently, and POS); neither survives 25 bps (both fail H1) or a second panel. **The whole INV arm — the live book's own tilt — passes 4b 0 of 12 and fails the CAGR floor 12 of 12.** **(7) Rule 8** (two selection rules fixed before any OOS number was read): the plain-Sharpe rule picks a **different scaler on every panel** — NONE n=20 on u56 (OOS **14.36% / 1.168 / -18.3%** at 10bps, 12.73% / 1.050 / -18.4% at 25, both clearing 4b on the OOS window), POS on broad (22.35% / 1.009 / -29.7%; 13.02% / 0.856 / -22.8%, neither clearing), INV n=20 on small (3.30% / 0.313 / -29.3%; -0.56% / 0.023 / -37.6%) — against **SPY OOS 15.45% / 0.882 / -33.7%** and RULES v1 OOS 1.11%-7.92% / 0.155-0.747 by cell. Pick counts NONE 2 / POS 2 / INV 2: the same universe fragility ideas 49 and 55 found, now on the scaler axis. **The 4b-aware rule picks NOTHING in 6 of 6 cells** (no in-sample point met the IS drawdown cap) — the fifth independent reproduction of ideas 38, 39, 46 and 49. **For the Sunday review:** (a) the evidence to drop `/sqrt(vol20)` is now overwhelming and comes from three independent runs (idea 1 at n=5, idea 2 at n=20, this one across 3 panels x 2 costs x 2 n with paired t-tests); (b) do NOT replace it with the opposite tilt; (c) any vol wording carries the large-cap universe clause. Caveats carried: survivorship on all three current-constituent panels (idea 54), which runs AGAINST the POS arm being real since the high-vol cohort is exactly where delisted names would sit and POS still loses on 2 of 3 panels; idea 128's shallow IS drawdown window; ideas 39/49 (the gate is inverted on the small panel, so every small-panel number describes a gate that does not work there); ideas 38 and 126; and no IWM in the cache, so the small panel is judged against SPY, stated not adjusted. New ideas 152, 153. Script: `research/backtests/2026-09-05_vol20-as-the-hidden-ranking-key_cloud.py`; result: `.result.md`.
- 2026-09-05 (cloud, idea 149 quote-the-lift-not-the-rate) — **ANSWERED: the CONDITIONING ARTIFACT generalises to both statistics the queue named and is worse there than where idea 141 found it; the PRESCRIPTION does not generalise and is killed. No RULES change, no new book, no KEEP candidate; one proposed PROTOCOL reporting clause.** Idea 94's harness, idea 129's screen machinery and idea 90's interval machinery were IMPORTED rather than re-implemented, and four checks passed before any new number was read: `H.run` vs `engine.backtest` **0.000e+00** on all three panels, idea 141's 306-row grid to **max 2.22e-16 with 0 adm_S1 disagreements**, idea 129's POINT census **306 / 29 / 27**, and idea 90's family (7,650 rows, read not re-derived — this run's own 306 rows match its m=1.00 slice at 2.22e-16) reproducing **72 / 58 / 29** and its published "**4 of 51** pairs". Corpus 3 panels x 3 books x 17 arms x 2 cost rungs = **306 backtests**; exactly two tuned parameters (tightness qmax in 4 values, conditioning convention in 2) = **8 grid points per statistic, all printed**, at 4,000 bar draws per (cell, qmax) and 20,000 size-matched null draws per published-bar statistic. **(1) The artifact is TOTAL, not partial (P1 held harder than its own >=2x bar).** Idea 90's operational KEEP reads **4 of 51 (book, arm) pairs over four large-cap cells and 0 of 51 over all six**; at m<=1.00, **3 -> 0**; idea 129's POINT census read the same way, **2 -> 0**. Per-cell interval counts are the whole story — u56@10 **31**, u56@25 **23**, broad@10 **14**, broad@25 **4**, **small@10 0, small@25 0** — because a joint "passes in every cell" statistic is multiplicative and one empty cell zeroes it. Under randomised bars the same convention inflates the count **20.2x** at qmax=1.00 (COND 0.319 vs UNCOND 0.016) and is identical to the digit at qmax<=0.50, where no cell is ever empty. **(2) Both statistics nonetheless survive their own nulls decisively (P3 held), so nothing published needs withdrawing — only re-quoting.** Cell count **4 vs a size-matched null of 0.301 = 13.3x, p<0.0001** (m<=1.00: 3 vs 0.127 = 23.6x); POINT census **2 vs 0.0076 = 263x, p<0.0001**; idea 129's floor-only KILL count **27 vs 19.48 = 1.39x, p=0.0010** against a null preserving each bar's per-cell failure count — so "27 of 277" is honestly *about seven more than chance*, not 27. The RANDOM control lands on the null to **0.0020** at every qmax (P5 of idea 141 re-held), so the nulls are trustworthy. **(3) P2 FAILED in 0 of 8 readings and REVERSES in one family: "quote the lift" is wrong here.** Raw vs lift across tightness — B1's best cross-cell pass rate is raw-**monotone** (1.000 -> 1.000 -> 0.802 -> 0.649) and lift-**non-monotone** (0.444 -> 0.678 -> 0.651 -> 0.573), the exact defect the lift was proposed to cure. The mechanism is measurable and is the transferable part: idea 141's artifact needs the conditioned-away mass to be large, and its cells hold **17** arms so P(non-empty) falls to **0.424** at qmax=1.00; these statistics live on cells of **51** (book, arm) pairs where P(non-empty) never falls below **0.997**. The artifact is a function of P(non-empty), not of the statistic — and where the raw rate is already well behaved, substituting the lift makes the reported quantity worse. Idea 141's own anchor re-derived with independent draws to **0.0039** (Monte-Carlo agreement, stated as such, not identity). **(4) Rule 8** (selectors read 2009-2016 only, picks read once on 2017-2026, 18 cells, equal-weighted): **debiasing a cross-cell pass count by its size-matched null changes 0 of 18 picks** — S3_LIFT and S2_RAW are the same book everywhere, because the correction is near-constant across arms within a cell. And every informed selector LOSES to doing nothing: **S0 do-nothing 10.65% / 0.762 / -27.4%**, S1 IS-Sharpe 9.06% / 0.695 / -23.1% (**-0.067** paired, better in 3 of 18), S2=S3 7.62% / **0.649** / -21.2% (**-0.113**, better in 6 of 18), S4 RANDOM 10.14% / 0.755 / -26.1% (**-0.007**) — a random admissible arm is closer to the control than any informed selector. Reference OOS: **SPY 15.45% / 0.882 / -33.7%**; RULES v1 1.11%-7.92% / 0.155-0.747 by cell. **(5) Both KEEP paths, on every distinct arm any selector picked**: 42 distinct (cell, arm) picks, **13 pass 4a, 6 pass 4b full-sample, 5 pass 4b on the OOS window alone** — every one a pre-existing leaderboard book. **No KEEP, no promotion, nothing changes.** Bears on ideas 90, 129 and 110: the recommended reporting fix is NOT idea 149's own, it is to quote the **cell set and the per-cell counts** beside any joint pass count and to state the un-conditioned reading (proposed clause in the memo). Caveats carried: survivorship on all three current-constituent panels (idea 54), which runs *against* this file's main finding since the small panel is the one that admits nothing; idea 128's shallow IS drawdown window, which biases every selector in (4) identically; ideas 38 and 126; and section C inherits idea 90's family file, licensed by check [d] and nothing else. Script: `research/backtests/2026-09-05_quote-the-lift-not-the-rate_cloud.py`; result: `.result.md`; memo: `.memo.md`.
- 2026-09-05 (lane C, idea 141 is-Calmar-immunity-general) — **KILL of the premise: the immunity idea 132 found belongs to ONE BAR VECTOR, not to risk-adjusted-return selectors as a class. Rules unchanged, no new book.** Idea 132's corpus was re-derived rather than read: engine-equivalence **0.000e+00** on all three panels, **306 of 306 rows to max|diff| 2.2e-16**, **0** adm_S1 disagreements, and its four published immunity rates back **exactly** (K_Sharpe 100%, K_Calmar 100%, K_CAGR 57%, K_MaxDD 29%) before any new number was read. TWO tuned parameters, both swept exhaustively: the selector (8 values — 5 risk-adjusted, 3 not) and the screen tightness qmax (0.25/0.50/0.75/1.00) = **32 grid points, all printed**, at 4000 randomised bar draws x 18 cells, plus a K_RANDOM control. Each bar is drawn at a quantile q ~ U(0, qmax) of that cell's own 17 arm values, so the screen keeps 4b's SHAPE (IS_H1 > b1, IS_H2 > b2, |IS_MaxDD| < bDD, IS_CAGR > bC) and loses its calibration. **(1) The premise dies at the published point already**: extending idea 132's table to eight selectors, the RA selector K_H2Sharpe scores **43%** against the non-RA K_CAGR's **57%** — the class boundary does not partition immunity even where idea 132 measured. **(2) Under randomised bars NOBODY is immune.** K_Sharpe falls 1.000 -> **0.634-0.789** and K_Calmar 1.000 -> **0.641-0.793** across the four tightness levels, against a pre-registered bar of >=0.90 (P1 FAILED). **(3) What survives is graded and real.** Lift over the size-matched null E[k/n | k>0] runs **+0.216 -> +0.491** (K_Sharpe) and **+0.220 -> +0.514** (K_Calmar): at the tightest screen a risk-adjusted argmax is admissible **4.0-4.2x more often than chance**. The control K_RANDOM reproduces the analytic null to **0.0027** (P5 held), so the lift is trustworthy. The correct claim is 'unusually screen-robust', a quantity, not 'immune', an assertion. **(4) The mechanism is CO-MONOTONICITY, not class (P2, P4 held).** Immunity is ordered by each selector's weakest rank-correlation with the four statistics the bars are written on: **Spearman(rho_min, immunity) = +0.881** over the 8 selectors (rho_mean +0.833), and the ordering crosses the RA/NRA boundary (K_CAGR, rho_min -0.433, outranks the RA K_H2Sharpe at 3 of 4 qmax). **What actually cuts a Sharpe/Calmar/Sortino argmax is 4b's DRAWDOWN CAP** (0.39-0.55 of exclusions), not its CAGR floor — the floor is what cuts K_MaxDD and K_NegVol (0.88 / 0.93). **(5) P3 FAILED and is reported as found**: the raw immunity RATE is non-monotone in tightness (0.789 -> 0.674 -> 0.634 -> 0.652) because at qmax=1.00 only **42.5%** of draws leave a non-empty set and conditioning on non-emptiness selects the looser draws; the LIFT, which that conditioning does not distort, IS monotone for all six informative selectors. **Quote the lift, not the rate** (new idea 149). **(6) Rule 8 was read once** on 2017-2026 (screens and selectors read 2009-2016 only, FALLBACK convention): all 32 selector x tightness points span **0.698-0.773 OOS Sharpe** and **7.1%-10.2% OOS CAGR**, with the RANDOM selector at **0.738-0.753** — inside the pack and above 5 of 8 selectors at qmax=1.00. Under PROTOCOL's own published bars the eight selectors span **0.017** (0.743-0.760). Every point loses to **SPY (OOS 0.882 / 15.45% / -33.7%)** and none beats the do-nothing ungated control (**0.762 / 10.65% / -27.4%**) by more than 0.011; all beat RULES v1 (0.451 / 4.86% / -25.3%). **Choosing among these selectors is worth nothing out of sample.** **(7) KEEP paths, both, on every arm any screen picked**: 248 distinct arms, **82 pass 4a**, **28 pass 4b full-sample**, **30 pass 4b on the OOS window alone** — every one a pre-existing leaderboard book. **No KEEP, no promotion, nothing changes.** Bears on idea 110, which this run says is mis-framed twice over: the immunity that motivated it is calibration rather than structure, and the selector choice moves OOS Sharpe by 0.017 under the published bars against a 0.120 gap to SPY — idea 110 should be answered on OOS return, not on screen behaviour. Caveats carried: survivorship on all three current-constituent panels (idea 54); idea 128's shallow IS drawdown window, which makes the DD bar's exclusion share a LOWER bound; t+1 execution only (idea 126); and the bars are drawn from each cell's own cross-arm distribution, so this file measures the screen's SHAPE, not 4b's economic content. New ideas 149, 150, 151. Script: `research/backtests/2026-09-05_is-Calmar-immunity-general_C.py`; result: `.result.md`.
- 2026-09-05 (cloud, idea 82 cross-universe-breakeven-as-a-KEEP-bar) — **ANSWERED: the cost breakeven IS a well-defined, statable KEEP bar; the proposed number is 25 bps; and idea 2's standing 4b KEEP does not clear PROTOCOL's own 10 bps assumption cross-universe.** 2 panels x 32 (book, arm) pairs x 61 cost points on a 0.5 bp grid = **3,904 backtests**; two tuned parameters (phi in 7 values x delta in 6), cost swept exhaustively, all 420 (phi, delta, C*) points reported. Checks first: `H.run` vs `engine.backtest` **max|diff| = 0.000e+00** and idea 94's published `EWall+vol60-dg` u56@10bps row exact (**11.587% / 1.133 / -16.884%**). **(1) IDEA 11 REPRODUCES TO THE DECIMAL.** Idea 2's `CAND-n20` in the full-gross-rebuild (`rw`) convention breaks even cross-universe at **7.5 bps** — idea 11's published number — and `EWall + v1gate-rw` on `broad` at **10.5 bps**, idea 11's 'EWall 10.5'. **(2) THE RANKING.** Cross-universe breakeven: idea 57 `EWall+band3-rw` **>= 30 bps** (grid-limited) = incumbent `EWall+vol60-dg` **>= 30** > idea 46's fraction book f=0.85 **14.0** > idea 2 **7.5 (rw) / 4.0 (dg)** > idea 72's `EWall` — which passes at **no cost at all** under either reading tried. **(3) THE DE-GROSS CONVENTION IS LOAD-BEARING, and this run got it wrong first.** `EWall + band3` holds cross-universe 4b to >= 30 bps under `rw` (idea 91's published form) and **never passes at any cost on either universe** under `dg`. A first draft of this run assumed `dg` for idea 57 and reported it as a never-passer; the arm space was widened to 4 gates x {dg, rw} and re-run rather than reconciled. Any cost bar RULES states must name the convention. **(4) THE OBJECT IS WELL DEFINED**: the passing cost set is contiguous in **27 of 27** measurable (book, arm, panel) triples, zero interior holes, so a single breakeven number is meaningful. First-failing bar over 64 triples: CAGR floor **31**, DD cap **22**, H2 12, H1 10, OOS Sharpe 9 — the DD-cap entries are mostly books already failing at 0 bps and are stated as not cost-driven. **(5) RULE 8** (choose on 2009-2016, evaluate 2017-2026 untouched): the cross-universe IS->OOS breakeven spread is mean **+3.2 bps**, median +4.5, range **-8.0 .. +12.0**, Spearman **+0.580** over the 6 books measurable in both windows, with the IS breakeven **understating** the OOS one in 5 of 6 — the opposite of idea 128's prediction. Per-panel the spread reaches **+29.5** (u56/TOP20 arms), which is the register idea 11's '+26 / -13.5' belongs to. **(6) THE BAR.** Books holding cross-universe 4b to at least C*, at the published bars, out of 36: **10** at 0 bps, 9 at 5, 7 at 7.5, **6 at 10**, 4 at 15, **3 at 20/25/30**. Proposed RULES clause: a KEEP must hold cross-universe 4b to at least **25 bps** (PROTOCOL's 10 plus the +12 bps upper walk-forward spread, rounded to the published rung), quoting the breakeven and the convention with it; the rung is a judgement and the full curve is published so the Sunday review can choose another. **(7) CONSEQUENCE**: at 10 bps idea 2's candidate fails 4b on `broad` (OOS 12.5% / 0.892 / -20.1% vs SPY 15.45% / 0.882 / -33.7%) while idea 57's `band3-rw` passes 4b on **both** universes at **both** published rungs (u56 OOS 13.4%/**1.203**/-17.7% at 10 bps, 12.7%/1.143/-17.9% at 25; broad 12.0%/1.076/-18.5% and 11.2%/1.010/-18.7%) — beating SPY's Sharpe in all four cells at roughly half its drawdown. Idea 46's fraction book clears both universes at 10 bps and neither at 25. No RULES change, no new book, no new KEEP candidate; proposed wording in `research/backtests/2026-09-05_cross-universe-breakeven-as-a-KEEP-bar_cloud.memo.md`. Survivorship (current-constituent lists), the small panel's deliberate exclusion, idea 38's calendar-day index, and the absence of any spread/impact model are carried in the script header and the memo.
- 2026-09-05 (cloud, idea 148 the-no-leverage-ceiling-is-load-bearing) — **ANSWERED: the premise is CONFIRMED but narrower than idea 144 implied. PROTOCOL rule 2's no-leverage clause IS doing part of 4b's adequacy work — and it does so exclusively through the CAGR floor.** Idea 94's harness and idea 144's corpus were imported rather than re-implemented, and four checks passed before any new number was read: `H.run` vs `engine.backtest` **max|diff| = 0.000e+00**, idea 94's published EWall+vol60-dg u56@10bps row (**11.587% / 1.133 / -16.884%**), idea 129/131's POINT census at m=1.00 (**306 rows / 29 pass4b / 27 floor-only KILL**), and idea 144's own floor-exclusion claim (**51 at c=1.00 -> 37 at c=1.30**, exact). 306 books x a 26-point gross family = **7,956 backtests**; two tuned parameters (phi in 7 values, delta in 6), the ceiling swept exhaustively over its legal range and every one of 13 x 42 = 546 verdict sets reported. **(1) THE ANSWER: 57 of 306 4b verdicts (18.6%) are decided by WHERE the ceiling is put rather than by either bar** over c = 0.75 .. 1.3333 (1.3333 x 75% target = 100% gross, which is where rule 2 actually bites — not idea 144's 1.30). 16 books pass at every legal ceiling, 233 at none, 57 flip somewhere. FAMILY-4b passes run **16 -> 73** across the sweep (58 at c=1.00, 72 at 1.30) and the CAGR floor's unique exclusions run **93 -> 36**. **(2) THE MECHANISM IS SINGLE-CHANNEL.** Of the 57 ceiling-decided KILLs at c=0.75, **57 fail the CAGR floor** and **55 fail only the floor**; **0 fail the DD cap at any ceiling** — as they cannot, since the cap tightens with gross while the floor relaxes. Deleting the floor (phi = 0) drives the ceiling-decided count to **0 at all 6 deltas x all 13 ceilings**. The clause has no bite of its own: it is a second, unlabelled setting of the adequacy bar. **(3) IDEA 144's OWN CEILING WAS SAFE BY PLACEMENT, NOT BY CONSTRUCTION**: c=1.30 vs the legal 1.3333 changes **1** verdict, but c=1.00 changes 15 and c=0.75 changes 57, and at c=1.30 the ceiling-decided count ranges **0..5 (median 0)** over the 42-point bar grid against **0..84** at c=0.75. **(4) 4a IS CEILING-BLIND**: 184 of 306 books pass FAMILY-4a at *every* ceiling, so the whole interaction is 4b's. **(5) Rule 8** (choose on 2009-2016, evaluate 2017-2026 untouched, 18 cells): every family ceiling beats the no-screen control by **+0.032** paired OOS Sharpe and the m-pinned screen (idea 146's recommendation) by **+0.010**, best at c=1.20 (**0.710**) — but all sit **0.17-0.20 below SPY's 0.882** (OOS CAGR 6.4-8.9% vs SPY 15.45%; MaxDD -17 to -22% vs -33.7%; RULES v1 4.86%/0.451/-25.3%). Raising the ceiling buys OOS CAGR and pays OOS drawdown at a near-constant rate while moving Sharpe by ~0.002 — the gross dial again, as ideas 66 and 146 found. **Not reproduced and reported as such:** idea 144's 'the m<=1.30 ceiling binds in 54 of 306 books' matches none of three readings tried (admissible set reaches the ceiling **9**; FAMILY verdict differs between c=1.00 and c=1.30 **14**; family-Sharpe argmax at m=1.30 **106**). No RULES change, no new book, no KEEP candidate; one proposed 4b reporting clause in `research/backtests/2026-09-05_no-leverage-ceiling-is-load-bearing_cloud.memo.md`. Survivorship, idea 128's shallow IS window, idea 38's calendar-day index and the two non-scale-free `ebud` arms are carried in the script header and the memo.

Inside the 67 books the binary screen already admits, which is the only place a width can add anything, Spearman(width, OOS Sharpe) = **+0.126** and AUC(width -> OOS pass at m=1.00) = **+0.467, below a coin flip**; the one real signal, Spearman(width, OOS CAGR) = +0.579, is near-tautological because a wide interval is by construction CAGR headroom over the floor. **(2) Idea 84's mechanism is confirmed exactly and can be stated as fact.** Over the 72 books with a non-empty set at the published bars the lower shoulder is the CAGR floor in **72 of 72** and the upper is the DD cap in 62, rule 2's no-leverage ceiling in 9, and a Sharpe bar in **1** — that one being a `ddctl` arm where m is not a pure rescale; - 2026-09-05 (cloud, idea 146 pin-m-or-let-the-screen-choose) — **ANSWERED: the static gross multiplier m should be PINNED at the published gross in PROTOCOL rule 8. Rules unchanged, no new book, no KEEP candidate; one proposed rule-8 clause for the Sunday review.** Idea 94's simulator and idea 144's cell builder were imported rather than re-implemented, so this run adjudicates the same corpus the question was asked about, and four checks passed before any new number was read: `H.run` vs `engine.backtest` **max|diff| = 0.0**, idea 94's published EWall+vol60-dg u56@10bps row (11.587% / 1.133 / -16.884%), **0 of 54** differing S0/S1/S3 picks against idea 144's committed picks file, and idea 144's paired aggregates exactly (S1 0.127/1.022/-0.211, S3 0.138/1.019/-0.228). 306 books x a 25-point gross family = **7,650 runs**; two tuned parameters (the m-rule {point, pin, hi, lo} and the ceiling m_max in {1.00, 1.30}), all swept, all reported; 4b bars left at the published phi=0.70, delta=0.60. **(1) The FAMILY screen is not a screen.** In **7 of 7** entering cells it keeps the arm the pinned screen picks and moves only m, so its exchange rate is the gross ladder's own local slope BY IDENTITY (the matched-DD control on that arm is therefore trivially 0.000, stated rather than presented as a finding). Splitting the screen confirms it: PIN — family admissibility with m forced to 1.00 — equals the incumbent POINT screen in 6 of 7 arms and by 0.001 Sharpe in the seventh, so the admissibility change is a null and the m-rule is the whole story. **(2) On idea 74's axis the trade prices well and is still not worth taking:** realised median **0.668** pp CAGR per pp of |MaxDD| given up, beating the fitted OOS ladder slope 7/7 and the 200d gate's price (median 0.448) 7/7 — but beating the **IS** ladder slope **0 of 7**, i.e. the same move was priced at a median 1.082 pp/pp on the window the screen actually reads, 1.6x what it delivered — while buying **-0.003** OOS Sharpe with **0 of 7** cells improved. **(3) What it spends is exactly what 4b rations:** pin -> hi at m_max=1.30 turns **2 of the 3** pinned 4b passes into failures (broad/EWall@10, u56/EWall@10) for Sharpe changes between -0.015 and 0.000. **(4) Its m is an artifact of the window, not a property of the book:** the within-family IS Sharpe range is 0.002-0.004 over all 25 gross points for the scale-free arm kinds, so the selector's own statistic cannot order m and the IS drawdown cap decides it alone — and that cap is too loose, admitting more gross than the OOS window in **4 of 5** comparable cells (mean +0.100) with the two broad/TOP20 cells admitting 8-11 IS points where the OOS window admits zero (idea 128's shallow-IS-crash finding, now measured on the gross axis). **(5) The opposite m-rule is no better as a rule:** FAM-lo gains +0.007 Sharpe and 3.0pp of shallower drawdown but gives up 1.9pp of CAGR, so hi and lo are a coin flip on Sharpe and a large unmandated risk decision on everything else. Idea 144's family convention **survives for REPORTING** (a rescaled book is the same book) and is barred only from SELECTION. Caveats carried: only 7 of 18 cells enter any screen and the small panel enters none; survivorship on all three current-constituent panels flatters high gross, i.e. the side this run rules out; the two `ebud` arms are not pure exposure rescales (idea 144 Q1) and are flagged in the price table; ideas 38 and 126 carry over; Q3's matched-DD control is hindsight and labelled as such. Script `research/backtests/2026-09-05_pin-m-or-let-the-screen-choose_cloud.py`, note `...result.md`, proposed clause `...memo.md`.
- 2026-09-05 (lane B, idea 90 gross-interval-as-a-pre-registered-KEEP-bar) — **ANSWERED, KILL of the proposal as written: the interval is a real object, its WIDTH is not a robustness statistic, and the thing that actually carries the out-of-sample content is the CELL COUNT, which is already a pass/fail.** Idea 84 showed 4b's drawdown cap and CAGR floor both live on the gross axis and idea 144 proved CAGR and |MaxDD| are monotone along a book's own gross family, so idea 90 asked PROTOCOL to quote the width of each book's 4b-passing interval instead of a single pass/fail. Idea 94's simulator was imported and idea 144's family rebuilt and checked before any new number was read: **7,650 of 7,650 rows, index identical, max|diff| 5.0e-7 on IS_Sharpe** (idea 144's CSV is written to 6 decimals; the bar was 1e-6), **0 pass4a disagreements**, and the census comes back exactly — FAMILY-4b 72 at m<=1.30 / 58 at m<=1.00, POINT-4b 29 at m=1.00 (published 72/58/29). Corpus 3 panels x 3 books x 17 arms x 2 cost rungs = 306 books x 25 gross points = **7,650 backtests**, weekly, t+1, 10 and 25 bps, bars held at the published phi=0.70/delta=0.60; exactly two tuned parameters (w*, k) with **all 52 grid points printed**; the m_max ceiling run as an arm at 1.00 and 1.30. **(1) The object is sound.** The passing set is a single contiguous run in **72 of 72** non-empty books at m<=1.30 and 58 of 58 at m<=1.00, n_runs max 1, with **no exceptions to list**, and contiguity holds for the four arms idea 144 flagged as not scale-free (bud 9/9, dd 8/8). **(2) Census.** Non-empty by cell: u56@10 31/51, u56@25 23, broad@10 14, broad@25 4, and **small@10 0, small@25 0 — 0 of 102 small-panel books have an interval at any gross level on either cost rung**, which is direct support for queue idea 136. Widths median 0.15, IQR 0.10-0.21, max 0.40; mean width orders gate 0.047 > dd 0.038 > bud 0.031 > ctl 0.028 > stop 0.010. The queue's own operational KEEP (non-empty on both large-cap universes at both costs) admits **4 of 51 (book, arm) pairs**, all on EWall, and in all four the **intersection is non-empty**: `vol60-dg` **m in [0.95, 1.05]**, `vol60-rw` [0.90, 0.95], `band3-rw` [1.00, 1.05], `ddctl-8/.5/recover` m=1.15. Two of those are the project's standing cross-cell 4b passers (idea 127), recovered independently and now carrying a sizing RANGE where RULES has a point. **(3) The decisive test kills the width.** Against the binary pass/fail it refines: Spearman(IS width, OOS width) **+0.307 vs +0.296**, Spearman(IS width, OOS Sharpe) **+0.540 vs +0.546 — the binary wins** — while the incumbent IS-Sharpe carries **+0.902**; and within the 67 IS-passing books the grade collapses to +0.177 and **+0.055**, i.e. once a book has an interval, how wide it is says essentially nothing about what happens next. P(OOS non-empty | IS width >= w) is non-monotone (0.478 -> 0.656 -> **0.444** -> 0.000). On cross-cell portability the graded statistic is WORSE than the binary in 2 of 4 cells and tied in a third. The one place grading looks better (w*=0.15 lifts, 0.207->0.422 on broad@10) is a threshold read in-sample over 51 books and is contradicted by both rank correlations and (4). **(4) As a bar it is actively harmful, and the cell count is the real statistic.** Holding k=1, raising w* from 0.05 to 0.30 discards 20 of 31 books and LOWERS admitted-set mean OOS Sharpe **1.022 -> 0.989**; holding w* at its floor, raising k from 1 to 4 lifts it **1.022 -> 1.039 (k=3) -> 1.126 (k=4)** against 0.827 for the unbarred large-cap corpus — and plain POINT-4b in >=3 of 4 cells already reaches 1.126. **(5) Rule 8** (screens read 2009-2016 only, OOS read once, 18 cells): the widest-IS-interval selector is the **second non-inert screen this project has found — 2 of 7 picks move, where the incumbent IS-POINT-4b and idea 144's family screen both move 0** — and the first that does not lose Sharpe: paired on the 7 cells every selector enters, S3/S4 **13.1%/1.0397/-21.0%** vs S0=S1 12.7%/1.0219/-21.1% and S2 13.8%/1.0192/-22.8%, against SPY OOS 15.45%/0.882/-33.7% and RULES v1 4.77%/0.480/-19.7%. But the paired gain is literally one substitution (band3-rw for abs12-rw on u56/EWall@10 +0.171; for vol60-dg on broad/EWall@10 -0.046), so it is not evidence, and the memo recommends AGAINST letting the interval choose the arm or the gross. Every screen enters only **7 of 18** cells, declining all 6 small-panel and all 4 V1u cells — idea 132's "abstention is the screen" reproduced on a sixth bar. **No book is proposed, no rule changes, nothing is promoted.** The memo puts an amended PROTOCOL reporting clause to the Sunday review: quote the NUMBER OF CELLS in which the interval is non-empty and the INTERSECTION [m_lo, m_hi] as the range a candidate can be sized at, and explicitly do NOT quote a single cell's width as a robustness statistic. Caveats carried: survivorship on all three current-constituent panels (idea 54); the shallow IS drawdown window biases every selector identically (idea 128); ideas 38 and 126 carry over; 7 paired walk-forward cells; and every Q3/Q4 number is a large-cap number because the small panel contributes no intervals at all. Script: `research/backtests/2026-09-05_gross-interval-as-a-pre-registered-KEEP-bar_B.py`; result: `.result.md`; memo: `.memo.md`.
- 2026-09-05 (cloud, idea 91 band-width-at-g085) — **ANSWERED, KILL of the gross-shortfall hypothesis; the published 3% band SURVIVES the move to g = 0.85 and should be pinned rather than re-chosen. Rules unchanged, no new book.** Idea 84's script was imported rather than re-implemented, so every number sits on the harness that produced the constant being re-opened: the band-parameterised weight builder reproduces idea 84's three fixed books at **0 differing cells**, engine equivalence is `max|diff| = 0.0`, and idea 84's published rows come back exactly (u56 12.8% / 1.135 / -17.1%, halves 1.11/1.16; broad 12.6% / 1.062 / -18.9%, 1.16/0.97) before anything new was read. 5 bands x 5 gross levels x 2 universes x 2 books x 2 cost rungs = 200 points, all printed; two tuned parameters, both swept exhaustively. **(1) The two dials are orthogonal on Sharpe.** Holding the band fixed, full-sample Sharpe moves at most **0.0048** (median 0.0012) across the whole 0.55-1.00 gross ladder, OOS Sharpe at most 0.0047, and the Sharpe-optimal band is **identical at every g in 8 of 8 cells** (8% in six, 3% in one, 0% in one) — so the premise that the width was chosen at the wrong exposure has no room to be true. **(2) The substitution mechanism is refuted with its sign reversed:** a wider band very slightly LOWERS mean realised gross (0.8375 -> 0.8353 at g = 0.85 going 0% -> 8%) rather than raising it. What it buys is turnover, 9.3 -> 3.7 turns/yr on u56/ew-all@10bps. **(3) The band is nonetheless a genuine instrument:** against a matched-gross control (the unbanded book of the same cell interpolated along its own g ladder to the banded point's realised gross) it wins full-sample Sharpe in **116/144** matchable points and OOS Sharpe in **136/144**; at band 3, +0.052 Sharpe, +0.075 OOS Sharpe, +0.59pp CAGR, 0.59pp shallower DD, -1.97 turns/yr. **(4) 3% is not the Sharpe argmax but is the right pin.** 8% wins Sharpe in 6 of 8 cells and pays for it in drawdown; pooled 4b passes run 0%->5, 2%->9, **3%->11**, 5%->10, 8%->9 (at g=0.85: 2/4/4/4/2). Under rule 8 over all 40 cells, **PIN3 12.3% / 1.054 / -17.7% beats SEL-IS 12.3% / 1.025 / -18.9% and the unbanded control 11.5% / 0.983 / -18.3%**, 4b 11/40 vs 9/40 vs 5/40; the IS window picks 8% in 30 of 40 cells and it does not hold up out of sample, and the IS-4b-screened selector abstains in 29 of 40. Only 4b's LEVEL bars (CAGR floor, DD cap) couple the two dials at all. Caveats carried: survivorship on both current-constituent panels (which flatters the wide-band end this run argues against), idea 38's calendar-day index, idea 126's t+1-only execution, idea 128's shallow IS crash. Script `research/backtests/2026-09-05_band-width-at-g085_cloud.py`, note `...result.md`.
