- 2026-09-05 (lane C, idea 144 is-the-ladder-even-a-candidate) — **ANSWERED, KILL of the pre-registered hypothesis; the construction fix survives as a reporting convention, not as a bar deletion.** Idea 131 established that every candidate version of 4b's adequacy bar exists only to keep the static-gross ladder out of the corpus, and that no return- or exposure-LEVEL threshold can do that without discarding real defensive books. Idea 144 tested the construction fix instead of another metric: **a static rescaling of a book is the SAME book**, so the ladder is not a candidate but the interior of each book's own gross family. Idea 94's harness is imported and idea 131's corpus reproduced exactly on all four checks (306 rows / 82 Pareto / 29 pass 4b / 27 floor-only; ladder 97 of 342 floor-only, all at m <= 0.80; IS groups 45/9/252). Corpus = 3 panels x 3 books x 17 arms x 2 cost rungs = **306 books x 25 gross points m in [0.10, 1.30] = 7,650 backtests**, weekly, t+1, no leverage; exactly two tuned parameters (phi, delta) with all 42 grid points reported under both conventions and the m_max ceiling run as an arm. **(1) The hypothesis is dead:** with the ladder dropped, the CAGR floor still uniquely excludes **51 of 306** books (37 at the no-leverage ceiling) and the MaxDD cap **18** (22); the floor is load-bearing at **36 of 42** phi x delta points under BOTH conventions. 4b loses no bar. The one bar that excludes nothing anywhere, under either convention, is the **OOS Sharpe bar** (0 unique exclusions in every cut). **(2) What it does lose is a degree of freedom, provably:** along a family CAGR and |MaxDD| both rise with m, so the cap and the floor can only bind at m* = the largest gross clearing the cap, and the m* rule reproduces the exhaustive family verdict in **306 of 306 books at both ceilings**. Five bars, four degrees of freedom. The closed-form single ratio bar `CAGR/|MaxDD| >= (phi x CAGR_SPY)/(delta x |MaxDD_SPY|)` reproduces it **89.2% / 93.1%** — a good approximation, not a substitute (compounding is not linear in m; the ceiling binds in 54 of 306). The cap's bite falls **62%** at the published coefficients (47 -> 18) and to **1 of 306** at delta = 0.70. **(3) The convention is only true for scale-free instruments,** a limit measured rather than assumed on a split pre-registered from the instruments' units: PURE (ctl/gate/stop, n=234) CAGR monotone 228/234, |MaxDD| monotone 234/234, max Sharpe range along the family **0.0130**; SCALE-DEP (ddctl/ebud, n=72) monotone 27/72 and 35/72, max Sharpe range **0.2924** against a cross-book Sharpe sd of 0.319. An 8%-drawdown trigger at half gross is a different rule, because the book can no longer fall 8%. **(4) Mechanism, which is the transferable part:** of the 72 family admits at m <= 1.30, **29 pass only BELOW** published gross (they failed the DD cap; de-grossing fixes it), **14 only ABOVE** (they failed the CAGR floor — 14 of idea 129's 27 floor-only victims), and 29 include m = 1.00 unchanged. At m <= 1.00 the fix saves **0 of the 27 victims**: de-grossing cannot cure a CAGR floor. **(5) Price:** admissions 29 -> 58 -> 72, both KEEP paths 6 -> 72, and admitted-set OOS quality 1.114 / -18.5% / 13.2% -> 1.087 / -19.1% / 12.9% -> 1.098 / -19.6% / 13.2%; the ungated `control` book is admitted in 5 of 18 cells where POINT-4b admits it in 0, and that concession is the whole cost. **(6) Rule 8 walk-forward** (screens read 2009-2016 only, picks read once on 2017-2026, 7 selectors x 18 cells): the FAMILY screen is **the first screen in this project that is not inert — 7 of 18 picks moved, where POINT-4b with and without the floor moves 0 of 18** (reproducing ideas 131 and 132 on a fourth and fifth bar) — but it buys no Sharpe. Paired on the 7 cells every selector enters: S1 = S2 = 12.7% / **1.022** / -21.1%; S3 (family) 13.8% / 1.019 / -22.8%; S4 (floor deleted) 12.4% / 1.020 / -21.2%; S5 (cap deleted) 10.8% / **1.029** / -18.1%; S6 (Calmar bar) 13.8% / 1.019 / -22.8%. Reference OOS over the 18 cells: SPY 15.45% / 0.882 / -33.7%, RULES v1 4.86% / 0.451 / -25.3%; all family selectors beat SPY on Sharpe in 5 of 7 and RULES v1 in 7 of 7. Letting a screen choose gross is therefore **safe but not valuable**: +1.1pp of OOS CAGR for -1.7pp of extra drawdown and -0.003 Sharpe, with the cap-deleted arm the exact mirror image — a clean out-of-sample confirmation of idea 66 (gross is an exact, Sharpe-neutral lever) on ground idea 66 never touched. **No book is proposed, no rule changes, nothing is promoted.** The memo puts two PROTOCOL clauses to the Sunday review — the same-book convention *restricted to scale-free instruments*, and the cap-plus-floor stated as one test at m* — and recommends AGAINST letting rule 8 choose m. Caveats carried: survivorship (idea 54) flatters exactly the 29 cap-rescued admits; the IS window's shallow SPY drawdown (idea 128) biases all selectors identically; 7 paired walk-forward cells and 11 of 18 cells admitting nothing (all 6 small-panel cells among them); ideas 38 and 126 carry over; the no-leverage ceiling is itself load-bearing (binds 54 of 306, moves the floor's exclusions 51 -> 37) and is now queue idea 148. Script: `research/backtests/2026-09-05_is-the-ladder-even-a-candidate_C.py`; memo: `.memo.md`; result: `.result.md`.
# Strategy Changelog
- 2026-09-03 — Scan v1: composite = mean(pct-rank 12-1 mom, 6m, 3m) × trend factor (0.5 if below 200d) ÷ sqrt(vol20). Note: vol scaling favors low-vol ETFs (SPY/RSP rank high); revisit weighting in first Sunday review. No trades yet — rules v1 pending David's risk answers.
- 2026-09-04 — RULES v1 adopted for the paper book (top-5 risk-adjusted momentum, 200d filter, 15% sizing, Friday rebalance, daily 200d hard exit). First trades expected Fri Sep 4 close.
- 2026-09-03 — Local sprint (6 ideas): dual-momentum, inverse-vol, vol-target, broad-universe, full-exposure all KILL/PARK. macro-trend-ensemble variant B is the first KEEP-candidate (Sharpe/DD better in both halves and OOS; CAGR 5% so it is a defensive sleeve). Key diagnostic: v1 gross exposure is a pure lever (corr 1.00 with 100% version); the CAGR gap to SPY comes from the selection composite, which has no demonstrated cross-sectional edge. Direction for next ideas: (a) core index exposure with trend/drawdown control, (b) ensemble sleeve + momentum sleeve, (c) test whether the composite adds anything over equal-weight of eligible names.
- 2026-09-03 (late) — Local sprint total 9 ideas. Decisive: v1 score has zero cross-sectional IC because /sqrt(vol20) cancels momentum; equal-weighting all eligible names gives Sharpe 1.05 (both halves > SPY). PROTOCOL gained KEEP path 4b (judge vs SPY). Recommendation memo: research/backtests/2026-09-03_RECOMMENDATION.md. Rules unchanged pending Sunday review + idea 28.
- 2026-09-04 (lane A, idea 1 no-vol-scaling) — **KILL** as a v1 replacement: all 12 grid points (vol_scale ON/OFF x n in {3,5,8} x gross in {75%,100%}) fail KEEP path 4a on MaxDD and 4b on the drawdown cap. But the treatment is confirmed decisively — dropping `/sqrt(vol20)` is worth **+10.1%/yr (t 3.33)** at v1's own n=5/75%, and beats v1 on Sharpe at every (n, gross). Mechanism: the scaler cuts the average vol20 of held names from 29.4% to 14.1% and leaves only 31% name overlap; it is a different (low-vol) book, not a risk adjustment. Walk-forward (IS 2009-2016 -> OOS 2017-2026) picks OFF n=3/100%: OOS 31.7%/1.06/-33.1% vs v1 7.8%/0.75/-13.8% and SPY 15.5%/0.88/-33.7% — beats both on Sharpe and CAGR, fails 4b only on drawdown. Nearest miss PARK: OFF n=8/75%, 13.8%/0.93/-17.9%, halves 0.92/0.95, OOS 15.4%/0.98, clears 4b's MaxDD, CAGR and OOS tests and fails on H1 Sharpe alone (0.918 vs SPY 0.957). Edge survives 25 bps, dies at 50. New ideas 38-40 queued.
- 2026-09-04 (lane A) — **DATA BUG, acts on live signals.** `data/prices.csv` is indexed on CALENDAR days from 2014-09-17 (BTC-USD's first date): yfinance returns a 7-day index when crypto is in the ticker list and `cache_prices.py` ffills every equity across weekends. `load_universe()` drops the crypto columns but keeps the rows, so ~30% of post-2014 daily returns are exactly zero and years have 365 rows, not 252. (1) `engine.metrics()` annualizes at 252, so every sandbox/Actions backtest understates CAGR and Sharpe, and only in the second half of the sample — which corrupts the H1/H2 test PROTOCOL rule 4 rests on. v1 reads 4.65%/0.555/halves 0.659-0.452 on the raw cache vs 6.48%/0.666/0.641-0.692 corrected; the corrected figures reproduce the Sep-3 leaderboard row exactly, so the Sep-3 rows (run locally on live yfinance) are sound and only cloud-run rows are affected. (2) It flips verdicts: SPY reads 11.5%/0.78 raw vs 15.3%/0.89 corrected, so 4b's SPY tests are far too easy — OFF n=8/75% *passes* 4b on the raw cache and fails it corrected. (3) `research/scan.py` downloads the universe **including** BTC/ETH and ffills, so the live daily scan runs on the same index: `rolling(200)` spans ~143 trading days and `rolling(20)` ~14. Measured 2015+: the 200d-MA flag is wrong on 7.6% of ticker-days, at least one name is mis-flagged on 95.8% of days (mean 4.2 of 56), full eligibility differs on 8.1% of ticker-days, and vol20 reads a median 0.84x of true — loosening the vol<0.60 gate and mis-scaling the `1/sqrt(vol20)` term. The paper book began trading on these signals today. Fix is queued as idea 38 (INFRASTRUCTURE, do first); PROTOCOL forbids a research script from patching baseline.py/scan.py, so this goes to the Sunday review.
- 2026-09-04 (lane B, idea 40 vol-scaler-replacement) — **KILL** as a capital-worthy (4b) replacement for the per-name vol scaler: 0 of 21 grid points pass. Base book = lane A's no-scaler book (top-n by the v1 composite without `/sqrt(vol20)`, eligible only, 75% gross, weekly); treatment = a book-level control instead of a per-name one; 2 tuned params (n in {3,5,8}, threshold). (1) **The book-level drawdown rule of QUEUE idea 22 is a KILL mechanism, not a mixed result.** A 6-12% drawdown trigger with a new-high reset holds a 15-21%-vol book at half exposure on **52-79% of all days** — permanent de-leveraging, not tail control. It keeps 49-62% of the raw book's CAGR to buy back 23-39% of its drawdown and lowers Sharpe at every one of the 9 (n, D) points. Idea 22 is answered here and needs no separate run. Two points (n=8, D=6% and D=8%) do pass **4a** — 7.1%/0.722/-12.1% and 7.4%/0.700/-13.8% vs v1's 6.5%/0.666/-13.8% — and are precisely the pathology 4b was added to catch: they clear the bar only because the live book is weak, and fail all four 4b tests. Memo recommends the Sunday review record them as a documented 4a pass and **reject** them. (2) **The 200d-breadth gate is nearly free and is the project's nearest 4b miss.** Halving exposure when the fraction of the universe above its own 200d MA falls below B costs ~1pp of CAGR for ~4pp of drawdown, improves MaxDD in 8 of 9 points, and sits at half exposure on only 7-17% of days — against the scaler's cost of 10.1%/yr. It never improves Sharpe (9 of 9 slightly worse): it is a drawdown reducer, not a Sharpe improver. **n=3/B=30% passes 4b's H1 (0.961 vs SPY 0.957), H2 (1.089), OOS Sharpe (1.081 vs 0.884) and CAGR (21.0% vs a 10.7% floor) and fails on MaxDD alone by 0.4pp: -20.6% against a -20.2% cap.** Not tuned further (rule 7). (3) Walk-forward (rule 8, two selection rules fixed before reading any OOS number): the breadth arm's IS-Sharpe pick is that same n=3/B=30%, OOS 23.8%/1.081/-20.6% vs RULES v1 7.8%/0.751/-13.8% and SPY 15.5%/0.884/-33.7%. The 4b-aware selection rule picks **nothing** in the NONE and BREADTH arms — no in-sample point met the in-sample drawdown cap — which is the structural finding: this book cannot be made to satisfy 4b's drawdown test by a book-level exposure gate alone. Diagnosis for the next ideas: **H1 Sharpe against SPY's 0.957 is now the single binding 4b constraint in ideas 24, 25, 28 and 40** — four independent candidates failing the same test points at the 2009-2017 window, not at the strategies. Ideas 41-43 queued. Survivorship caveat (current-constituent universe.json) applies to the 21%/yr figures.
- 2026-09-04 (lane A, idea 2 position-count) — **KEEP-candidate under PROTOCOL path 4b — the project's first.** The question is not "3 vs 5 vs 8": RULES v1 holds the top 5 at a *fixed 15% each*, so its position count and its gross exposure are one dial (n=3 is a 45%-invested book, n=8 a levered 120% one), and ideas 1 and 40 therefore varied two things at once. This run separates them — arm FIXEDW keeps v1's w=15% (n<=6, since n=7 is leverage), arm EQW holds gross constant at 75% with w=0.75/n — and runs both on the v1 composite (ON) and without the `/sqrt(vol20)` term (OFF). Tuned parameter: n only; all 30 points reported. Harness sanity: `ON */n=5` reproduces the v1 baseline exactly and `OFF */n=5` reproduces idea 1's row. **5 of 30 points pass 4b, 2 pass 4a.** (1) The candidate: **top-20 eligible names, equal-weight at 75% gross, ranked without the vol scaler — 12.7%/1.093/-18.3%, halves 1.088/1.103** (SPY 0.957/0.837), and it is the rule-8 walk-forward pick of the 4b-aware selection rule, chosen on 2009-2016 alone, with untouched **OOS 14.4%/1.170/-18.3% vs SPY 15.5%/0.884/-33.7% and RULES v1 7.8%/0.751/-13.8%**. It is *not* the in-sample Sharpe maximum (0.993 vs n=4's 1.101) — it was selected on in-sample drawdown and improved out of sample, the opposite of the overfitting signature; the plain-Sharpe rule picks n=4 and fails on drawdown both in and out of sample. Memo with exact RULES wording: `2026-09-04_position-count.md`. (2) **Position count is what resolves the H1-Sharpe constraint that ideas 24, 25, 28 and 40 all failed on.** The `fail4b` column splits cleanly: n=2-5 fail on MaxDD (too concentrated), **n=6-10 fail on H1 Sharpe alone** (0.892-0.918 vs 0.957 — this is idea 1's nearest miss and idea 40's entire BREADTH arm), n>=12 pass everything. So the answer to queue idea 43 is at least partly "a concentration failure, not a 2009-2017 regime failure", and 4b is reachable on this universe. (3) **The other two 4b passes are a lever, not an edge.** `OFF/FIXEDW n=3` and `n=4` pass, but their Sharpes are identical to `OFF/EQW n=3`/`n=4` — they clear the drawdown cap only because 45%/60% gross scales a -25.8%/-23.8% drawdown under it. That is idea 20's "gross is a pure lever" result and the Sunday review should not read those rows as support for concentration. Only the n=20 pass is obtained at v1's own 75% gross. (4) **The whole ON (scaler) arm fails 4b's CAGR floor at every n**, best case 9.9% vs a 10.7% floor: the scaler caps the book's return however it is sized. (5) Honest limits on the KEEP, all three in the memo: the *direction* replicates on the 136-name broad universe (Sharpe 0.698 at n=2 -> 0.958 at n=20, MaxDD -40.6% -> -20.1%) but **the same n=20 rule fails 4b's H2 there by 0.02** (0.814 vs 0.837), and only n=40 passes — selecting it would be tuning; survivorship bites hardest exactly here, since a 20-name book holds over half of a 56-name current-constituent list (mean 37.5 eligible/day); and at matched 12% vol the gain over v1's n=5 is only ~1.9pp/yr on a U-shaped, non-monotone curve (n=4 gives 12.7% against n=20's 13.2%), so most of the 4b clearance is vol reduction fitting under the cap with a 1.9pp margin. Turnover falls monotonically with n and the candidate is the cheapest point in the grid at 9.6x/yr. Ideas 44-46 queued (broad-universe confirmation, cost/execution-lag sensitivity, fixed-n vs fixed-fraction).
- 2026-09-04 (lane B, idea 46 eligible-fraction-vs-n) — **KILL for the fixed-fraction rule as an improvement on fixed-n, plus one 4b KEEP-candidate that fixed-n does not produce.** The question is whether the book should pin the position COUNT (idea 2's top-20) or the FRACTION of eligible names, since the universe averages 37.5 eligible but swings from 3 to 55: on **11.0% of days fewer than 20 names are eligible**, and there the memo's n=20 rule holds *everything* and silently de-grosses to as little as 45% invested. Three arms, one tuned parameter each, 24 points all reported: **N** = top n at 0.75/n (idea 2's construction, cash when E_t<n), **NF** = same count cap renormalised to 75% gross (a decomposition arm so the N-vs-F comparison is not confounded), **F** = top ceil(f x E_t) at 0.75/ceil(f x E_t). Scorer fixed at the candidate's own (no `/sqrt(vol20)`); everything else v1's. Harness sanity: `N n=20` reproduces idea 2's KEEP row exactly and `N n=5` reproduces idea 1's `OFF EQW n=5` row. **(1) The idea's headline test fails.** At matched average book size the fraction rule beats the gross-matched count arm on Sharpe at 3 of 8 pairs (mean dSharpe **-0.002**) and on OOS Sharpe at 3 of 8 (mean -0.002). Adapting the count to breadth buys nothing on this universe and costs a parameter. **(2) The walk-forward agrees and is not close.** Rule 8 with two selection rules fixed in advance: arm N's picks (both rules) n=20, OOS 14.4%/1.170/-18.3%; arm F's 4b-aware rule picks f=0.85, OOS 12.4%/1.132/-16.7%; arm F's plain-Sharpe rule picks f=0.15 and **blows the drawdown cap out of sample** (-27.8%) — the fraction arm is the one that needs the guard rail. vs SPY 15.5%/0.884/-33.7% and RULES v1 7.8%/0.751/-13.8%. **(3) But f=0.85 is a 4b KEEP-candidate on portability, and it is the arm's own pre-registered pick, not a post-hoc point.** 11.3%/1.072/-16.7% with halves 1.092/1.058 on universe.json, and it passes 4b **again on the 136-name broad universe** (11.2%/1.024/-18.6%, halves 1.128/0.928) with the same parameter. Idea 2's n=20 fails 4b's H2 there by 0.02 and only n=40 passes — selecting it would be tuning. A count is a different rule on a differently-sized list; a fraction is the same rule. The trade is explicit: f=0.85 gives up 1.4pp of CAGR and 0.04 of OOS Sharpe to n=20 and its 4b CAGR margin is only ~0.6pp on both lists (vs n=20's 2.0pp on the primary). Memo with exact RULES wording: `2026-09-04_eligible-fraction-vs-n_B.memo.md`. **(4) Idea 28's 'equal-weight all eligible' (f=1.00), which missed 4b's CAGR floor by 0.23pp on universe.json, passes 4b outright on the broad list** (10.7%/1.026/-17.7%). **(5) A correction to the idea-2 memo's reading of its own wording:** the clause 'if fewer than 20 are eligible, hold all of them at 3.75% and leave the rest in cash' is not an arithmetic artefact — it is worth **+0.022 Sharpe at n=20 and +0.046 at n=30** against renormalising to 75%, one-directional at every n>=8. Keep it deliberately. **(6) The adaptive count is a real bear-market defence that full-sample Sharpe hides:** 2022 returns f=0.35 -0.8% and f=0.45 -1.5% vs n=20's -9.0% and SPY's -18.2% (E_t averaged 16 in 2022 against 37.5 overall); it pays for that in every other year, which is why it does not show up in the aggregate. 14 of 24 points pass 4b, **0 of 24 pass 4a** (every growth book fails v1's -13.8% drawdown). Survivorship caveat: both universes are current-constituent lists, so absolute CAGRs are optimistic — the N-vs-F comparison much less so, since both arms hold the same names. Ideas 47-49 queued (cost/lag on the fraction book, a breadth-conditional hybrid, and a third-universe replication on the small-cap panel).
- 2026-09-04 (lane B, idea 49 third-universe-portability) — **KILL for BOTH 4b KEEP-candidates on a third universe: 0 of 28 grid points pass either path.** The question was whether idea 2's `N n=20` (top 20 eligible, EW, 75% gross, no `/sqrt(vol20)`) and idea 46's `F f=0.85` (top 85% of eligible) are rules or artefacts of the list they were fitted on. Both were validated on `universe.json` (56 names) and `universe_broad.json` (136) — two overlapping current-constituent US large-cap lists in the same mega-cap regime, where broad is nearly a superset. Re-run unchanged, parameters fixed in advance, on the 439-name sub-$2B panel (483 less the 44 corrupted, 2011-01-13 -> 2026-09-03, trading-day index): **n=20 reads 6.7%/0.469/-27.4%, halves 0.613/0.352, OOS 7.4%/0.496** (vs 12.7%/1.093/-18.3% on universe.json) and **f=0.85 reads 4.3%/0.371/-40.9%, halves 0.466/0.302, OOS 3.6%/0.318** (vs 11.3%/1.072/-16.7%), against SPY 14.2%/0.863/-33.7% and RULES v1 6.3%/0.650/-13.8% on the same window. Every point fails all five 4b tests — not a near miss. **(1) The decisive control: the rule subtracts value from its own asset class.** Equal-weighting all 439 names with no filter and no ranking returns **10.2%/0.677/-36.2%** — higher CAGR *and* Sharpe than all 28 filtered books (consistent with idea 50's 100%-gross control, 14.3%/0.74). So this is not "small caps did badly": the panel's own beta earned 10.2% and the machinery turned it into 3.5-9.4%. **(2) The damage is the eligibility filter, and it is not costs.** `F f=1.00` *is* the control plus the 200d-MA + vol20<0.60 filter: 3.5%/0.329 vs 10.2%/0.677, i.e. the filter costs **6.6pp/yr of CAGR, of which only 1.2pp is turnover** (13.3x/yr vs 1.7x/yr at 10 bps) and **5.4pp is the filter's own timing at 0 bps** — whipsaw on noisy sub-$2B names. The momentum ranking on top is close to irrelevant (the whole f-sweep sits in a 3.5-6.2% band). This is the first evidence that the trend-eligibility filter underneath ideas 2, 25, 28 and 46 is regime- and universe-specific rather than general. **(3) The portability argument for f=0.85 does not survive either.** At matched book size the fraction arm beats the count arm on Sharpe at 6/8 pairs here (mean +0.017) against 3/8 (mean -0.002) on universe.json — the sign flips and both magnitudes are noise; fraction-vs-count was never the load-bearing part. **(4) N ≈ NF confirmed:** `E_t < 20` on 0.89% of days (E_t means 141 of 439), so the cash clause almost never fires and the arms differ by at most 0.014 Sharpe — direct support for idea 46's finding that the entire N-NF gap on universe.json was the cash sleeve, not the position count. **(5) Walk-forward (rule 8): the 4b-aware rule picks NOTHING in all three arms** — no in-sample point met the -11.2% in-sample DD cap (shallowest -19.6%); the plain-Sharpe rule picks `N n=15` (OOS 8.3%/0.524) and `F f=0.70` (OOS 4.0%/0.345), both below SPY's OOS 0.884 and below RULES v1's OOS 0.751. **(6) Costs:** turnover is 13-30x/yr here vs 9.6x for the candidate on universe.json; at 25 bps n=20 falls to 3.5%/0.288 and f=0.85 to 2.0%/0.215, and **at 50 bps both are negative**. A one-week execution lag changes nothing — there is no fast signal to decay. **Survivorship runs the wrong way for a KILL, which strengthens it:** all 483 panel names trade through 2026-09-03, so this list is *more* flattered than the large-cap ones and the books still fail every test. Benchmark caveat stated not adjusted: no IWM in the cache, so 4b compares a small-cap book to SPY; the control comparison in (1) does not depend on the benchmark and the rule loses that one too. **For the Sunday review: the 4b passes are a property of that universe, not of the rule.** Neither candidate should be adopted as a general rule; if n=20 is adopted at all it should carry an explicit large-cap universe clause with this failed replication on the record. Ideas 51-53 queued (filter-by-market-cap, slower small-cap trend, universe-composition robustness of the KEEP).
- 2026-09-04 (lane C, idea 39 small-cap-eligible-equal-weight) — **KILL, and an independent replication of lane B's idea 49: the two 4b KEEP-candidates fail their first out-of-universe test.** Written and run separately from idea 49; the two scripts agree to the decimal on the shared point (f=1.00 CAGR 3.5%), which is the strongest confirmation the project has for any result so far, and the diagnostics below are additive to it rather than a repeat. Question: ideas 2 (top-20 EQW @75% gross) and 46 (f=0.85) pass PROTOCOL 4b on universe.json and universe_broad.json, but those are two heavily-overlapping current-constituent lists of ETFs and large caps. Does the book they are built on — equal-weight everything above its 200d MA with vol20 < 0.60 — survive on the 439-name sub-$2B small-cap panel (483 less the 44 with `max_1d_move >= 1.0`)? Two tuned parameters (selection breadth in {F f=0.45/0.85/1.00, N n=20/40}, gross in {75%,100%}), all 10 points reported, weekly, 10 bps, t+1, sample 2011-01-13..2026-09-03. **(1) 0 of 10 points pass 4a or 4b and none is close.** Idea 39's own arm, `F f=1.00 @75%`, reads **3.5%/0.329/-40.2%, halves 0.433/0.250, OOS 0.280** against SPY 14.2%/0.863/-33.7% (halves 0.886/0.864) and RULES v1 on the same panel 8.1%/0.602/-32.8%. Every point fails all five 4b tests (H1, H2, OOS, MaxDD, CAGR). The two live candidates' own rules are among the *worst* points here: f=0.85 0.371/0.372, n=20 0.469/0.470. **(2) Rule-8 walk-forward is not close either** (params on 2011-2016 only, two selection rules fixed in advance): the plain-Sharpe rule picks f=0.85 g=1.00, OOS 4.4%/0.318/-51.2% vs SPY 15.5%/0.884/-33.7%; the 4b-aware rule picks **nothing** — no in-sample point met the in-sample drawdown or CAGR bar. **(3) The mechanism is that the eligibility gate is INVERTED on small caps, not merely weak.** Equal-weighting all 439 names with no filter gives **8.1%/0.640/-30.7% at 1.5x turnover** — better than every filtered arm on Sharpe, CAGR *and* drawdown. Decomposed at matched 75% gross: 200d gate only 6.4%/0.500/-38.2%, vol20 gate only 5.4%/0.441/-35.0%, both 3.6%/0.332/-40.0%; each gate subtracts and they compound, and a trend filter making drawdown 9pp *worse* is the tell. Costs are only part: the same book at 0 bps still reads 5.0%/0.432, so ~1.4pp of the 4.5pp CAGR gap is the 13.4x turnover and ~3.1pp is selection. The complement book (hold every name the filter rejects) returns 11.9%/0.731; **eligible minus complement is -8.41%/yr, t -3.77**. **(4) It is a cross-sectional failure, not an exposure one** — the f=1.00 book is 74.4% invested on average and spends exactly one day in cash (2020-03-19, when the eligible set hit 0 of 439). It bleeds in every V-shaped year, holding the names that never fell and missing the rebound cohort: 2020 +3.2% vs unfiltered +28.3% and SPY +18.3%, 2019 +2.8% vs +12.8%, 2023 +5.4% vs +12.8%, 2024 +2.4% vs +9.1%. **(5) Survivorship discipline on the complement result:** the panel is current constituents of a sub-$2B screen, so names that crashed and then delisted are absent, and that bias falls hardest on exactly the beaten-down high-vol names the complement holds. "Buy the rejects" is NOT a tradeable finding and is recorded as evidence on the gate's *sign* only. The KILL itself does not depend on it — the unfiltered control and the filtered arms hold overlapping survivor sets and the filtered arms still lose. **Consequence for the Sunday review:** two large-cap current-constituent lists that overlap heavily are one replication, not two. Ideas 2 and 46 should be promoted (if at all) as **large-cap rules with a stated universe condition**, not as general ones, and not sized on the assumption that the mechanism is universal. Memo: `2026-09-04_smallcap-eligible-equal-weight_C.memo.md`. Idea 51 as filed by lane B already covers the gate-sign-by-universe follow-up; ideas 54-55 queued (a delisting-aware small panel, and trend-gate lookback on the primary universe).
- 2026-09-04 (cloud, idea 38 small-cap-momentum-clean) — **KILL, 0 of 8 grid points pass 4a or 4b**, but the run **partially overturns ideas 39/49's reading of their own mechanism.** Question: those two runs killed the v1 *composite* book on the 439-name sub-$2B panel and concluded the momentum ranking was "close to irrelevant" there (every filtered arm below a 10.2%/0.677 unfiltered control). This run strips the scorer to **plain 12-1 momentum** — no 6m/3m blend, no `/sqrt(vol20)`, no vol20 gate — top-n equal-weight at 0.75/n, weekly, t+1, 10 bps, 2011-01-13..2026-09-03; two tuned parameters (n in {10,20,40,60}, 200d gate ON/OFF), all 8 points reported. Harness sanity: RULES v1 on the small panel and the EW-all control reproduce ideas 39/49 to the decimal (8.1%/0.602/-32.8% and 10.2%/0.677/-36.2%). **(1) The verdict is unambiguous.** Best point `no-gate/n=40` reads 14.6%/0.797/-33.9%, halves 0.803/0.816, OOS 0.818, against SPY 14.2%/0.863/-33.7% (halves 0.886/0.864): below SPY's Sharpe in both halves and out of sample, and its drawdown is 13.7pp outside 4b's -20.2% cap. Every one of the 8 points fails H1, H2, OOS and DD; n=60 with the gate also fails CAGR. No point passes 4a either (v1's -13.8% drawdown). **(2) But pure 12-1 momentum does rank on this panel, where the composite did not.** `no-gate/n=40` beats the panel's own unfiltered beta by **+4.4pp CAGR and +0.12 Sharpe, net of 11.5x/yr turnover at 10 bps**. Ideas 39/49's "the ranking is worth nothing" was a property of the v1 composite (rank-blend plus vol20 gate plus vol scaler), not of momentum. **(3) The gate damage localises to the vol20 filter, not the 200d MA.** Holding the ranking and n=40 fixed: none 14.6%/0.797, 200d only 11.4%/0.693, **vol20 only 7.4%/0.524**, both 5.6%/0.441 — the vol20<0.60 gate alone costs 0.27 of Sharpe against the 200d gate's 0.10, and they compound. Ideas 39/49 only ever measured the two together. **(4) The 200d gate's sign is not stable**: +0.053 Sharpe at n=10, +0.015 at n=20, -0.105 at n=40, -0.117 at n=60 — it helps a concentrated book and hurts a broad one, so neither idea 39's "inverted" nor RULES v1's "the gate is the edge" generalises here. **(5) The decisive counter-evidence against trading any of it, stated against our own finding (2):** compounded EW decile portfolios by 12-1 momentum (daily, 0 bps) are U-shaped, not monotone — D1 21.5%/0.865, D2-D9 flat in an 8.0-12.4% band, and **D10 (worst momentum) 29.2%/0.937, higher CAGR and Sharpe than D1**; the arithmetic D1-D10 spread is -8.11%/yr, t -1.15. D10 is precisely the beaten-down cohort the survivorship bias flatters most, so it is not tradeable — but with both tails beating the middle and only one of them survivorship-clean, finding (2) cannot be read as a demonstrated cross-sectional premium. **(6) Cost-fragile.** At 0 bps the best point reads 15.9%/0.856 — it merely ties SPY frictionless; 10 bps 0.797, 25 bps 0.709, 50 bps 0.561. An 11.5x-turnover sub-$2B book would realistically pay more than 10 bps. **(7) Rule-8 walk-forward** (IS 2011-01-13..2017-01-03, two selection rules fixed in advance): plain-Sharpe picks `no-gate/n=40`, OOS 16.3%/0.818/-33.9% vs SPY 15.5%/0.884/-33.7% and RULES v1 live 7.8%/0.751/-13.8% — beats SPY on CAGR, loses on Sharpe, beats the live book on both at 2.5x its drawdown; the OOS Sharpe *exceeded* the IS Sharpe (0.818 vs 0.785), so this is not an overfitting signature, the book is simply not better than SPY risk-adjusted. The **4b-aware rule picks NOTHING** — no in-sample point met the -11.2% in-sample drawdown cap (shallowest -20.1%) — the same structural outcome as ideas 39, 46 and 49. Survivorship (current constituents of a sub-$2B screen as of 2026-09-03) is one-directional and strengthens the KILL; benchmark caveat stated not adjusted (no IWM in the cache). **For the Sunday review:** nothing to adopt, and one live-book item — the `vol20 < 0.60` gate is the larger of RULES v1's two eligibility filters by damage here and has never been tested in isolation on universe.json, where it is assumed to be part of the edge. Queued as idea 56. Result memo: `research/backtests/2026-09-04_small-cap-momentum-clean_cloud.result.md`.
- 2026-09-04 (cloud, idea 55 trend-gate-lookback-primary) — **KILL for the 200d trend gate as a source of edge on the universe it was fitted to, plus a 4b KEEP-candidate that strictly dominates idea 2's.** The 200d-MA gate is the load-bearing assumption of RULES v1 and of the project's first KEEP-candidate; ideas 39 and 27 measured it at -1.7pp CAGR on small caps and -6.4pp on QQQ, but nobody had varied the LOOKBACK on `universe.json`, where it is supposed to be the edge. Idea 2's construction held fixed (top-n by the v1 composite without `/sqrt(vol20)`, EW at 0.75/n, 75% gross, weekly, t+1, 10 bps, cash when E_t<n), two tuned parameters — K in {200d,100d,50d,none} x n in {5,10,20,30} — all 16 points reported, `vol20<0.60` held at v1's value throughout. Harness check: **K=200d n=20 reproduces idea 2's KEEP row to the decimal** (12.7%/1.093/-18.3%, halves 1.088/1.103). **(1) K=none beats K=200d at every n on universe.json**, on CAGR and Sharpe both: n=20 reads 13.7%/1.123/-18.5% (halves 1.154/1.101) against the incumbent's 12.7%/1.093/-18.3% at the same 9.5x/yr turnover, and n=5/10/30 all show the same sign. 6 of 16 points pass 4b, 1 passes 4a. **(2) The gate's own contribution is statistically indistinguishable from zero.** Paired daily t-tests on the same names and days: dropping 200d is worth +0.35 to +1.47%/yr on universe.json (**t +1.42 to +1.76** — positive at every n, significant at none) and -0.22 to +0.19%/yr on universe_broad.json (t -0.50 to +0.68). So "the gate is the edge" is not supported on the primary universe; equally, "the gate destroys value here" is NOT established — the honest statement is that it does nothing measurable, and its sign is unstable between two heavily-overlapping large-cap lists, which is itself evidence it is noise rather than structure. **(3) Faster IS decisively worse, and that is the only significant effect in the lookback dimension.** The 50d gate costs 3.0-5.5%/yr on BOTH universes at every n (t +2.59 to +3.85), monotone in K. Whipsaw is real; it has simply stopped mattering by 200d. If a gate is kept at all, keep a 200d-class lookback. **(4) The cleanest number, consistent in sign on both universes: for the un-ranked book (equal-weight every eligible name, no ranking) the 200d gate costs ~1.8-2.2pp of CAGR and ~0.09 of Sharpe (t +1.47 primary, t +2.13 broad) and adds 6.4x/yr of turnover, to buy 2.5-3.1pp of drawdown.** That is drawdown insurance, not alpha, and the Sunday review should describe it that way in RULES. Pricing that trade properly is queued as idea 57. Calendar-year check at n=20 agrees: the gate helped only in 2020 (+1.6pp) and was a dead heat in 2022 (-9.0% vs -8.9% with SPY -18.2%). **(5) Rule-8 walk-forward: on universe.json BOTH pre-registered selection rules pick K=none n=20** (IS Sharpe 1.071, IS MaxDD -11.9%), OOS **14.9%/1.164/-18.5%** vs SPY 15.5%/0.884/-33.7% and RULES v1 7.8%/0.751/-13.8%, clearing every OOS 4b bar; OOS Sharpe exceeds IS Sharpe, so this is not an overfitting signature. On universe_broad.json both rules pick **K=200d n=30** instead (OOS 11.6%/0.904/-20.3%, misses the OOS 4b bars) and **0 of 16 broad points pass 4b**. The two universes pick opposite arms — the candidate inherits idea 2's universe fragility exactly and must not be promoted as a general rule. **(6) One diagnostic worth recording and not trading:** the K=none un-ranked control passes 4b outright on universe.json (12.2%/1.127/-18.4%, halves 1.141/1.116, OOS 1.183) at **1.9x/yr turnover** and misses on the broad list by 0.6pp of drawdown alone. It is also ~52 of 56 current constituents equal-weighted — close to an equal-weight index of a list chosen in 2026 — so survivorship is the leading explanation for its 1.13 Sharpe against SPY's 0.89. Recorded as a diagnostic, not a candidate. **For the Sunday review:** (a) stop describing the 200d filter as the source of the live book's edge; (b) if idea 2's candidate is adopted, adopt it WITHOUT the 200d gate — same construction, one fewer filter, +1.0pp CAGR, +0.030 Sharpe, -0.2pp drawdown, identical turnover, and it is the walk-forward pick under both rules (exact wording in `research/backtests/2026-09-04_trend-gate-lookback-primary_cloud.memo.md`); (c) either version still needs the explicit large-cap-universe clause ideas 39/49 called for. Ideas 56-57 queued. Result memo: `research/backtests/2026-09-04_trend-gate-lookback-primary_cloud.result.md`.

- 2026-09-04 (lane C, idea 8 lookback-blend) — **KILL for changing the ranking lookback.** 20 grid points (12-1 / 6-1 / 3-1 / blend-v1 / blend-skip x n in {5,10,20,30}) on universe.json and universe_broad.json, gate/gross/cadence/costs held at idea 2's KEEP construction; harness reproduces that row to the decimal. Nothing beats the incumbent blend at n=20/30 on either list (|t| < 2 in both directions), and **IS->OOS Sharpe rank correlation across the 20 points is +0.000 on universe.json**, so the horizon is not selectable in-sample on the list the live book trades. Two results to carry forward: the **3-1 leg is the weak one** (weekly rank IC t 2.07 primary / 1.40 broad vs 12-1's 4.29 and the blend's 4.74; -2.11%/yr t -3.17 at n=20) and **12-1 n=30 is the only point passing 4b on both universes** (10.9%/1.097/-15.8% and 13.0%/1.004/-20.2%) — PARK, not KEEP, because rule 8 never selects it and its broad drawdown sits exactly on the cap. Ranking beats an unranked equal-weight of the eligible set only in concentrated books (n=5 t +2.4..+3.3; n=30 t +0.1..+0.7). Rules unchanged. Memo: research/backtests/2026-09-04_lookback-blend_C.memo.md. Idea 68 (cost/lag on the 12-1 n=30 arm) queued.

- 2026-09-04 (lane B, idea 66 gross-exposure-is-the-error) — **KILL for "raise gross" as the fix for the standing 4b failure, and a correction to idea 63's diagnosis.** Idea 63 attributed the books' second-half shortfall to 75%-gross cash drag and repaired it with a 25% passive core; the queue asked whether simply being fully invested does the same thing, in which case the sleeve is a detour. 36 arms (3 pre-chosen books x gross in {0.75,0.80,0.85,0.90,0.95,1.00} x core fraction in {0,0.25}) x 6 cost levels x 2 universes, weekly, t+1, long-only, no leverage; two tuned parameters (gross, core fraction), all points reported; analytic cost identity asserted against a real `cost_bps=10` engine run. **(1) Gross is an exact lever, now verified rather than assumed:** against the same book at g=1.00 every arm shows **corr 1.0000, beta = g to three decimals, and dSharpe = 0.000**; the Sharpe slope d(Sharpe)/d(gross) is -0.001 to +0.008 across all three books and both lists. CAGR, MaxDD and turnover all scale linearly (universe.json `ew-band3`: 11.3%/-15.1%/4.9x at g=0.75 -> 15.1%/-19.9%/6.4x at g=1.00, Sharpe 1.136 at every point). **(2) Therefore gross cannot fix an H2 Sharpe bar, and the cash-drag story does not explain the failure it was invoked for.** The bar idea 63 targeted is the broad ranked book's H2 of 0.814 against SPY's 0.837; across the whole gross sweep it reads 0.814/0.814/0.814/0.815/0.815/0.815 while MaxDD deepens 6.1pp. Cash drag is a real CAGR cost and undoing it does raise CAGR — but at exactly the drawdown you would predict, so no risk-adjusted bar moves. **(3) The sleeve is NOT a detour — it is the only one of the two that touches a Sharpe bar,** because it changes the composition of the return stream rather than its scale: the same broad arm with the 25% SPY core reads H2 0.861-0.862 at *every* gross (+0.047). But the effect is not significant (paired daily t < 1 in every book) and it still fails 4b on drawdown there; and at matched total investment (g=1.00) the core is strictly **worse** on universe.json — 1.106/-21.7% against all-active's 1.136/-19.9%, paired +0.23%/yr t +0.44. **(4) Rule-8 walk-forward: all four picks FAIL out-of-sample on drawdown,** by 0.4-1.9pp (universe.json R1 `g=1.00 b=0.25` OOS 16.7%/1.174/-21.7%, R2 `g=0.95 b=0.25` OOS 15.8%/1.174/-20.6%; broad R1 `g=1.00 b=0` OOS 14.9%/1.073/-22.1%, R2 `g=0.95 b=0` OOS 14.2%/1.073/-21.1%; OOS cap -20.2%). The mechanism is worth carrying forward: **gross is not a selectable parameter.** No Sharpe-based rule can choose it (Sharpe is flat, so R1's pick is decided in the 4th decimal), and the drawdown-aware rule still overshoots because 2009-2016 contains no 2020 or 2022, making its -13.2% in-sample cap loose against the -20.2% the OOS period enforces. Gross is a risk-budget decision, and in-sample drawdown is not a safe way to set it. **(5) One PARK:** `ew-band3, g=0.90, core=0` clears all five 4b tests on **both** universes at **5, 10, 15, 20 and 25 bps** and clears the OOS 4b bars on both (13.5%/1.136/-18.0%, halves 1.113/1.160, OOS 1.234, 5.8x; broad 13.4%/1.064/-20.0%, halves 1.164/0.971, OOS 1.073, 6.2x) — a wider cost survival than idea 63's QQQ-core arm with one fewer moving part. PARK not KEEP: rule 8 does not and cannot select it, and g=0.90 is merely the largest gross whose linearly-scaled drawdown still fits under the broad list's cap, not evidence of an edge — its Sharpe is identical to g=0.75's. **For the Sunday review:** (a) stop treating gross as something a backtest can choose — state it in RULES as a drawdown budget with the number derived from the budget; (b) idea 63's core sleeve survives this test on its own terms but its H2 repair is t < 1 and costs drawdown, so it should not be adopted on the cash-drag argument as stated. Rules unchanged. Ideas 69-70 queued. Result memo: `research/backtests/2026-09-04_gross-exposure-is-the-error_B.result.md`.
- 2026-09-04 (lane C, idea 10 sector-only-universe) — **KILL for the ETF-only book, and the complement control is the finding.** 30 points (6 panels x [RULES v1 + EW-all-eligible + top-n EQW n in {5,10,20}]), all reported; harness reproduces idea 2's published KEEP row (12.7%/1.093/-18.3%, halves 1.088/1.103) and the live v1 row to the decimal. **(1) 0 of 10 ETF-only points pass 4a or 4b.** Best ETF arm `ETF36/CAND-n20` 6.8%/0.817/-15.2% (halves 0.754/0.878, OOS 8.1%/0.943) fails 4b on **H1 and CAGR** — the floor is 10.68% and it earns 6.8%; the literal queued arm `ETF24` (broad + sector ETFs, no bonds/commodities) 6.7%/0.779/-15.2% fails H1, H2 and CAGR. **(2) Half the hypothesis is right and it does not help.** Matched construction, same days, same gross, vs the full-list control: ETF36 has **lower vol in 10 of 10 pairs** and ETF24 in 6 of 10 — the idiosyncratic-risk claim is real on volatility — but MaxDD is shallower in only 4/10 and 2/10, and **Sharpe is worse in 20 of 20**, because the return loss dwarfs the risk saved: **-6.15%/yr (t to -5.31)** for ETF36 and **-5.02%/yr (t to -4.11)** for ETF24. Risk came down; return came down more. **(3) The complement carries the entire edge.** The same 56-name list stripped to its 20 mega-cap single stocks gives the run's only 4a pass — `STK20/CAND-n20` **12.1%/1.338/-12.1%, halves 1.341/1.344**, MaxDD shallower than v1's -13.8% — which also passes 4b; `STK20/CAND-n10` reads 19.0%/1.305/-17.3%; running v1's *own* construction on stocks only is worth **+11.6pp CAGR and +0.468 Sharpe (t +4.07)**. **Both rule-8 selections pick a STK20 arm and both clear every OOS 4b bar** (S1 -> n=10, OOS 21.2%/1.366/-17.3%; S2 4b-aware -> n=20, OOS 14.0%/1.449/-12.1%), with **Spearman(IS, OOS Sharpe) = +0.608** over the 18 ranked points — the project's best IS/OOS rank agreement (idea 8 measured +0.000 on this list). **(4) It is a PARK, not a KEEP, and the run's own control is why.** universe.json's megacap group is 20 tickers selected as 2026 constituents (NVDA, AVGO, PLTR, META, TSLA...) — the most survivorship-exposed panel in the repo. The stock-leg advantage is +5.19%/yr (t to +4.45) on those 20 names but only **+0.92%/yr (t max +2.04)** on universe_broad.json's 100 single stocks, which **fail 4b on drawdown at every n**. A 5.6x shrink when the stock leg widens from 20 curated names to 100 is selection, not a premium. Memo written recommending **rejection**, with the exact RULES wording it would require so the Sunday review rejects a written rule rather than a vibe; note that at n=20 on a 20-name panel the ranking is inert (mean 13.2 eligible, 49% gross) so the rule is EW-all-eligible with a gate. **(5) Universe invariance is not a substitute for return.** The 36 ETFs are bit-identical in universe.json and universe_broad.json (symmetric difference `[]`), so an ETF-only book passes idea 53's cross-universe composition test **by construction** — and still fails 4b outright. **(6) Two side results for the Sunday comparison:** `B136/EWall` — equal-weight every eligible name in the broad list, no ranking, no vol scaler, 8.3x turnover — **passes 4b** at 10.7%/1.027/-17.7% (halves 1.146/0.917, OOS 1.021), the simplest 4b-passing book the project has; and ranking helps *more* on the low-dispersion ETF panel (+0.186 Sharpe over EWall) than on the full list (+0.043), which does not rescue it. Survivorship: current constituents of both lists, one-directional — it runs **against** the ETF KILL (strengthening it) and **hard in favour** of STK20 (which is why that arm is parked). Ideas 71-73 queued (point-in-time mega-cap panel, `B136/EWall` head-to-head against the standing candidates, and cross-sectional dispersion as the mechanism).

- 2026-09-04 (lane B, idea 73 asset-class-dispersion) — **KILL for cross-sectional dispersion as a universe clause, and idea 10's ETF KILL is re-explained.** 35 points (7 panels x {EWall, RULES v1, CAND-5/10/20}) on a common 2011-01-13→2026-09-03 window so every panel is judged on identical days, 10 bps, weekly, t+1, 75% gross; two tuned parameters (panel, n), all points reported; the harness reproduces idea 2's KEEP row (12.7%/1.093/-18.3%, halves 1.088/1.103) and the live v1 row (6.5%/0.666/-13.8%) to the decimal before any new number is read. **(1) The premise is true as a fact about the panels.** Mean cross-sectional sd of 12-1 momentum over the eligible set orders exactly as idea 10 assumed: ETF24 0.129 < ETF36 0.145 < B136 0.257 < BSTK100 0.278 < U56 0.281 < STK20 0.378 < SMALL484 0.682, stable across halves — the ETF panels really do have 2.6x less to sort than the mega-cap stock panel and 4.7x less than small caps. **(2) The inference drawn from it is false.** The net ranking premium (CAND-n Sharpe minus the SAME panel's equal-weight-all-eligible book, same days, same gate, same gross) has Spearman **-0.107 / -0.071 / -0.071** with dispersion at n=5/10/20. The largest premium in the entire run is **ETF36/CAND20 at +0.228 Sharpe (t +2.41)** — on the second-*lowest*-dispersion panel — while the widest panel, SMALL484, has the worst net Sharpe of all 21 CAND points (0.493). Ranking works best where there is least to sort. **(3) What the cross-panel pattern is actually made of.** The gross selection spread (forward 1-week return of the top-n eligible minus the equal-weighted eligible set, no costs) does rise with dispersion (Spearman +0.429/+0.679/+0.357) but rises *more* with the number of eligible candidates (**+0.571/+0.857/+0.964**), and the two are confounded across panels because the widest panel is also the largest. n=7 panels; treated as suggestive, not evidence. **(4) The test with power is flat.** Within each panel, weeks classified into dispersion terciles by an EXPANDING quantile of that panel's own history (no look-ahead): high-dispersion weeks beat low-dispersion weeks in only **2 / 2 / 3 of 7 panels**. Pooled with panel fixed effects over **4,877 panel-weeks**, corr(dispersion percentile, weekly selection spread) = **-0.006 / -0.015 / +0.001 (|t| ≤ 1.01)**. Holding the panel, its names and its survivorship exposure fixed, dispersion predicts nothing. **(5) Rule 8 rejects it outright.** The pre-registered dispersion rule S3 (highest 2009-16 mean dispersion at n=20) picks **SMALL484 → OOS 7.6%/0.506/-26.3%, failing every OOS 4b bar** and finishing last of the seven panels; S1 (highest IS Sharpe) picks STK20/CAND10 → **21.2%/1.366/-17.3% PASS** and S2 (4b-aware) picks STK20/CAND20 → **14.0%/1.449/-12.1% PASS**. Spearman(IS dispersion, OOS Sharpe) = **+0.134** over the 21 points (+0.143 over the 7 panels at n=20) against Spearman(IS Sharpe, OOS Sharpe) = **+0.710**. The panel choice *is* selectable out of sample; dispersion is simply not what selects it — and used as a rule it picks the worst panel available. **(6) Idea 10's ETF KILL survives with a different mechanism.** The ETF book fails on the LEVEL of return, not on the ranking: ETF36/CAND20 earns 6.6% CAGR against this window's 9.91% 4b floor while carrying the best ranking premium in the run. 'Too few things to sort' is not why ETFs failed; 'not enough expected return in the panel' is. **(7) Nothing is upgraded.** The only 4b passes are already-known books — U56/EWall, U56/CAND10, U56/CAND20 (idea 2's candidate) and all four STK20 books (idea 10's PARK, which idea 71 attributes to selection). No new KEEP-candidate. **Survivorship** is one-directional on all three lists and falls hardest on STK20/BSTK100/SMALL484, the high-dispersion panels, so the run is biased *toward* the hypothesis it rejects. **For the Sunday review: recommended RULES wording is none** — do not add a dispersion clause. If a universe clause is wanted, the supported form is about return level and candidate count, not spread: *'The ranked book may be run on any panel of at least 20 eligible names; a panel is disqualified by its own realised CAGR against the 4b floor, not by its cross-sectional dispersion.'* Rules unchanged. Ideas 77-78 queued. Memo: `research/backtests/2026-09-04_asset-class-dispersion_B.result.md`.

- 2026-09-04 (cloud, lane C) — **Idea 13 `52w-high-proximity`: KILL.** 28 points (2 universes × [live-v1 + EW-all-eligible control + 3 signals × n∈{5,10,20,30}]), all reported; harness reproduces idea 2's KEEP row (12.7%/1.093/-18.3%, halves 1.088/1.103) and idea 10's `B136/EWall` (10.7%/1.027/-17.7%) to the decimal. **(1) The literal idea loses everywhere.** Replacing RULES v1's return composite with George & Hwang's nearness-to-52-week-high `P_t / max(P_{t-251..t})`, holding names/days/gate/gross fixed, is worse in **8 of 8 matched-n pairs**: dSharpe **-0.045..-0.754**, paired daily **t -3.07..-4.16**, every sign negative, mean **-7.2%/yr** on universe.json and **-9.9%/yr** on broad; the pre-registered 50/50 BLEND arm loses in 7 of 8. **0 of 16 PROX/BLEND points pass 4b anywhere; 0 of 28 points pass 4b on both lists.** **(2) The signal is negatively informative inside the eligibility gate.** Weekly rank IC vs the following week's return over 905/914 weeks: PROX **-0.0208 (t -2.13)** on universe.json and **-0.0252 (t -3.34)** on broad, negative in BOTH halves of BOTH lists, against the incumbent composite's +0.0427 (t +4.16) and +0.0242 (t +3.22). The mechanism is that the 200d gate has already spent the signal — mean PROX of *eligible* names is 0.953 vs 0.902 across all names — so what is left is a level statistic only +0.192/+0.324 Spearman-correlated with the composite, and the part that differs is the part that loses. It is **de-risking, not signal**: vol falls in 8/8 pairs and MaxDD is shallower in 6/8 while CAGR falls further every time — idea 10's ETF shape exactly (risk fell, return fell more). **(3) It loses to no ranking at all**: against EW-all-eligible, dSharpe -0.900..+0.004 with t -4.10..-1.98 at every n on both lists, while the incumbent composite beats that same control on return in 8/8. **(4) The project's own whipsaw prior pointed at this idea and the measurement points the other way — the run's most transferable finding.** A *level* key bounded near 1.0 is FASTER, not slower: at matched n on universe.json turnover is 40.2/31.1/14.8/7.8×/yr for PROX vs **17.6/13.9/9.6/7.0×** for the composite, and flips per ticker per year 4.8/7.4/7.0/5.4 vs 2.1/3.2/4.4/4.7, because eligible names cluster within a few percent of their highs and tiny moves reshuffle the top of the book. "Slower signal" is a property to be measured, not inferred from the signal's form. **(5) Rule 8 rejects it unanimously and with power.** Spearman(IS Sharpe, OOS Sharpe) over the 12 CAND points is **+0.888** (universe.json) and **+0.930** (broad) — against idea 8's +0.000, this is a choice the walk-forward *can* make — and it picks the incumbent every time: S1 and S2 both select COMP-n20 (OOS 14.4%/1.170/-18.3%, clears every OOS 4b bar) and COMP-n30 (OOS 11.6%/0.904/-20.3%, misses OOS DD by 0.1pp), with the composite winning 7 of 8 within-n signal audits. **(6) Nothing is upgraded.** The run's three 4b passes are reproductions of ideas 2, 8 and 10's published rows; 0 of 14 points pass 4a on universe.json (the six 4a passes are all on broad, where RULES v1 itself draws down -21.2% and 4a is not a bar). **Survivorship** is one-directional and flatters PROX — names that spent years far below a 52-week high and then delisted are absent, which favours a signal that avoids them — and it loses anyway, so the KILL is conservative. **For the Sunday review: recommended RULES wording is none.** Rules unchanged. Ideas 79-80 queued. Memo: `research/backtests/2026-09-04_52w-high-proximity_C.result.md`.
- 2026-09-04 — Research lane B, idea 83 `turnover-budget-instead-of-a-cost-assumption`: **KILL**. 176 points, all reported. A per-rebalance turnover budget budgets the EXITS, so it makes MaxDD WORSE (mean -2.4pp pro-rata / -5.1pp largest-trades-first) — the opposite side of the trade from the gross lever, which buys drawdown at 0.677 pp CAGR per pp MaxDD (range 0.626-0.729 over 16 arms, dSharpe -0.001, replicating ideas 66/74). Read in one direction: raising gross buys return ~3x more efficiently (0.68 vs ~0.22 pp/pp) and Sharpe-neutrally, so the budget is dominated on its own axis. 0 of 48 broad budget points pass 4b at 10 bps; no arm passes 4b on both universes. Rule 8: B is not selectable (Spearman IS/OOS +0.14/-0.07 on universe.json; the IS pick loses to its own control OOS in 2 of 4 cases). Two findings carried to QUEUE: (a) which 4b bar BINDS decides which lever can help — gross is strictly counterproductive on a CAGR-bound book (32/32 gross arms fail on CAGR) and the budget is the only lever that helps there, lifting `EWall`'s u56 breakeven 7.0 -> 20.0 bps (does not replicate on broad); (b) truncating a trade list is not self-financing, so the QUEUE's literal `largest-changes-first` budget is partly a disguised gross lever (realised gross 0.815 vs control 0.717), the mirror of idea 73's de-grossing artefact. Rules unchanged.

- 2026-09-04 (lane C, idea 15 crypto-sleeve) — **PARK: the largest and most consistent improvement the repo has produced, rejected by rule 8 four times out of four.** `baseline.load_universe` has excluded BTC-USD/ETH-USD from every backtest the project has run, yet both are cached and both are trend instruments of the kind the 200d gate is built for. 78 points (2 universes x 3 books [live v1 / idea 2's CAND20 / EWall] x [control + 2 funding modes x 2 gates x 3 caps]), all reported; harness reproduces idea 2's KEEP row (12.7%/1.093/-18.3%) and the live v1 row to the decimal and c=0 equivalence against the crypto-free panel is 1.4e-17. Two tuned params only: cap c in {0,5,10,15}% per name and gate in {`same` = v1's own 200d AND vol20<0.60 applied to crypto, `trend` = vol cap waived}; funding (`matched` = sleeve funded by scaling the equity leg down, gross unchanged; `add` = gross rises) is a reported structural variant, gross-matched pre-registered as primary because ideas 66/73/81 showed an unmatched gross change is an exact return lever. **(1) The effect is real and one-directional: 36 of 36 sleeve arms beat their own crypto-free control on full-sample Sharpe on BOTH lists** (paired daily t +1.71..+2.81 matched, +2.70..+3.21 additive), and **5 of 39 points pass cross-universe 4b at 10 bps** — `CAND20/matched/same/c5`, `EWall/matched/same/c5`, `EWall/matched/same/c10`, `EWall/matched/trend/c5`, `EWall/add/same/c5`. Both controls fail that test (CAND20 on broad H2 0.814 vs 0.837, EWall on u56 CAGR 10.4% vs the 10.68% floor — the two near-misses ideas 28/44 have been circling) and **the sleeve repairs exactly the bar each was missing**. The literal queued arm on universe.json, `CAND20/matched/same/c10`, reads 14.3%/1.178/-19.1% (halves 1.254/1.127, OOS 1.237) against 12.7%/1.093/-18.3%, at 10.3x turnover, and its advantage WIDENS with cost (dSharpe +0.154 at 10 bps -> +0.276 at 100) because the sleeve adds return without adding turnover. **(2) Why it is a PARK: the passing arms are unreachable.** In-sample Sharpe is monotone in the cap and higher for the loose gate in nearly every cell, so both pre-registered selection rules buy c=15/`trend` in 4 of 4 walk-forwards and **every one fails the OOS drawdown bar** (rule 8's window: u56 28.4%/1.315/**-31.1%** and broad 24.3%/1.226/**-30.4%** against a -20.2% cap; a crypto-era window IS<=2021: -19.4% and -24.1% against -14.7%); within-book audits pick c=15 in **11 of 12** cells and **not one** of the 5 cross-universe passers is ever selected. Spearman(IS,OOS Sharpe) is +0.771/+0.855 on rule 8's window but **+0.362/+0.252** on the crypto era — the stable ordering is the equity book's, not the sleeve's. **This is idea 66's failure shape exactly** (`ew-band3 g=0.90` also passes cross-universe 4b and is also unselectable), and two instances make it a pattern: on this repo a 4b pass sitting at an INTERIOR parameter value has twice been unreachable by any rule maximising in-sample Sharpe. **(3) Rule 8 cannot validate this idea on the data that exists.** BTC is tradeable from 2015-07-02 and ETH from 2018-08-27, so rule 8's in-sample window holds ~1.5 yr of BTC and ZERO ETH; the crypto-era walk-forward was added for that reason and rejects it too on 3.3 yr of in-sample. **(4) The t-stat is one calendar year.** 2017 (BTC +1425%, ETH +135%) sits inside H1 — which is why sleeve H1 Sharpes look spectacular (1.088 -> 1.408) even though only 13.1% (`same`) / 24.0% (`trend`) of H1 days hold any crypto at all. Restricted to 2018-09+ the effect shrinks by a third and loses significance (t_cry +0.66..+2.06; dSharpe negative in 4 of 18 gross-matched arms on universe.json), and 2018 is the counter-year the full sample hides: BTC -74%, ETH -82%, `CAND20/matched/trend/c10` **-0.4% against the control's +5.9%**. **(5) Survivorship is worse here than anywhere in the repo:** BTC and ETH are the two survivors of the crypto field and LUNA/FTT/the 2014-18 altcoin cohort went to zero rather than to an acquirer, so every figure is an upper bound on a two-ticker sleeve chosen with ten years of hindsight. Secondary: crypto trades 24/7 but the index is equity trading days, so weekend moves land in the Monday bar and a Friday rebalance cannot react — this understates realised drawdown; and 10 bps is an equity cost assumption. **(6) Transferable finding for RULES: the vol20<0.60 cap is a drawdown instrument whose value is conditional on the instrument's own vol distribution.** BTC's median vol20 is 0.532 and ETH's 0.714, so the cap is near-binding by construction: it cuts BTC's eligible days 43.3% -> 24.9% and ETH's 24.2% -> 7.8%. `trend` earns more standalone (BTC gated-only 38.8%/0.966 vs `same`'s 21.0%/0.855) but **every 4b failure among the sleeve arms is `DD`**, and `same` vs `trend` is the only lever in the run that changes which side of the cap a book lands on (mean dMaxDD -2.9% vs -7.0%). That is the mirror image of idea 56's small-cap result, where the same cap destroys value. **Recommendation to the Sunday review: do not adopt.** The exact RULES wording that would be required is recorded in the memo and recommended against; revisit only when a delisting-aware crypto panel exists (cf. idea 54) and ETH has >=8 further years. Memo: `research/backtests/2026-09-04_crypto-sleeve_C.result.md`. Ideas 87-89 queued (margin-aware selection for interior 4b passes, the vol cap as an explicit satellite clause, and a leave-one-year-out audit of every standing candidate).

- 2026-09-04 — Research cloud (idea 14, rsi2-sleeve): **KILL**, decisively. A Connors RSI(2)<thr-in-uptrend mean-reversion sleeve on the 16 sector/industry ETFs, funded by scaling the trend book down, was tested at f in {0.25, 0.50} and thr in {5, 10, 20} against three pre-chosen cores (live v1, idea 2's top20 KEEP, ew-all) on both large-cap lists at 5/10/25/50 bps — 42 leaderboard points, every grid cell printed. **The sleeve loses in 36 of 36 (universe, core, f, thr) cells at 10 bps** (mean dSharpe -0.412, median -0.371, paired daily t between -2.5 and -9.5 vs each arm's own control), and rule 8 selects f=0 (no sleeve) in 5 of 6 walk-forwards; the single arm a selection rule does buy (broad/top20 under the IS-drawdown-capped rule) fails OOS on Sharpe and CAGR. **The mechanism is turnover, not signal.** The signal is real and correctly signed — entry-day forward returns 0.209%/0.087%/0.049% at thr=5/10/20 vs 0.063% unconditional over 1026/2088/3830 entries, gross sleeve Sharpe 0.461/0.550/0.747 — but the sleeve holds 2.2-3.6 names for a mean 2.8 days and turns over **65x/113x/165x per year**, i.e. 6.5/11.3/16.5 pp of annual cost at PROTOCOL's 10 bps, which converts every positive gross book into a negative net one (-1.6%/-4.2%/-5.3% CAGR standalone). This is idea 76's holding-period finding at its extreme: the fastest instrument the project has tested is also the only one whose entire gross edge is consumed by its own trading. Two controls rule out the obvious alternatives: adding a one-day execution lag changes the standalone Sharpe by only -0.08/+0.04/-0.05 (the h=0..4 forward-return profile is flat, so there is no one-day pop being forfeited), and buying one day EARLY loses 45-72% CAGR, confirming the signal is a genuine dip-buy rather than an artefact. The only variant that comes close to breaking even is `div4` (never more than 25% of the sleeve in one name), which halves turnover to 28x and lifts standalone Sharpe to +0.26 at thr=5 — still dSharpe -0.003 to -0.057 on the blended books, i.e. at best free. **Transferable finding for RULES: a sleeve's admissible turnover is bounded by its gross Sharpe, not by its correlation with the core.** The sleeve's correlation with the trend books is only 0.37-0.49, so the diversification premise held; it simply cannot pay for itself. No RULES change proposed. Script: research/backtests/2026-09-04_rsi2-sleeve_cloud.py.

- 2026-09-04 — Research cloud (idea 89, one-year-leverage-audit): **infrastructure delivered, and it changes how two standing candidates should be read.** A generic leave-one-year-out harness (delete a calendar year, chain-link the remainder, recompute every statistic AND every 4b bar on the retained days so the benchmark moves with the sample) was run over 13 (book, universe) cells x 18 years at 10 bps, with zero tuned parameters; it reproduces idea 2's KEEP (12.7%/1.093/-18.3%) and idea 72's `B136/EWall` (10.7%/1.027/-17.7%) to the decimal. **(1) Only 2 of the 7 published 4b passes survive deletion of every single year:** idea 2's `top20` and idea 46's `frac085`, both 18/18 on universe.json. `ew-band3` (idea 57) is 17/18 on both lists, `frac085` 17/18 on broad, and idea 72's `B136/EWall` **10/18** — the weakest standing candidate by a wide margin. **(2) The far more important finding is that 12 of the 14 verdict flips are the BAR moving, not the book weakening.** Deleting 2020 raises SPY's 60%-of-MaxDD cap by 5.53pp (the COVID crash is most of SPY's -33.7%) and deleting 2022 raises the 70%-of-CAGR floor by 1.68pp, while the candidate books barely move — so PROTOCOL 4b's two absolute bars are themselves one-year-levered, and a LOYO 'failure' on DD or CAGR is usually a statement about SPY, not about the candidate. **(3) No equity candidate is unusually year-dependent:** worst-drop Sharpe deltas are SPY -0.059, v1 -0.065, `ew-band3` -0.071, `frac085` -0.074, `top20` -0.077 on u56 (1.10-1.30x the benchmark's own; 1.22-1.56x on broad). Nothing here resembles idea 15's shape. **(4) Every candidate's single best year is 2022** (excess over SPY +7.3 to +14.0pp) except `top20`/u56 (2018, +10.5pp): the 4b edge over SPY is, in the main, one bear market avoided, and that is the exposure to price — not per-parameter fragility. **(5) Harness validation:** applied to idea 15's PARKed crypto arm as a PAIRED DIFFERENCE (`EWall+c10` minus its own control), t is +2.04 full-sample and falls below 2.0 when any of 2016, 2017, 2020 or 2024 is deleted (worst 2020, t +1.48) — the harness flags it, and finds the dependence broader than the single year idea 15 named. LOYO on the same book's own Sharpe does NOT flag it, so **the audit must be run on the statistic the idea is argued on**, which is the one methodological rule this run would add. **(6) Rule 8 says LOYO-robustness is a disqualifier, not a selector:** on the broad list max-IS-Sharpe picks `ew-band3` (OOS 11.2%/1.074/-16.8%, 4b PASS) while max-IS-worst-case-LOYO picks `top20` (OOS 12.5%/0.894/-20.1%) — robustness-aware selection did strictly worse out of sample, so it must not be used to choose books or parameters. Proposed PROTOCOL rule 10 with exact wording is in the memo, recommended as a PARK-trigger only; PROTOCOL.md not modified (Sunday review decides). Caveat carried into the memo: a spliced equity curve's MaxDD is not a realisable path, so DD-based LOYO verdicts are optimistic. Memo: research/backtests/2026-09-04_one-year-leverage-audit_cloud.result.md. Script: research/backtests/2026-09-04_one-year-leverage-audit_cloud.py.

- 2026-09-04 — Research cloud (idea 16, monthly-seasonality): **KILL**, and the reason generalises. 144 grid points (2 universes x 3 books [live v1 / idea 2's `top20` 4b KEEP / `EWall`] x 6 month-masks x f in {0.00,0.25,0.50,0.75}), all printed, plus 6 controls; 2 tuned params (mask, f); harness reproduces idea 2's KEEP row (12.7%/1.093/-18.3%, halves 1.088/1.103) and the live v1 row (6.5%/0.666/-13.8%) to the decimal. **(1) The conditioning variable is not persistent.** Spearman between each series' H1 mean-daily-return-by-month ranking and its H2 ranking is **+0.000 / -0.182 / -0.364 / -0.021** on universe.json and **+0.000 / -0.049 / -0.322 / +0.028** on broad — 6 of 8 negative, max +0.028. Corroboration: September is the worst full-sample month in all four series yet appears in **0 of 6** in-sample-fitted `worst1` masks (in-sample worst-1 is Jan or Aug everywhere) and 1 of 24 `worst-k` masks overall, i.e. it is hindsight, not signal. Month t-stats agree: SPY's Sep is t -0.22 and the only |t|>2 months are the positive ones (Jul +3.54, Nov +3.05). **(2) The literal idea loses.** Only 24 of 144 arms beat their own control on full-sample Sharpe (mean dSharpe -0.080; best anywhere +0.063 on the weakest book, +0.003 on the standing KEEP), monotone in f — the more exposure the mask removes the worse it gets — and the removed exposure is bought back more cheaply by the plain gross lever (ideas 66/83). The KEEP candidate is the book it hurts most (mean -0.113). **(3) Rule 8 is anti-predictive 6 of 6.** The IS-Sharpe selector picks `worst6` (the freest mask) in every single case and loses **-0.292 to -0.570** of OOS Sharpe against its own control. **(4) The two cross-universe 4b passes are not results:** `top20`/`sep` at f=0.00/0.25 clear 4b only by lifting broad H2 from 0.814 past SPY's 0.837 while *lowering* full-sample Sharpe, on the one mask rule 8 never selects. **Carry-forward for PROTOCOL:** report the cross-half rank correlation of the conditioning variable BEFORE building any book — it is one line, and where it is ~0 no grid can rescue the idea. Applies to every queued regime/breadth/VIX/day-of-week conditioning idea. Rules unchanged; standing candidates unaffected.

- 2026-09-04 — Research cloud (idea 87, interior-4b-passes-are-unselectable): **KILL for the proposed rule-8 amendment; the two ideas it was meant to rescue were parked correctly.** 66 grid points across six 1-parameter risk grids re-run from scratch (GROSS/top20, GROSS/EWall, BAND/ew-all, N/ranked, CRYPTO/CAND20, CRYPTO/EWall) x 2 universes, 48 selections, all printed; 2 tuned params (grid parameter, margin m); harness reproduces idea 2's KEEP row (12.7%/1.093/-18.3%, halves 1.088/1.103), idea 72's `B136/EWall` (10.7%/1.027/-17.7%) and idea 57's broad `ew-band3` (11.1%/1.064/-16.8%) to the decimal. **(1) Rm(m) = argmax IS Sharpe s.t. the IS 4b drawdown cap met with margin m does not beat rule 8 on anything that matters:** OOS Sharpe vs the default arm +0.027/+0.029/+0.033 at m=0/1/2pp against R0's +0.031, it reaches the grid's OOS-best in 0 of 10 / 0 of 9 / 0 of 4 cells against R0's 2 of 12, and **13 of 48 selections are infeasible** (8 of 12 at m=2pp), so the amended rule is not even well-defined without a fallback. Its one incremental recovery, `GROSS/EWall` g=0.80 on broad, differs from R0's pick by **+0.0009** OOS Sharpe — 4b compliance, not performance. **(2) The premise's fatal half: the grids that produced the interior passes have no selectable content.** A 2x change in gross moves IS Sharpe by **0.0002-0.0046** and OOS Sharpe by 0.0001-0.0038 while moving IS MaxDD by 7-9pp (R0's pick there is fourth-decimal noise, landing on g=1.00 in 3 of 4 cells), and the crypto cap grid's IS MaxDD spread is **exactly 0.0000** on both universes because the in-sample max-drawdown episode predates the sleeve's tradeable window — a constraint blind to the parameter it constrains. **Rm recovers idea 15's c=0.05 in 0 of 8 (rule, universe) cells**, picking c=0.15 whenever feasible, which fails 4b on DD both full-sample and OOS on both lists. **(3) The margin is a second tuned parameter wearing a safety label:** `GROSS/top20` broad picks 1.00 -> 0.70 -> 0.60 -> 0.60 across R0/m=0/1/2 and four of six grids give a different pick per universe under at least one rule. **(4) Confirmed without any amendment:** 8 arms pass full-sample 4b on both universes, and idea 57's band book is the most robust object in the run (all four non-zero bands pass cross-universe; every feasible rule picks band=0.08), while idea 2's n=20 is picked on u56 and n=30 on broad, independently reproducing ideas 44/53's universe-specificity warning. **Proposed for the Sunday review — PROTOCOL 8a, a pre-test rather than a new selection rule:** report each grid's IS objective spread and the spread in each binding 4b bar; where IS Sharpe spread across the whole grid is under 0.02, or a binding constraint's spread is 0.00, the parameter is a policy dial, not an estimate — rule 8 does not apply and it must be set by a stated risk budget (idea 69). Rules unchanged; PROTOCOL rule 8 unchanged pending review.
- 2026-09-04 (lane C) — Idea 89 is-window-has-no-crash: **KILL**, rule 8's split stays at 2016. Moving it changes 8/12 (S13) and 5/12 (S21) published selections but <=0.034 realised 2022+ Sharpe on the selectable cells. Idea 87's premise refuted: S13/S16 share the same IS MaxDD (-22.1%) yet picks move, and a crash in the IS window makes 4b's DD cap LOOSER (it is relative to SPY on the same window). The binding in-sample 4b constraint is the CAGR floor, not the drawdown cap. Also: rule-8 selection is degenerate on grids whose parameter has no data in the IS window (crypto under S13). No RULES change.

- 2026-09-04 (cloud, idea 88 selectability-pre-test-across-the-leaderboard) — **KILL for PROTOCOL 8a as idea 87 worded it; a narrower replacement is recommended.** 11 one-parameter grids (N, fraction, gross x2, band, lookback, cost x2, cadence x2, hysteresis) x 2 universes = 22 cells / 122 arms, all reported; 2 tuned params (grid parameter, selection rule R0/R2); weekly, t+1, 10 bps except in the cost grids. Harness reproduces idea 2's KEEP row (12.7%/1.093/-18.3%, halves 1.088/1.103) and idea 10's `B136/EWall` (10.7%/1.027/-17.7%) exactly, and the cost-free simulator matches `engine.backtest` to 0.00e+00. **(1) The retrospective the queue asked for comes back empty.** 8a fires on 4 of 22 cells and all four are the gross grids idea 87 proposed it from; every published n / fraction / band / lookback / cost / cadence / hysteresis sweep passes with IS Sharpe spread 0.040-0.502 against the 0.02 floor (nearest: `HYST/top20` u56 at 0.0398, where the pick is also the grid's OOS-best). **No walk-forward conclusion on the leaderboard outside idea 66's rested on a grid with no selectable content**, and the classification is unchanged for any threshold in 0.005-0.02. **(2) The clause's stated rationale is refuted, with the sign reversed.** 8a is worded as a safeguard on selection; mean REGRET (pick's OOS Sharpe minus the grid's OOS-best) is **-0.001 on flagged cells against -0.061 on unflagged**, Spearman(IS Sharpe spread, REGRET) over the 22 cells is **+0.256** where 8a predicts negative, and **the four worst selections in the run all PASS 8a**: broad `F/fraction` -0.310, u56 `F/fraction` -0.232, u56 `CADENCE/EWall` -0.182, broad `CADENCE/EWall` -0.105. The fraction cells reproduce idea 46 exactly — rule 8 picks f=0.15 and takes OOS 16.8%/0.941/**-27.8%** (u56) and 11.0%/0.715/**-29.9%** (broad) while the grid's OOS-best reads 1.173 and 1.026, and the 4b-aware rule R2 is *infeasible* on u56. This is structural, not bad luck: a flagged grid's arms differ in OOS Sharpe by 0.001-0.004, so the pre-test can only ever flag grids where the choice is costless on the objective. Mean SKILL (pick minus grid mean) is +0.039 (t +1.49) on PASS cells and +0.000 (t +0.37) on FAIL cells — the separation exists but it is the flagged grids being *inert*, not the unflagged ones being safe. **(3) What the flag does track is risk.** Flagged cells' OOS MaxDD spread is **11.3%** against 4.8% unflagged: u56 `GROSS/top20` has an IS Sharpe spread of **0.0002** (identical to 3 dp at every g) while OOS MaxDD runs -12.4% to -24.0%, and the fourth-decimal pick lands on g=0.80 on u56 and g=1.00 on broad. That is a genuine defect worth a clause — a risk-disclosure one. **Recommended wording for the Sunday review: `8a. Risk-consequence disclosure` — report every walk-forward grid's IS objective spread AND its OOS MaxDD spread; where the IS objective spread is under 0.02 Sharpe while the parameter moves MaxDD by more than 5pp, the parameter is a policy dial: record that rule 8's pick is not an estimate and set it from a stated drawdown budget (idea 69). Explicitly reject the draft's second half** ("only parameters that pass the pre-test are walk-forward selected") — on this evidence it would license exactly the selections that go wrong and forbid only the ones that cost nothing, and passing 8a must not be reported as evidence that rule 8 selected well. **(4) By-products, all reproductions:** `N/ranked` u56 picks n=20 under both rules (OOS 14.4%/1.170/-18.3%, idea 2 confirmed) and n=30 on broad (4b fail on DD — ideas 44/53 again); `CADENCE/top20` picks monthly on both lists with zero regret and monthly fails 4b on broad drawdown at -26.1% (idea 64's trade-off, walked into by the selection rule); `HYST/top20` u56 picks k=2.00, 12.7%/1.145/-17.2% at 3.9x/yr turnover, 4b PASS full and OOS (idea 86 confirmed). Both KEEP paths on all 122 arms: 4b full-sample 32/61 (u56) and 16/61 (broad), 4b OOS 43/61 and 19/61; **4a 3/61 on u56 and all three are g<=0.60 arms** that clear v1's -13.8% drawdown by de-grossing and then fail 4b's CAGR floor (idea 20/66's lever), 46/61 on broad where v1 itself draws down -21.2%. No new candidate. Survivorship: both lists are current constituents, so absolute CAGRs are optimistic; the run compares selection rules on common grids, so the bias falls on every arm alike. Rules unchanged; PROTOCOL rule 8 unchanged. Result memo: `research/backtests/2026-09-04_selectability-pre-test_cloud.result.md`.

- 2026-09-04 (cloud, idea 85 exit-schedule-vs-entry-schedule) — **Idea 83's mechanism claim is HALF REFUTED, and the remedy it implies works exactly where the claim is true and nowhere else: PARK on the unranked book, KILL on the ranked one.** 44 arms plus a 16-point gross ladder across 2 books x 2 universes, all reported; 2 tuned parameters (budget B in {inf,0.30,0.20,0.15,0.10,0.05}, mode in {entry-only, idea 83's pro-rata total}); weekly, t+1, 10 bps with 25 bps also reported; harness reproduces idea 2's `CAND20` (12.7%/1.093/-18.3%, halves 1.088/1.103) and idea 10/72's `B136/EWall` (10.7%/1.027/-17.7%, halves 1.146/0.917) exactly. **(1) The decomposition.** The entry and exit legs are 50.0-50.1% and 49.9-50.0% of Sigma|dw| on all four books — a self-financing long-only book that resets to a fixed gross cannot be otherwise, so "which leg is bigger" was never the question. The informative split is what the exit leg is made of: **gate exits are 24.8% (u56 CAND20) and 11.1% (broad CAND20) against rank displacement of 67.8% and 82.7%**, versus **56.6%/57.0% gate on EWall** (rank 0%, drift trims 43%). Idea 83's "book turnover is mostly the 200d gate's EXITS" is therefore **false on the ranked book its budget was tested on** — on the broad list 8 of every 9 dollars of selling is rank rotation — and true on the unranked one. **(2) The remedy, priced against the lever.** An entry-only budget (every exit honoured in full, buy leg capped at B, unspent proceeds to cash) on `EWall`: u56 B=0.10 reads **10.0%/1.100/-12.9% at 6.3x turnover** against the control's 10.4%/1.050/-15.9% at 8.2x, and broad B=0.20 reads **10.9%/1.077/-16.4% at 7.8x** against 10.7%/1.027/-17.7% at 8.3x. Exchange rate **-0.14 and -0.01 pp of CAGR per pp of MaxDD, against the gross lever's -0.63..-0.73 measured on the same days** (the ladder reproduces ideas 66/83 to 0.01 and confirms dSharpe = 0.000 across it). It is **not** the lever in disguise: at u56 B=0.10 gross falls only 5.5%, which at the lever's rate buys 0.9pp of drawdown, and the observed improvement is 3.0pp. The advantage widens with cost (u56 EWall Sharpe at 25 bps: 0.925 control -> 1.012 at B=0.05). **On `CAND20` the same instrument does nothing**: dSharpe +0.001..+0.007 on u56 until the budget begins de-grossing (0.717 -> 0.538 at B=0.05, where its exchange rate converges on the lever's own -0.68), and **negative at every budget on broad** (-0.002..-0.016, t -1.08..-3.68). The exit attribution predicts which book responds. **(3) Idea 83's total budget reproduces, sign and size:** gross untouched (0.739-0.750 at every B), turnover cut up to 70%, and **MaxDD deepens in 19 of 20 arms, mean -3.0pp** (idea 83 reported -2.4pp pro-rata), with dSharpe positive but never significant (t +0.17..+1.89). **(4) PARK, not KEEP.** No cross-universe 4b pass at a common budget: broad `EWall` B=0.20 turns the control's OOS 4b CAGR miss into a PASS (4b full PASS, 4b OOS PASS) while the same B on u56 `EWall` still fails 4b's CAGR floor as its control does; over all 44 arms 4b full is 16/44, 4b OOS 24/44 and 4a 17/44 (all the 4a passes are on broad, where v1 itself draws down -21.2%). And **rule 8 picks the wrong end of the grid — a textbook case of idea 88's finding**: on u56 `EWall` in-sample Sharpe is monotone DOWN in the budget (0.970 -> 0.934) while OOS Sharpe is monotone UP (1.114 -> 1.224), so R0 selects the weakest arm B=0.30 (OOS 1.124 against the grid's 1.224) and R2 is infeasible; on broad `EWall` R0 picks B=0.20 (OOS 1.097 vs best 1.113). The IS Sharpe spread there is 0.036 — just above idea 88's 0.02 floor, i.e. a grid that passes the selectability pre-test and is still selected backwards. Note the paired daily return t-test has no power on this instrument (CAGR moves -0.4pp/+0.2pp; the whole effect is in drawdown and vol), so t -0.23..+0.58 at the favourable arms is not evidence against it. Survivorship: both lists are current constituents, absolute CAGRs optimistic; every arm shares the panel and the days, so the control-vs-arm comparisons are far less exposed than the levels. **For the Sunday review: if a turnover instrument is ever adopted it must be entry-only and only on an unranked book** — the ranked book's selling is rank rotation, and constraining it either does nothing or silently de-grosses. Rules unchanged. Result memo: `research/backtests/2026-09-04_exit-schedule-vs-entry-schedule_cloud.result.md`.
- 2026-09-04 — Research lane B, idea 84 `which-4b-bar-binds-decides-the-lever`: **KILL idea 83's two-branch lever rule as worded; replacement clause proposed; KEEP-candidate by-product.** 480 grid points + a 312-point fine gross ladder, all reported; harness reproduces `engine.backtest` to 0.000e+00 and the published control rows of ideas 2/46/57/72 exactly.
