The census is degenerate — the CAGR floor binds 6 of 8 (candidate, universe) cells, the DD cap 1, a Sharpe bar 1 — and unstable in both cost and window (IS vs full-sample binding bars disagree 3 of 8). The mechanism kills the branches: the Sharpe bars are **g-invariant in all 24 (cell, cost) triples** (Sharpe spread <= 0.0061 over g = 0.40->1.00, reproducing idea 66 on four books), so gross is ONE axis carrying BOTH non-Sharpe bars (~+1.4pp CAGR, -2.0pp MaxDD per +0.10 g at dSharpe 0.000) and "DD-bound -> cut g" / "CAGR-bound -> raise g" is the same lever in two directions. Idea 83's CAGR branch names the wrong instrument: the entry-only budget is CAGR-neutral by construction (it saves cost but raises cash), giving d_CAGR +0.01..+0.20pp against a 0.26pp gap where gross gives +3.7pp; the budget's real content is Sharpe (+0.047/+0.050 on broad) and MaxDD. Third branch idea 83 lacks: no lever converts a Sharpe-bound cell (best improving move +0.003 over 20 arms). **Replacement clause: 4b's DD cap and CAGR floor both sit on the gross dial, so they define an interval [g_min, g_max]; set g inside the interval that holds on both universes at 10 AND 25 bps, and if it is empty or a Sharpe bar fails, change the book — no exposure or turnover setting repairs it.** By-product KEEP-candidate: idea 57's `ew-band3` at **g = 0.85** — u56 12.8%/1.136/-17.1% (halves 1.113/1.160, OOS 14.4%/1.234), broad 12.6%/1.064/-18.9% (halves 1.163/0.971, OOS 12.7%/1.073), 5.5x/5.8x turnover — the project's first arm passing 4b full AND OOS on BOTH large-cap universes at 5, 10 AND 25 bps, and rule-8 selected at g=0.85 on both. This answers idea 58: the published g=0.75 dies at 25 bps on the CAGR floor and gross is exactly the CAGR lever, so the band was never what needed fixing. The rule under test has no net walk-forward content (mean OOS Sharpe 1.098 vs argmax-IS 1.097 vs control 1.083). Memo written. Ideas 90-92 queued. Rules unchanged.

- 2026-09-04 (lane C, idea 22 drawdown-control) — **KILL for a book-level drawdown trigger, and the instrument is now PRICED against the only lever the project trusts.** Idea 40 reported this mechanism as a KILL on lane A's no-scaler book at three thresholds, one cut depth and one reset rule; this run does idea 22 *as worded* on the live book, adds the control idea 66 made mandatory (gross is an exact, Sharpe-neutral lever, so a rule that spends half its life cut is a de-grossing in disguise and must be judged against a STATIC book at the same average gross), and puts the result on idea 74's axis. 3 books (RULES v1 as live; idea 2's `CAND20` KEEP-candidate; idea 72's `EWall`) x 2 universes x [control + D in {4,6,8,10,12,15}% x k in {0.00,0.25,0.50,0.75}] x 2 reset arms (`high` = idea 22's new-equity-high; `recover` = release at -D/2) x 3 cost levels = **882 points, all reported**; exactly two tuned parameters (D, k) with RESET run as an arm and every walk-forward selection confined inside one reset arm. Harness sanity: the control reproduces `engine.backtest` at max|diff| 0.0 and reproduces idea 2's KEEP row (12.7%/1.093/-18.3%, halves 1.088/1.103) and idea 10's `B136/EWall` (10.7%/1.027/-17.7%) to the decimal. **(1) The literal rule loses in 6 of 6 cells** — u56: V1 3.7%/0.485/-12.1% (dSharpe -0.182), CAND20 8.4%/0.917/-14.2% (-0.176), EWall 6.7%/0.833/-12.5% (-0.217); broad: V1 3.2%/0.440/-15.3% (-0.200), CAND20 7.5%/0.749/-15.3% (-0.209), EWall 7.2%/0.882/-14.3% (-0.146) — and in every cell it **converts a 4b-passing or near-passing book into a 4b failure on the CAGR floor**. Drawdown does improve in 6/6 (+1.7 to +5.9pp); it is simply not worth what it costs. 269 of 288 treated arms lose Sharpe against their own control; the best arm in the entire run is +0.003. **(2) The price, which is the transferable result: 1.02 pp of CAGR surrendered per pp of MaxDD bought (median over 288 arms) against the static gross lever's 0.31-0.70 in the same cells (mean 0.57) — the drawdown rule pays ~1.8x the going rate, and 252 of 288 arms are dominated outright.** Held against a static book at the arm's own realised average gross, only 3 of 288 arms beat the matched static book on both Sharpe and MaxDD, by +0.001..+0.003. **(3) The de-levering ratchet is confirmed and its extreme case is structural, not a parameter choice.** Median share of days spent cut is 50.5% (`high` reset) and 37.5% (`recover`), reproducing idea 40's 52-79% on different books: cutting exposure slows the recovery that is required to un-cut it. At **k=0.00 both reset rules are ABSORBING by construction** — a book earning nothing can neither make a new high nor recover to -D/2 — so `u56 CAND20 D=4%/k=0` exits in 2011 and never returns (97.3% of days in cash, +0.0% in 2020 and 2022, second-half Sharpe undefined because the return stream is exactly zero). A quarter of the k-grid is degenerate, and that is a property of the rule FAMILY: any exposure rule whose re-entry condition is a function of its own equity has this failure. Queued as idea 93. **(4) Rule-8 walk-forward: the rule is not selectable.** S1 (argmax IS Sharpe) picks the do-nothing corner D=15%/k=0.75 in **12 of 12** cells — bit-identical to the control in the 4 cells where it never triggers, and losing OOS Sharpe in **8 of 8** where it binds (-0.013..-0.066); mean OOS Sharpe control 0.922 vs S1 0.900. S2 (4b-aware) **picks nothing in 10 of 12 cells** (no IS point cleared the IS 4b bars) and in the other 2 picks that same do-nothing arm, -0.066. **(5) It does not even buy the bear it was written for:** 2022 is -9.0% un-cut vs -8.2% with the literal rule on u56/CAND20 (SPY -18.2%) and *worse* on broad/EWall (-10.1% vs -8.4%), while 2020 is where it is paid for (+15.4% -> +2.6%). Only the absorbing k=0 arms improve 2022, by being in permanent cash. **(6) Two results recorded against our own KILL:** turnover FALLS under the rule (u56 CAND20 9.6x -> median 7.8x/yr), so none of the loss is a trading-cost story — it is missing exposure during recoveries; and 36 of 288 arms are NOT dominated on the CAGR/drawdown axis, 24 of them in the single cell `broad/V1`, the worst book in the run (0.640 Sharpe, -21.2% MaxDD, failing all five 4b bars) — a drawdown rule is cheapest exactly where the book is not worth running. **(7) The 4a pathology again:** 4a passes RISE with cost (10 and 62 at 10 bps, 30 and 74 at 25 bps across the two lists) while 4b passes fall to **0 of 294 at 25 bps** — cutting exposure clears 4a only because RULES v1 is weak. **For the Sunday review: recommended RULES wording is none.** Idea 22 is now answered twice (idea 40 on the growth book, this run on the live book) and should be closed rather than re-parameterised; if drawdown is to be reduced, the supported instrument is the gross number itself, stated as a budget. Survivorship (current-constituent lists) runs *against* a drawdown control — a survivor list has shallower drawdowns than the live universe did — so the KILL is conservative. Rules unchanged. Ideas 93-94 queued. Result memo: `research/backtests/2026-09-04_drawdown-control_C.result.md`.

- 2026-09-04 (lane C, idea 26 ensemble-plus-momentum) — **KILL as worded; PARK a by-product that is the project's only cross-universe 4b pass.** 60 grid points (f in {0,0.25,0.50,0.75,1.00} x 3 books [live v1 / idea 2's `top20` KEEP / `EWall`] x 2 universes x 2 gross conventions [natural, gross-matched]), all reported, plus a year table, a 20-point cost ladder and a correlation matrix; 2 tuned params (f, book); harness reproduces idea 2's KEEP row (12.7%/1.092/-18.3%, halves 1.088/1.102), the live v1 row and idea 18's standalone sleeve row. **(1) The idea as written is a 4a pass and a two-bar 4b failure.** 50% macro-trend-ensemble B + 50% v1 top-5 gives 5.8%/0.791/-11.9% (halves 0.725/0.854, OOS 0.935) on u56: it beats the live book in both halves with a better drawdown on both universes and survives rule 8, but 4b dies on H1 (0.725 vs SPY 0.957) and on CAGR (5.8% vs the 10.66% floor). **(2) The proposed mechanism is CONFIRMED and still insufficient** — dSharpe against the linear blend of the parts is positive in 36 of 36 interior cells (mean +0.052, range +0.008..+0.085), i.e. the blend really does beat the weighted average of its sleeves everywhere; but Sharpe buys ~0.05 of convexity while CAGR falls almost exactly linearly toward the sleeve's own 5.0%, so 4b's CAGR floor binds by f=0.50 in every cell. The reason is in the correlation matrix: sleeve-to-book daily correlation is 0.63-0.82 (u56) / 0.63-0.75 (broad) because 5 of the sleeve's 9 assets are equity ETFs — it is a lower-return version of the same long-equity trend exposure, not a diversifier. **(3) By-product `top20 + 25% sleeve`** is the ONLY (book, f) in the grid passing 4b on both universes under both gross conventions (u56 10.8%/1.085/-16.0%, halves 1.061/1.110, OOS 1.184; broad 11.1%/0.972/-17.3%, halves 1.106/0.854, OOS 0.940) and it repairs idea 2's known broad-universe H2 failure (0.811 -> 0.854 against SPY's 0.834). It is PARK, not KEEP, on three counts: **rule 8 cannot select it** (IS Sharpe is monotone DECREASING in f on both lists, 0.993->0.614 and 1.044->0.612, while OOS Sharpe rises monotonically on broad 0.892->1.090, so the walk-forward picks f=0.00 in all four cells — the same unselectability idea 88 declined to amend around); **its whole effect is one calendar year** (the sleeve's contribution to annual return is negative in 17 of 18 years on both universes, positive only in 2022 at +1.6pp/+2.2pp); and **it is priced at the protocol's assumption rather than inside it** (the joint cross-universe 4b pass holds at 5 and 10 bps and dies at 15 — an improvement on the incumbent's 5-bps-only window measured in idea 82, but not a margin). Rules unchanged. Follow-ups queued as ideas 98 (leave-one-year-out as a KEEP bar), 99 (are defensive overlays rule-8-invisible by construction?) and 100 (re-run with the sleeve restricted to its 4 non-equity assets).

- 2026-09-04 (cloud, idea 23 earnings-season-avoidance) — **KILL.** 372 points (2 universes x 3 books [live v1 / idea 2's `top20` KEEP / idea 10's `EWall`] x 2 exclusion conventions [`replace` = promote the next eligible name at constant gross, `cash` = sit the holding out] x [1 no-blackout anchor + 15 post-quarter-end season windows + 15 placebo windows]), all reported; 2 tuned params (start in {7,14,21,28,35} days after quarter end, length in {14,21,28}); the anchor reproduces `rules_v1_weights` to 0.0. **(1) The premise is refuted before the rule is tested.** Single-stock SPY-excess daily return in-season vs out is **+0.81 bps/day on u56** (positive in 10/15 windows, max |t| 2.39) and **+0.05 bps/day on broad** (8/15, max |t| 1.42) — and the +45-day PLACEBO season's own max |t| is *larger* on both panels (3.10 / 2.16), so no window is distinguishable from window-shopping. The single real in-season effect is volatility (u56 0.36-0.39 in-season vs 0.30 out, for windows starting 14-28 days after quarter end): more risk, no more return. **(2) The rule loses in 179 of 180 season cells** (dSharpe mean **-0.129**, dCAGR **-2.19%/yr**, dMaxDD +0.50pp), and the best-of-15 window *cherry-picked per cell* still loses to its own anchor in **11 of 12 cells** (-0.075..-0.009); the one exception is +0.004 on `broad/v1/cash`, the run's weakest book. **(3) The placebo splits the damage in half.** Sitting out ~30% of stock-days costs -0.061 Sharpe wherever you do it; the season window costs twice that at the same duty cycle, i.e. the post-quarter-end weeks are where the *return* is — the Frazzini-Lamont announcement-premium sign, opposite to the idea. Turnover also RISES (15.5x -> 17.9x/yr), so this is not a trading-cost story in reverse. **(4) Priced as drawdown insurance it is off the bottom of idea 94's menu:** MaxDD improves in 99/180 season cells at a median **2.88 pp of CAGR per pp of MaxDD** (aggregate 4.34) against idea 94's dearest priceable instrument at 0.91 and the static gross lever at 0.57 — the same drawdown is ~5x cheaper by holding less. **(5) Rule 8 is a clean selection demonstration:** IS (2009-2016) Sharpe is HIGHER for a blackout arm than for the anchor in 10 of 12 cells (mean +0.100), so the walk-forward picks a season window in 10/12 and OOS Sharpe then falls **-0.106..-0.420 (mean -0.188)**, regret <= 0 in 12/12. **(6) The 7 season rows that pass 4b are inherited, not caused** — all sit on `u56/top20`, whose anchor already passes at 12.7%/1.092/-18.3%, and every one of the 7 has a lower Sharpe than that anchor; zero fail-to-pass conversions. **(7) The 4a pathology again:** 4a passes rise from 4/12 anchors to 47/180 season rows, because cutting exposure clears 4a only while RULES v1 is weak (ideas 22, 40, 94). Recorded against our own KILL: nothing. KILL rather than the queue's expected PARK — the calendar approximation covers most of the reporting mass of most names in both panels, and the effect is large, negative and monotone across 180 cells and two panels; exact dates would sharpen the window, not change its sign. A resurrection should have to clear the premise test (|t| > 3 on broad) first. Survivorship (current-constituent panels) runs *against* resurrection: survivors had fewer catastrophic announcements. Recommended RULES wording: none. Rules unchanged. Result memo: `research/backtests/2026-09-04_earnings-season-avoidance_cloud.result.md`.

- 2026-09-05 (cloud, idea 100 sleeve-with-a-real-diversifier) — **PARK (strong): the project's best drawdown-adjusted book, blocked on one un-pre-registered dial.** 120 points (5 sleeve fractions x 2 sleeves x 3 books [live v1 / idea 2's `top20` KEEP / idea 10's `EWall`] x 2 universes x 2 gross conventions), all reported; 2 tuned params (f, sleeve); the S9 control arm reproduces idea 26 exactly (dSharpe +0.052 in 36/36; `top20 + 25% S9` 10.8%/1.085/-16.0%, halves 1.061/1.110, OOS 1.184). **(1) The premise is confirmed and larger than supposed.** Restricting idea 26's 9-asset sleeve to its four NON-equity assets (TLT/GLD/DBC/UUP) moves sleeve-to-book correlation from **0.626..0.820 to -0.011..+0.212** (S4-to-SPY **-0.141**): the 5 equity ETFs were carrying essentially all of it. **(2) The convexity is 5.1x larger and 2.9x cheaper.** dSharpe against the linear blend of the parts is positive in 36/36 interior cells for BOTH sleeves, but averages **+0.265 (+0.070..+0.479) for S4** vs **+0.052 (+0.008..+0.085) for S9**, at a median **0.090 vs 0.031 Sharpe per pp of CAGR surrendered**. The queue's question answers yes on the exchange rate, **no in absolute CAGR** — S4 costs MORE total CAGR (-3.19% vs -2.15%) because it earns only 2.6% standalone against S9's 5.0%. **(3) Both objections that killed idea 26's by-product are repaired.** Rule 8: idea 26's sleeve was unselectable (IS Sharpe monotone falling in f, picks f=0 in every top20/EWall cell); S4's IS surface is not monotone, rule 8 picks **f=0.50 in 8/8 S4 cells** and the pick beats its own no-sleeve anchor OOS in **8/8** (u56 top20 1.308 vs 1.168; broad EWall 1.232 vs 1.019), regret 0.000..-0.104 with four cells at exactly 0.000. **This is a direct counter-example to idea 99** — defensive overlays are not rule-8-invisible in general; S9's invisibility was a property of an equity-correlated sleeve. One-year dependence: S4's year table looks like idea 26's (contribution positive in 2/18 years) but the **Sharpe advantage does not depend on it** — with 2022 deleted, dSharpe vs the anchor stays positive in **8/8** S4 cells (mean shrink -0.015) where S9's is negative in 4/8 both ways; leave-one-year-out is also kinder to the S4 blend (-0.065/-0.074) than to the bare book (-0.077/-0.083). **(4) And yet the literal 4b footprint is SMALLER** (interior passes S4 2/36 vs S9 4/36; the only cell passing all four universe x convention combinations is still S9/top20/f=0.25), because **every S4 failure is the CAGR floor alone**: u56 `top20 + 25% S4` natural reads 10.2%/**1.144**/-14.2%, halves 1.114/1.175, OOS 1.231 — clear of the Sharpe bars by 0.16-0.34 and the drawdown cap by 6pp, short of the 10.66% floor by 0.5pp. **(5) That shortfall is a GROSS artefact, not a return artefact.** The natural blend de-grosses to 0.648-0.700 (the trend vote is often partial); rescaling the rule-8 pick moves Sharpe by <=0.001 across g in {0.75,0.85,1.00,1.25} — ideas 66/84's exact-lever result reproduced — and at **g=1.00 (fully invested, no leverage) it clears 4b on BOTH universes**: u56 **11.8%/1.149/-14.2%** (halves 1.099/1.197, OOS 1.236), broad **12.2%/1.063/-15.6%** (halves 1.173/0.961, OOS 1.020), both with materially better drawdowns than the incumbent candidate's -18.3%/-20.1%, at **0.20-0.22pp of CAGR per pp of MaxDD** — level with the cheapest instrument on idea 94's menu (band3-rw 0.18) and unlike it Sharpe-positive. **(6) Recorded against our own case:** at the natural gross the cross-universe 4b pass holds at 5 bps and is lost by 10 (the CAGR floor again), and the g=1.00 arm was never cost-laddered. **PARK, not KEEP:** the 4b-passing version needs a third dial (exposure) that this run did not pre-register, and gross-tuning to clear a floor is the move rule 7 warns about even when idea 84 has shown the dial is exact. Regime caveat that must ship with any adoption: 2009-2021 is a falling-rate sample and the sleeve's one materially positive year (2022) is the year its duration vote went short. Recommended RULES wording: none this week; the wording a follow-up would take is in the memo, marked not-yet-adoptable. Rules unchanged. Ideas 101 (pre-registered fixed-g run + cost ladder + cadence bar), 102 (is the sleeve just TLT?) and 103 (correlation as the sleeve design variable) queued. Result memo: `research/backtests/2026-09-05_sleeve-with-a-real-diversifier_cloud.result.md`, `.memo.md`.
- 2026-09-05 — Research cloud (idea 114, IS-Sharpe-margin-as-the-reportable-selector-statistic): **PARTIAL — the margin is a selector-stability statistic and nothing more.** On idea 99/109/112's 44-cell harness (6 overlay grids x 2 books x 2 universes x 2 cost rungs, 178 grid points, weekly, next-day, 10/25 bps; the pooled LOYO pick-change rate reproduces idea 112 exactly at 72/352 = 20.5%), the margin M = IS Sharpe(best) - IS Sharpe(runner-up) at rule 8's pick was measured in every cell against two pre-registered hypotheses. **(1) M predicts leave-one-IS-year-out pick instability:** Spearman(M, change rate) = **-0.368** with one-sided permutation p 0.046 on the 22 primary (10 bps) cells, -0.240 at 25 bps, -0.317 pooled; the scale-free variant M/sd(IS Sharpe) is much stronger (-0.548 p 0.005; -0.652 p 0.001 at 25 bps) and is the only variant that also prices the swap cost (-0.461 / -0.609). Splitting the primary cells at the median margin (0.008 Sharpe) separates mean instability 0.125 vs 0.330 and 'pick was also OOS-best' 45% vs 18%. **(2) M does NOT predict out-of-sample regret:** rho **+0.119** (p 0.705), wrong sign at both cost rungs — and the reason is that there is no regret to predict. Mean OOS regret across the 44 cells is **0.015 Sharpe** (median 0.005, max 0.072) and rule 8's pick is already the OOS-best point in 16/44 cells, with pooled LOYO swap cost -0.003. Idea 112's 'the IS surface is flat and noisy rather than wrong' now has its OOS counterpart: **both** surfaces are flat over the same points, so the selector's choice barely matters either way. **(3) The margin is a property of the instrument:** band 0.044, crypto 0.036, sleeve 0.034 vs breadth 0.004, stop 0.004, gross 0.001 — and the two flattest grids (gross, breadth) carry mean instability 0.375 and 0.469, i.e. rule 8's pick there is close to a coin flip between IS-equivalent points. **(4) It does not separate KEEP candidates:** at 10 bps the 4b-passing cells have mean M 0.021 / instability 0.308 vs 0.016 / 0.111 for the failures — if anything the passers are the less stable picks. **(5) Walk-forward (rule 8, picks made on IS only):** 10 bps 4a 9 / 4b 13 / 4b-OOS 15, mean OOS Sharpe 1.115; 25 bps 4a 8 / 4b 7 / 4b-OOS 7, 0.982. The standing KEEP-4b candidate's cell (sleeve / u56 / top20 / 10 bps) is **unchanged**: pick f=0.50, full 12.3% / 1.180 / -14.3%, halves 1.161/1.200, OOS 13.6% / **1.261** / -14.3% vs RULES v1 OOS 0.699 and SPY 15.5% / 0.882 / -33.7% — with M 0.018 (below the 44-cell mean) and regret 0.000, which is itself the cleanest demonstration that margin says nothing about OOS quality. **Recommendation to the Sunday review (no file modified):** PROTOCOL rule 8 should quote M and M/sd next to every walk-forward, with the caveat stated in the same sentence — a stability flag, never a confidence interval, and M < 0.01 Sharpe is a tie. Caveats: survivorship (current constituents, identical across every cell compared), 22 points is a small sample and H1 clears at p 0.046, crypto's IS window starts 2014-09-17 (ex-crypto rho -0.368, p 0.058). Script `research/backtests/2026-09-05_IS-Sharpe-margin-as-the-reportable-selector-statistic_cloud.py`.
- 2026-09-05 — Research cloud (idea 98, one-year-dependence-as-a-KEEP-bar): **the proposed bar is KILLED as a gate and kept as a reported statistic.** Idea 89's LOYO harness (delete a calendar year from the book AND SPY AND v1, chain-link the remainder, recompute every statistic and every 4b bar on the retained days) was run over **every standing 4b pass the project has** — 9 books (idea 2's top20, idea 46's frac085, idea 57's ew-band3, idea 84's ew-band3-g085, idea 72's EWall, idea 94's EWall+vol60dg, idea 101's top20+50S3, the idea 99/101/114 sleeve blend top20+50S4, and v1 as a control) x 2 universes x 2 cost rungs x ~18 years, each book reconstructed from its own source script and reproduced against its published leaderboard row first. **(1) The queue's question:** 10 of the 14 full-sample 4b passes survive deleting their own single best year; the four that do not are u56 ew-band3 (CAGR), broad EWall (CAGR), broad top20+50S4 (OOS) and broad top20+50S3 (H2,OOS). **The best year is 2022 for 13 of the 14** (top20/u56 is 2018), mean excess over SPY in that year +11.7pp — the 'one bear market avoided' shape is now confirmed across every candidate, not one book. Only 5 of 14 survive deleting EVERY year; idea 72's EWall is again the weakest at 10/18 (idea 89 reported 10/18). **(2) The finding that kills the bar:** **14 of 16 verdict flips are the 4b BAR moving, not the book weakening** (idea 89: 12 of 14). All 6 MaxDD flips are bar-side — deleting 2020 raises SPY's 0.60x-MaxDD cap by 5.53pp while the books move 0.68-2.85pp — and under a relative-only 4b (H1/H2/OOS vs SPY on the same retained days, absolute bars checked full-sample only) **every candidate is 18/18 at 10 bps**. All of 4b's one-year dependence lives in its two ABSOLUTE bars, which are statements about SPY. **(3) The pre-registered rule-8 walk-forward (every bar applied on 2009-2016 ALONE, the admitted books then judged on the untouched 2017-2026 window):** on universe.json the ex-best-year, every-year and relative-only bars each admit **ZERO** books, while status-quo 4b admits 4 that go on to average **1.213 OOS Sharpe with 4/4 beating SPY's 0.882**; on the broad list ex-best-year admits the identical 7 books (+0.000) and relative-only 5 (+0.020), both under the pre-registered +0.05 bar. A gate that discards four true positives to buy zero true negatives is not a gate — all three variants are REPORT-ONLY. **(4) By-product worth the Sunday review's attention:** idea 94's `EWall+vol60dg` is the most year-robust book in the project (17/18 on u56, **18/18** on broad, at BOTH cost rungs) and one of only two standing candidates still passing 4b on both universes at 25 bps (u56 11.4%/1.113/-16.9%, broad 12.1%/1.119/-18.7%; the other is idea 84's ew-band3-g085). **Recommendation (no file modified):** PROTOCOL should require every 4b KEEP to REPORT its LOYO survival count, its single best year and whether 4b survives deleting it, plus the book-side/bar-side decomposition of each failing year — descriptive, never a gate. Caveats: survivorship (current constituents, identical across every book and window compared); MaxDD on a spliced series is an approximation applied identically to book and SPY (idea 89's convention); the selection test is 8 candidate books x 2 universes, so the u56 zero-admission result rather than broad's +0.020 is the load-bearing evidence; the S4 blend is run here on the crypto-excluded 56-name panel (11.9%/1.147/-14.2%) for comparability with every other book, vs 12.3%/1.180/-14.3% on the 58-column panel its published row used — verified directly. Script `research/backtests/2026-09-05_one-year-dependence-as-a-KEEP-bar_cloud.py`.

- 2026-09-05 — Research lane B (idea 117, crisis-depth-as-the-price-denominator): **PREMISE CONFIRMED on the large-cap panels and REFUTED on the small one; the episode-level replacement PASSES its pre-registered portability bar and is proposed to PROTOCOL rule 4 as a reporting clause, NOT a selector. Rules unchanged, no new book KEEP.** Idea 94's simulator was imported rather than re-implemented, so every number sits on the harness it audits: engine-equivalence `max|diff| = 0.0`, `EWall+vol60-dg` reproduces at 11.6%/1.133/-16.9%, and **idea 97's published headline reproduces exactly** (u56 gate tier 4.108 IS / 0.404 OOS against levers 1.002/0.616) before anything else was read. New: idea 62's episode classification (still Open) and the episode price layer. 3 panels x 3 books x 2 cost rungs = 18 cells, 306 arm-points plus a 19-point gross ladder each, 7,776 arm-episode prices, all reported; TWO tuned parameters (the episode depth threshold theta, with 8/10/15% all run and reported, and the instrument family in the selector). **(1) The premise holds on large caps and fails on small.** Regressing log(cell median price) on log(that window's SPY MaxDD): slope **-4.04 on u56** (t -5.78, R2 0.68), -3.05 on broad (t -3.35), **+0.13 on the 439-name panel (t 0.12)**. A window whose worst crash is shallow leaves little drawdown to buy, so everything in it looks dear — but that is not why the small-panel price is unstable. Stated limit: each panel supplies only two distinct depths, so these are IS-vs-OOS contrasts re-expressed as elasticities, not 54 independent points; the median IS/OOS ratio over the 18 cells is 2.18x, i.e. **idea 97's 10.2x pair is near the top of the distribution rather than typical**. The IS window's -22.1% 'worst crisis' turns out to be a 27-day fragment of the GFC that the 2009-01-13 evaluation slice merely clips, against OOS's 2020 (-33.7%) and 2022 (-24.5%) — idea 111's year-composition finding restated on the episode axis. **(2) The proposed replacement works.** Pricing per crisis — premium measured on CALM days only, divided by the drawdown saved inside each SPY episode, medianed within a 20 pp SHALLOW/DEEP bin — cuts the median cross-window |log10(IS/OOS)| from **0.455 to 0.184** against a pre-registered bar of 0.227, and is more portable in **92 of 109 arms** (sign-test z +7.18; 36/42 u56, 30/33 broad, 26/34 small). **The exact object idea 97 published, the u56 gate tier, goes from a 10.2x IS/OOS swing to 0.93x inside DEEP episodes and 1.34x inside SHALLOW ones: the whole gap was a depth-mix effect, not a change in the instrument.** **(3) The mechanism is measured with real variation in the regressor.** Protection delivered inside an episode, regressed on that episode's SPY depth over 2,400 arm-episodes: **+0.161 pp per pp, t +15.07**. So a whole-window MaxDD denominator is a crisis depth in disguise times a tier coefficient. Tier by tier: book-level DD control **+0.320** (t 11.6, R2 0.31 — the steepest, and the direct mechanism behind open idea 118), per-name gate +0.179 (t 12.4), entry budget +0.062, **per-name trailing stop +0.012 (t +1.46): its protection does not respond to crisis depth at all**. That is a mechanical and newly falsifiable version of idea 94/97's 'the stop is the dearest tier' — it is not merely expensive, it is inert with respect to the thing it insures. Across episodes the dearest crisis is **E5 (2018-02, -10.1% in 9 trading days) at 9.15 on u56 and 7.60 on broad**; the cheapest is E7 (2020) at 0.37-0.41 — a factor of ~25 inside one price list. **(4) It is a description, not a selector.** Rule 8 with parameters chosen on IS only: `Sdepth` (argmin IS depth-matched episode price) LOSES to idea 94's `S1` — mean OOS regret **+0.425 vs +0.337**, rank-1 4/18 vs 8/18, mean dOOS Sharpe -0.016, same pick in 8/18 cells. Its one signed difference is buying more drawdown for less return (OOS 7.4%/0.713/-19.5% vs S1's 8.2%/0.729/-21.3%; 4a 10/18 vs 5/18), which is the wrong trade under 4b's CAGR floor. Both sit well below SPY OOS 15.5%/0.882/-33.7% and above live RULES v1's 4.9%/0.451. **(5) No new KEEP.** 4b at 10 bps: u56 10 arms, broad 4 (`band3-rw`, `g200-rw`, `v1gate-rw`, `vol60-dg` — idea 94's published pair reproduced plus two it did not flag), **small 0, and none on all three panels**; at 25 bps 6/2/0. 4a passes 3/11/12 including the small-panel *control*, the known 4a pathology (ideas 22/40/94/97) driven by live v1 being weakest of all on small caps. **Recommendation to the Sunday review (no file modified):** amend PROTOCOL rule 4 with one sentence — a drawdown price must be quoted per crisis episode at a stated depth, with the premium measured on calm days, and a price whose denominator is a whole-window MaxDD may not be compared across windows or panels — and add the stop's non-response to depth as a citable clause. Do not adopt the episode price as a selector. Caveats: survivorship on all three current-constituent panels (idea 54), worst on small and in the direction that flatters gates; the calm-day annualisation compounds a non-contiguous day subset at 252 d/yr and is a premium proxy, not a tradeable return; 9 episodes at the point theta is a small sample and only P3 and P2's sign test have real margin; the small panel's refutation of the depth story is unexplained and belongs with open idea 118; calendar-day index (idea 38) still unfixed for u56/broad. Result memo: `research/backtests/2026-09-05_crisis-depth-as-the-price-denominator_B.result.md`, `.memo.md`.
- 2026-09-05 — Research lane B (idea 97, price-list-tier-bar): **KILL of idea 94's tier statement as worded; one clause of three survives every panel and every window and is proposed to PROTOCOL. Rules unchanged.** Idea 94's simulator was imported rather than re-implemented, so every number comes from the identical harness — engine-equivalence vs `engine.backtest` is `max|diff| = 0.0`, the published `EWall+vol60-dg` (11.6%/1.133/-16.9%) and `EWall+band3-rw` (12.2%/1.161/-17.7%) rows reproduce exactly, and idea 94's 12 walk-forward picks reproduce arm-for-arm. New: a **third panel** (data/prices_small.csv.gz, 439 names after holding SPY out and dropping the 44 corrupted series) and a tier layer. 3 panels x 3 books x 2 cost rungs = 18 cells, 17 arms + a 19-point gross ladder each, 816 arm-points, all reported; ONE tuned parameter (the instrument family/tier in the selector). The sentence was split into three falsifiable inequalities: C1 per-name gate < static gross lever, C2 lever < book-level DD rule, C3 DD rule < per-name stop. **(1) C1 is true in 33 of 54 panel x window x cell rows** — 6/6 on u56 and 6/6 on broad full-sample, but **2/6 on the small panel and 0/6 in u56's own 2009-2016 in-sample window**. C2 is 38/54 (full 13/18, OOS 9/18) and also inverts on small (2/6). **C3 is 50/54 and 6/6 on all three panels: the only clause that may be quoted.** The exact four-tier order holds in 11/18 full-sample cells, 2/18 IS, 7/18 OOS. **(2) The small panel does not weaken the ordering, it reverses it.** Median full-sample tier prices: u56 T1 0.468 < T2 0.592 < T3 0.659 < T4 unpriceable; broad 0.294 < 0.519 < 0.579 < unpriceable; **small T3 0.219 < T2 0.279 < T1 0.350 < T4 0.746**. The mechanism is not the one ideas 38/49/51 predicted — the 200d gate does not price negatively on `EWall/small` (it buys 21.9pp of MaxDD for 6.6pp of CAGR, rate 0.302) — it is that a book drawing down -30.8% gives the crude book-level DD rule far more drawdown to cut per pp of CAGR than any per-name signal finds. On `V1u/small` every gate prices **negative** (-0.34..-1.41: more CAGR AND less drawdown, +0.02..+0.10 Sharpe, 6/6 arms), which is queued as idea 119 for audit rather than cited. **(3) Idea 94's "a stop is not insurance" is refuted outside large caps:** on u56/broad the stop buys negative drawdown (median -0.69 / -1.03 pp, priced 0/6 each), on small it buys **positive** drawdown in 9 of 12 cells (median +1.07pp, up to 4.0pp). It is the dearest tier everywhere, which is the invariant statement; "inert" was a large-cap accident. **(4) C1 is also window-conditional on the panel it was derived from:** the u56 gate tier prices at **4.108 in-sample against a 1.002 lever** (SPY MaxDD -22.1% in 2009-2016) and 0.404 against 0.616 out-of-sample (SPY MaxDD -33.7%). An instrument that pays in crashes looks dear in a window without one — the crisis-density signature ideas 99/111 measured on overlays, reproduced on the drawdown-price axis, and the specific reason no price may be quoted without its window's depth. **(5) Tiers are more portable but not more stable.** IS->OOS median Spearman: tier +0.400 vs instrument +0.418 (idea 94's published 0.442 reproduces at 0.418 on the enlarged cell set) — a dead heat. Cross-panel: **tier +0.800 vs instrument +0.386**, with the instrument ordering going to -0.717/-0.833 on `u56 vs small, EWall`. **(6) Rule 8 refutes the tier selector.** `Stier` (argmin IS tier price over the rule tiers, then the median-IS-rate member) is worse than idea 94's plain `S1` in every panel: mean OOS regret **+0.489 vs +0.337**, rank-1 **4/18 vs 8/18**, OOS Sharpe a wash (+0.007). Report-only by-product: **in 12 of 18 cells no rule tier was cheaper in-sample than simply de-grossing** — the honest default is the lever. **(7) No new KEEP.** 4b passes at 10 bps: u56 14, broad 4 (`EWall` + `g200-rw`/`band3-rw`/`v1gate-rw`/`vol60-dg` — idea 94's published pair reproduced plus two it did not flag), **small 0**, every small arm failing H1, H2, OOS and the drawdown cap together against a control running 14.7%/0.767/-30.8% vs SPY 14.1%/0.862/-33.7%. 4a passes 3/11/12 including the small-panel *control*, which is the known 4a pathology (ideas 22/40/94) amplified by live RULES v1 being weakest of all on small caps (0.603 Sharpe, -32.8% MaxDD). **Recommendation to the Sunday review (no file modified):** amend PROTOCOL rule 4 with one sentence — price insurance against the same base book on the same days, quote the static-gross lever beside it, state the window's own MaxDD, and treat only "the per-name trailing stop is the dearest instrument" as general; every other ordering is panel- and window-specific and must be requoted. Do not adopt a tier-based selector. Caveats: survivorship on all three current-constituent panels, worst on small and **flattering** to gates there (it excludes the beaten-down names a gate would have sold), so the C1 inversion is a lower bound; the small panel's evaluation starts 2011-01-13 and its IS window is 2010-2016; the band width, vol threshold, DD-control settings and stop depths are inherited from earlier runs on overlapping data, so the IS/OOS split validates the ORDERING, not those constants; 18 cells is a small sample and only C3's 50/54 has real margin. Ideas 117 (price at matched crisis depth), 118 (why the DD control is cheap on small caps) and 119 (audit the negative `V1u/small` gate price) queued. Result memo: `research/backtests/2026-09-05_price-list-tier-bar_B.result.md`, `.memo.md`.
- 2026-09-05 — Research lane C (idea 116, 2011-the-opposite-outlier): **KILL as posed. 2011 really is rule 8's pick-moving year, but the dependence is a feature worth +0.010 of OOS Sharpe, and one clause of the premise is false.** Idea 112 left 2013 acquitted and 2011 as the open suspect on the other tail: the sample's best overlay year (+0.215), the year whose deletion moves the most picks (16/44), and — per the queue — the only year whose deletion widens the IS→OOS gap G. **(0) Reproduction is exact and mechanical**: idea 112's committed `picks.csv`/`deltas.csv`/`swaps.csv` are read back at runtime rather than transcribed, and this run agrees on **0 mismatches over 44 cells × 9 windows = 396 picks**, on the G-leverage table to 4 dp (full-IS −0.058, ex-2011 −0.101) and on the 2011 swap price (−0.0331). **(1) One premise clause is FALSE.** "The only year whose deletion makes G more negative" is wrong in idea 112's own data: **four** years do (2011 −0.0437, 2014 −0.0095, 2016 −0.0092, 2010 −0.0054). 2011's shift is 4.6× the next largest so the ranking stands; the word "only" is withdrawn. **(2) The controlled selector test says NOT SUPPORTED.** The harness was extended to **46 distinct IS year-sets** (FULL + 8 LOYO + 28 matched-length leave-two-out + 15 contiguous re-cuts) × 44 cells = 2,024 window-cell picks, nothing selected on the window axis. On the 28 six-year L2O windows — same length, only the year composition varies — the agreement premium A_keep − A_drop is **+0.169 for 2011**, the largest of the eight years and 3.9× the next (2009 +0.043), with four years negative. That is below the pre-registered 0.20 bar, so the claim as worded fails; a post-hoc check confirms the uniqueness is nonetheless real (the five lowest-agreement windows of 28 all drop 2011; unstructured-null p 0.00018, structurally-matched null at its 0.125 floor). **(3) It would not matter if it cleared the bar.** Keeping 2011 is worth **+0.010** of mean OOS Sharpe and **−0.010** of mean OOS regret, and mean regret across the whole design is only 0.018–0.028 — there is almost nothing for a selector to get wrong, independently reproducing idea 114 (mean OOS regret 0.015). The 16 ex-2011 swaps cost **−0.033** mean OOS Sharpe, worse in 11/16, buying +1.6pp of OOS CAGR for −3.1pp of OOS drawdown, net 4b −2 and 4b-OOS −3. **2011 is a FEATURE of rule 8's selector, not a defect**: 11 of the 16 swaps turn the defensive dial down (all 8 sleeve arms, both stop arms, gross 0.75→1.00), i.e. without 2011 in the window the defensive arms stop looking good. **(4) The re-cut clause is window LENGTH, not 2011.** Contiguous spans containing 2011 agree 0.682 with pick(FULL) vs 0.568 without, but agreement is monotone in length (4y 0.577, 5y 0.608, 6y 0.674, 7y 0.773, 8y 1.000) and the 2011-containing spans average 5.6 years against 4.3; the three spans that omit 2011 are short by construction. **(5) Walk-forward: rule 8's own window is the best of all 46** — OOS Sharpe 1.048, 4b 20/44, 4b-OOS 22/44, against ex-2011's 1.036/18/19 and the RECUT-omits-2011 family's 1.027/16.7/18.7; no re-cut of the IS window improves on rule 8 as written. SPY OOS 15.5%/0.882/−33.7%. **Standing KEEP-4b candidate untouched**: u56/top20/sleeve at 10 bps picks f=0.50 in **28 of the 46 windows** (f=0.25 in 8, f=0.00 in 6, f=0.75 in 4, f=1.00 never), 12.3%/1.180/−14.3% full sample (H1 1.161, H2 1.200), OOS 13.6%/1.261/−14.3%. No RULES change, no PROTOCOL change, no new KEEP. Caveats unchanged: survivorship on both panels (identical across every window compared), 2009 partial (~11.5 months), crypto arms near-inert before 2014-09-17, calendar-day index (idea 38). `research/backtests/2026-09-05_2011-the-opposite-outlier_C.py`, `.result.md`, `.console.txt`
- 2026-09-05 (cloud, idea 115 sleeve-G-is-2013) — **KILL as posed: the sleeve's rule-8 gap is not a gold story, and 2013 is a property of the WINDOW rather than of any sleeve asset.** Idea 112's 32 sleeve points reproduced exactly (max |diff| 1.1e-16; pooled G -0.1694 -> G_ex2013 +0.0350) before any new number was read; 8 cells (2 universes x 2 base books x 2 cost rungs) x 7 sleeve compositions x 5 fractions = 280 points, all reported; two tuned parameters (f, composition). **(1) All four sleeve assets lose in 2013 and gold is only the third-largest loser inside the leg** — TLT -1.16pp, DBC -0.80, GLD -0.71, UUP -0.69 — because the inverse-vol weighting gives the most volatile asset the smallest weight (GLD 1.92% of the book against UUP's 16.5%), so a -28.8% price move produces the third-biggest loss. 2013 is the only year in the sample in which all four lose together. **(2) Deleting gold does not remove the 2013 dependence.** exGLD halves the LEVEL of G (-0.169 -> -0.074) but still moves +0.186 when 2013 is deleted against S4's +0.204, and GLDonly passes 4b at 0 of 40 grid points with rule 8 picking f=0 (no sleeve) in 6 of 8 cells. The pre-registered test fails 2 of 3 conditions -> **FALSE**. **(3) The honest decomposition of 2013:** measured as excess over each variant's own other years, a zero-return leg holding the sleeve's exact weights (FLAT4) reproduces **27.5%** of S4's 2013 excess damage, gold **10.9%**, and the remaining ~62% is the other three assets losing simultaneously. 2013 is S4's worst year of 18 (z -3.4) and 2011 its best (+1.09); 2011's deletion moves G FURTHER (0.225) than 2013's (0.204), agreeing with idea 116. This is idea 111's crisis-density caveat restated with a number: rule 8's IS window contains a +32% equity year that punishes any diversifier, and a quarter of the penalty would fall on a leg earning nothing at all. **(4) On the composition axis it is DBC, not GLD, that carries the sign.** exDBC is the only variant whose pooled G is positive (+0.021) — removing commodities removes the sleeve's rule-8 blind spot — while exTLT roughly doubles the gap (-0.285). **(5) One KEEP-candidate, gated.** `exDBC / top20 / 10 bps` is the rule-8 pick (f=0.50, chosen on 2009-2016 alone) and passes **both** KEEP paths on **both** large-cap universes: u56 12.1%/1.200/-13.4%, halves 1.237/1.170, OOS 12.9%/1.240/-13.4%; broad 11.9%/1.068/-14.6%, OOS 11.0%/0.974 — against SPY 15.2%/0.889/-33.7% and RULES v1 6.3%/0.649/-13.8%. It dominates the incumbent S4 candidate full-sample in 8 of 8 cells (mean dSharpe +0.027, drawdown 0.021pp shallower) and loses to it marginally out of sample (mean dOOS Sharpe -0.015). Composition was a tuned axis, so this is a candidate and not a selection this run made, and it must not be adopted before queue idea 106 settles whether DBC's drag is a 2009-2013 contango artefact. Memo with exact RULES wording: `2026-09-05_sleeve-G-is-2013_cloud.memo.md`. **For the Sunday review:** do not write a gold clause into RULES on the strength of the sleeve's G; state the blind spot as a property of the IS window; and treat idea 106, not idea 105, as the load-bearing open sleeve question. Rules unchanged. Survivorship: both lists are current-constituent, so absolute CAGRs are optimistic — every statistic above is a same-names, same-days difference plus four ETFs with no survivorship exposure. Result: `research/backtests/2026-09-05_sleeve-G-is-2013_cloud.result.md`.
- 2026-09-05 (lane C) — Idea 113 crisis-beta-as-the-pre-registrable-selector: **KILL**. Idea 99's -1.03 pooled slope of overlay value on the year's SPY MaxDD does NOT survive being estimated per grid point and used as a selector. `argmax [IS Sharpe + beta_IS x B]` at B in {0.05,0.10,0.20} loses to plain rule 8 (B=0) in all three budgets on the same 44 cells: mean OOS Sharpe 0.960 vs 1.048, dOOS -0.088 against a +0.0150 ceiling (independently reproduced), 4b passes 20 -> 15/14/14; the sign-flipped control does BETTER (-0.025), which falsifies the mechanism. Why: IS crisis beta shrinks ~6x out of sample (slope +0.172) and is fitted at R^2 ~0.5-5% on 7 years, and beta_IS is an unbounded superlinear function of overlay DOSE so any B>0 saturates the dial at the grid endpoint. Realised budget was only B*=+0.027, and shrunk the right coefficient is ~0.005 — rule 8's B=0 is within a rounding error of optimal. Third selector proposal in a row to lose to argmax IS Sharpe (idea 109's CAGR floor -0.010). Recommend closing the selector line. No RULES or PROTOCOL change.
- 2026-09-05 (cloud, idea 119 V1u-small-negative-price) — **INDEPENDENT REPLICATION of lane B's run of the same idea, written and run in parallel without sight of it; the two agree to the decimal on every shared number (premise count 6 priceable / 5 negative at 10 bps; rule-8 pick `g200-rw` with OOS 8.9%/0.631/-32.8% against live v1 7.9%/0.581 and SPY 15.5%/0.882; concentration direction; 0 KEEP passes) and reach the same verdict by different attacks.** **KILL the citation as worded: the `small/V1u` negative gate prices are a ratio of noise, and the one effect that survives resampling is a SELECTION change, not free insurance.** Idea 97's 32 committed pricelist rows for that cell reproduced exactly (max |diff| 1.8e-15) through idea 94's own simulator and idea 97's own panel loader before any new number was read; two tuned parameters (gate family, position count n); every arm, floor, cost rung and draw reported. **(1) The queue's wording is not literal.** 11 negative of 13 priceable of 20 gate rows — `band3-dg` prices POSITIVE at both cost rungs (+0.604, +0.299) and both `vol60` arms buy exactly zero drawdown, so 'every gate prices negative, 6/6' overstates it. The quoted -0.34..-1.41 range is right for the arms it covers. **(2) The decisive objection is the denominator.** The control book draws down **-34.0 pp**; the negative-priced arms buy 0.05-3.28 pp, i.e. **0.15-9.7% of the book's own depth**, and under the pre-registered materiality bar (dMaxDD >= 10% of the control's |MaxDD| = 3.40 pp) **0 of 11 negative rows are material**. Raising idea 94's 0.10 pp priceability floor to 3 pp leaves ONE gate row in the whole cell. The headline -1.41 is 1.19 pp of drawdown on a 34 pp book. **(3) 200-draw name-subsample bootstrap (100 draws dropping 10% of the 439 names, 100 dropping 25%), pre-registered decision rule: ARTEFACT in 7 of 12 audits.** The split is structural, not random: the three arms whose CAGR gain keeps its sign in 93-100% of draws are all **`-rw`** arms, which idea 94 itself defines as rebuilding the book among gated-in names — a SELECTION change — while every honest de-gross (`-dg`) insurance arm fails (sign held in only 52-82% of draws). **(4) It is not one year and not costs.** Deleting any single calendar year leaves the CAGR gain signed in 0 of 96 arm-years (2020 is the largest single year: `abs12-rw` +10.9 pp, `g200-rw` +10.3 pp); the gain is already present at **0 bps** (-1.76 pp for `g200-rw`) and shrinks as costs rise; and every gated arm turns 32.8-33.5x/yr against the control's 33.3x, so the gate saves no trading. **(5) It IS largely top-5 concentration.** Negative-priced arms fall 5/6 -> 4/8 -> 4/9 -> 2/9 as n goes 5 -> 10 -> 20 -> 40; the de-gross arms' CAGR sign flips to the normal one (gate costs return) by n=20, and dMaxDD rises 0.57 -> 7.69 pp — only a wider book buys material drawdown, and there it pays for it in the ordinary way. **(6) Rule-8 walk-forward** (idea 94's selector S1 on the IS window only) picks `g200-rw` at both rungs, OOS price -1.501/-1.589 with regret 0.000 — it lands on the OOS-cheapest arm of a ratio finding (2) shows is uninterpretable. The BOOK reads 8.7%/0.634/-32.8% full sample (halves 0.689/0.587), OOS 8.9%/0.631/-32.8%, against RULES v1 on the same panel 7.9%/0.581/-32.8% and SPY 15.5%/0.882/-33.7%; **0 of 14 audited books pass 4a and 0 of 14 pass 4b** at either rung. **Survivorship is stated because it runs AGAINST the artefact hypothesis**: the panel is current constituents, which flatters the UNGATED book (it holds beaten-down names that all survived) and so makes a gate look worse here, not better — the artefact case rests on the denominator, the concentration and the resampling instability, which is where this run puts it. **For the Sunday review:** (a) do not cite `small/V1u` as evidence that gates are free insurance on small caps — the supportable sentence is in the result memo; (b) **proposal that does not touch RULES: replace idea 94's absolute priceability floor (dMaxDD > 0.10 pp) with a RELATIVE one — an instrument is priceable only if it buys >= 10% of the control book's own |MaxDD|.** Under the absolute floor this cell publishes 11 negative prices; under the relative floor it publishes none, which is the correct answer. Re-tabulating every published price under that floor is queued as idea 120; (c) this and idea 117 are the same finding from two directions — a price is not interpretable without the depth of the drawdown it is divided by. Rules unchanged. Result: `research/backtests/2026-09-05_V1u-small-negative-price_cloud.result.md`.
  ADDITIVE in this run against lane B's: the pre-registered MATERIALITY bar (0 of 11 negative rows move >= 10% of the control's own drawdown; a 3 pp floor leaves one gate row in the cell), the `-rw` vs `-dg` split inside the bootstrap (every arm whose CAGR sign survives name-resampling is the SELECTION convention and every de-gross insurance arm fails), the 0-bps decomposition showing the gain is not a cost saving, and the relative-priceability-floor proposal (queued as idea 123, complementing lane B's idea 122 on the denominator's SIGN with a test of its SIZE). ADDITIVE in lane B's and not measured here: the vol-scaler attribution (/sqrt(vol20) costs 11.7 pp/yr at n=5 on this panel) and the investability bound (held-name median ADV $4.33M). Two scripts, two authors, one verdict.

- 2026-09-05 (cloud, idea 118 why-the-DD-control-is-cheap-on-small-caps) — **ANSWERED; KILL for citing idea 97's small-panel row as "the cheapest instrument there".** Idea 94's simulator imported unchanged: all 36 committed ddctl rows reproduce at 1.1e-16. 126 arm-points (3 panels x 3 books x 2 costs x [3 D x 2 k + 1 reset]), 25 depth-matched re-pricings, 6 vol-split re-pricings; 2 tuned params (D, k). **(1) The price is mostly the panel's own exchange rate**: `lever` (static-gross ladder slope) equals the control's Calmar in 18/18 cells, Spearman(rate, lever) = 0.831 over 108 arms, and panel rate 0.824/0.547/0.324 (u56/broad/small) normalises to 1.718/1.360/1.153 — spread 2.54x -> 1.49x (the pre-registered "half" bar is missed; 68% of the excess over parity is removed). **(2) At matched control drawdown the ordering INVERTS**: small goes 0.324 -> 0.608 and becomes the DEAREST panel, broad the cheapest — idea 97's row is a reading of a -34% control, not of small caps. **(3) Name vol is not an independent channel** (raw: low-vol half 0.228 vs high-vol 0.290, refuting the queue's guess; the sign only flips after normalisation). **(4) The mechanism is idea 93's absorbing state**: on the small panel the control is armed 74.2% of days (u56 50.6%), mean episode 465 days, and it ends the sample still armed in 24/36 arms — it IS a de-grossing, which is why its price converges on the lever. **(5) Decisive control**: at matched drawdown the static-gross ladder beats the DD arm in 107/108 arms (36/36 on small), so idea 22's headline is not reversed and idea 74's menu should quote the matched-drawdown comparison, never the pp/pp ratio without its panel's lever. 4b 3/126, 0 on the small panel. Rules unchanged.


- 2026-09-05 (cloud, idea 121 liquidity-screened-small-panel) — **ANSWERED; a $1M ADV floor proposed as PROTOCOL clause 10, a reporting requirement plus a stated default, NOT a `load_universe` change.** Engine equivalence 1.4e-17; idea 120's no-ranking control reproduced to 3dp. Pre-registered capacity criterion (ADV only, no return input): smallest ladder floor at which $10M trades <= 10% of the p25 held-name 20d median dollar volume in the ranked n=20 book. Unscreened that book trades **17.6%** of its p25 name's daily volume per rebalance (R10 46%, R5 **137%**); **$1M** brings it to 7.3%. **(1) The floor's price is the whole panel**: equal-weight-all goes 10.18%/5.92%/1.64%/-4.92% CAGR (Sharpe 0.678/0.413/0.181/-0.163) on 348/252/141/44 names at none/$1M/$5M/$20M, and that decay is inseparable from survivorship because the missing delisted cohort IS the thin cohort. **(2) The floor deletes every 4a claim this panel has made**: 7 of 48 (book,gross) verdicts move, all 4a True->False, 4a by floor 7/0/0/0; 4b is 0 of 192 at every floor, so nothing capital-worthy is lost. **(3) But the cited findings SURVIVE**, which is the opposite of idea 120: the gate's -5.4 pp/yr small-cap cost is -6.52/-4.87/-4.03/-3.56 pp by floor, negative in 12/12 cells, -3.70 pp even at 0 bps; and the four-way gate ordering holds exactly at the proposed floor. Idea 120's scaler premium remains the only published small-panel finding a floor reverses. **(4) A sharper, unrelated exposure surfaced**: the four-way gate spread is **0.080** under gross-matched weights (idea 81) against **0.356** as published — the ordering is stable, the magnitude that the QUEUE cites is a weighting convention. **(5)** Rule-8 picks give OOS Sharpe 0.286/0.163/-0.026/-0.608 vs SPY 0.882, monotonically worse as the floor rises, and the drawdown-capped selector is empty at every floor >= $1M. Rules unchanged.

- 2026-09-05 (cloud, idea 96 stop-as-negative-insurance) — **ANSWERED; idea 94's per-name trailing-stop KILL STANDS and is strengthened. The negative dMaxDD sign is neither the instrument nor the weekly grid: it is the ONE-DAY EXECUTION LAG, and PROTOCOL rule 2's own convention is the one under which the stop destroys drawdown. Rules unchanged; an extension of rule 2 is proposed.** Idea 94's module imported unchanged — the generalised simulator at (check=daily, lag=t+1) reproduces its stop arms at **max|diff| 0.000e+00** with zero firing-count differences, and its published medians re-derive (dMaxDD -0.69/-1.32 pp vs -0.69/-1.25, dSharpe -0.036/-0.007, turnover 12.5x/11.3x). 144 arms + 12 matched controls + 132 static-gross ladder points, all reported; two tuned params (check frequency, execution lag). **(1) The queue's premise about the code was wrong**: idea 94's stop is evaluated on every row, so the exit was already intra-week and the weekly grid governs re-entry only; check frequency moves median dMaxDD by 0.1-1.3 pp and never flips a sign. **(2) The lag does everything, and non-monotonically**: median dMaxDD **-0.69 pp** at t+1, **+2.44 pp** at t+2, **-0.25 pp** at the next rebalance. This run's own opening audit claimed idea 94 was one bar FAST; that was wrong (`engine` holds `w.shift(1)`, so idea 94 is conformant), and the correction is recorded in the script beside the predictions it invalidated rather than edited away. **(3) The mechanism is a short-term reversal, measured**: the day after a stop fires the triggering name earns **+0.57% to +2.21%** against an unconditional +0.06%/day (**t +2.70 to +10.37**, 9/12 cells, persisting +1.2..+2.6% over five days). A one-day-slower exit is not better insurance — it is a bounce harvested 15-280 times a year, which is idea 94's 'sells into the drawdown' sentence quantified. **(4) Nine arms appear to pass 4b and none may be quoted**: all sit on the non-conformant t+2 lag, all on `u56`, clearing 4b's drawdown cap by 0.15-1.6 pp on a single-path extremum against controls that fail 4b on that cap alone; **rule 8 picks the no-stop control in all four cells where they live** (IS differences <= 0.001, mean OOS regret +0.002), and 0 of 9 appear on `broad`. **(5) The generalisable exposure**: every drawdown price the project has published (ideas 9, 22, 40, 74, 94, 97, 117) is quoted without an execution lag, and for a fast instrument the lag moves the price by more than the instrument does — queued as idea 126. The post-trigger reversal is queued separately as idea 125 rather than smuggled in as a stop result. **Proposed to the Sunday review (no file modified):** extend PROTOCOL rule 2 — any rule acting between scheduled rebalances must state its trigger frequency and execution lag separately, the lag must match the scheduled book's, and no drawdown price may be quoted without the lag it was computed at. Caveats: current-constituent survivorship on both panels runs WITH this conclusion (it flatters the always-invested control), so the result is a lower bound on the stop's value; MaxDD is one number off one path; the reversal is not a tradeable return; idea 38's calendar-day index is still unfixed for these panels and applies identically to arm and control. Result: `research/backtests/2026-09-05_stop-as-negative-insurance_cloud.result.md`, `.memo.md`.
- 2026-09-05 — Research lane B (idea 95, vol-threshold-sweep-on-ewall): **KILL of `0.60` as a DERIVED threshold; the incumbent book survives the sweep unbeaten. Adoption of `EWall + vol60-dg` is UNBLOCKED on the numbers and CONSTRAINED on the wording. Rules unchanged.** Idea 94's script was imported rather than re-implemented, so every number sits on the simulator being audited: engine-equivalence `max|diff| = 0.000e+00`, idea 94's published `EWall+vol60-dg` u56 row reproduces at **11.6%/1.133/-16.9%** exactly, and `theta=0.60/dg` in this grid matches it to 0.000e+00 before anything new was read. `theta in {0.40,0.50,0.60,0.80,1.00}` x convention `{dg,rw}` x 2 universes x 10/25 bps = **40 points, all reported**, plus 4 ungated controls; exactly TWO tuned parameters. **(1) The surface is flat in Sharpe and monotone in everything else.** The whole theta axis moves Sharpe by **0.009 to 0.074**, and the ungated control sits INSIDE the sweep's own range in **7 of 8 (universe x cost x convention) cells**; the argmax wanders across 0.40/0.50/0.60/1.00 with no stable location, including both ENDPOINTS on the same universe at different cost rungs. What theta does move, monotonically in 8/8 cells, is exposure: on u56 @10bps `dg`, CAGR 9.4% -> 12.7% and MaxDD -13.7% -> -20.9% against a control at 13.3%/-22.5%, mean names held 47.2 -> 53.8 of 54.1. **Every one of the 40 arms LOSES CAGR to its own ungated control** (-0.25..-4.04 pp/yr, negative 40/40, |t| >= 2 in only 13/40; at the published 0.60, -1.05..-1.87 pp/yr at |t| 1.53-2.00). The vol20 gate on this book is a de-risking dial, not a signal. **(2) 0.60 is not chosen by an edge — it is where 4b's two ABSOLUTE bars bracket.** 18/40 pass 4b, 19/40 pass 4a, controls 0/4 and 1/4. The failure column splits perfectly along the axis: theta=0.40 fails the **CAGR floor** (9.0-10.5% vs 10.66%), theta=0.80/1.00 fail the **MaxDD cap** (-18.9..-25.2% vs -20.23%), and **no Sharpe bar ever binds anywhere in the grid**. Only two of the 40 points pass 4b in all four (universe x cost) cells: the incumbent `0.60/dg` (u56 11.6%/1.133/-16.9%, halves 1.156/1.113, OOS 1.186, 1.39x turnover; broad 12.4%/1.138/-18.7%, halves 1.255/1.027, OOS 1.122) and a genuine co-equal `0.50/rw` (11.7%/1.123/-18.2%, OOS 1.185; broad 12.5%/1.127/-19.5%, OOS 1.107) at 2.4x turnover. This is idea 84/90's gross-interval result reproduced on a new axis: the threshold is a risk-budget LOCATION, not a discovered constant. **(3) Rule 8 cannot set it, and its selectability FLIPS SIGN between two heavily-overlapping large-cap lists.** Both selection rules fixed in writing first (S1 argmax IS Sharpe; S2 same among points meeting 4b's two absolute bars on the IS window alone). **0 of 8 walk-forward selections pick 0.60.** On u56 both rules pick **theta=1.00** — the loosest, effectively no gate — at OOS rank 7 and 8 of 10, and the pick **LOSES to the ungated control out of sample** (1.133 vs 1.136; 1.111 vs 1.125); on broad both pick **theta=0.40/dg** at OOS rank 1 (10.2%/1.195/-12.6%), beating the control. Opposite endpoints, and **rho(IS Sharpe, OOS Sharpe) = -0.697/-0.636 on u56 against +0.588/+0.939 on broad** — in-sample Sharpe is *anti*-informative about the threshold on the primary universe. vs SPY OOS 15.5%/0.882/-33.7% and RULES v1 OOS 7.7%/0.747 (u56) / 5.9%/0.576 (broad). **(4) The one thing the gate earns, and it points away from the incumbent.** Against idea 94's 19-point static-gross ladder interpolated to each arm's OWN MaxDD, the gate reaches the same drawdown at a HIGHER CAGR in **38 of 40 points** (+0.11..+3.85 pp; ladder slopes 0.595/0.588 u56, 0.565/0.558 broad), pricing at 0.257-0.318 pp/pp at 0.60 — about half the cost of simply holding less. But the largest lever edge, the best Sharpe (1.190) and the best drawdown (-12.6%) in the entire run all sit on `broad theta=0.40/dg`, which **fails 4b in 4/4 cells on the CAGR floor alone** — ideas 100/117's shape again: 4b's absolute bars, not its risk-adjusted ones, decide these cases. **(5) Supports open idea 123:** under idea 94's absolute priceability floor all 40 arms are priceable; under idea 123's proposed RELATIVE floor (dMaxDD >= 10% of the control's own |MaxDD|) **12 of 40 stop being priceable**, every one of them theta in {0.80,1.00}, and both ratios above 1.0 in this run are inside that discarded set. **Recorded against our own reading:** the incumbent is not beaten, is the cheaper of the two 4/4 points in turnover, and a wide plateau is the BENIGN form of unselectability (idea 66's gross result) — the walk-forward's failure is evidence about the parameter, not against the book. **Survivorship** (idea 54) runs toward this run's finding, not against it: a vol20 gate is exactly the instrument a current-constituent panel flatters, and it flatters LOOSE thresholds more than tight ones. Calendar-day index (idea 38) still unfixed. **For the Sunday review:** do not re-tune the threshold; if the book is adopted, adopt it with the exact wording in the memo, which states 0.60 as a **declared risk-budget constant with its measured plateau** and forbids RULES from calling it a volatility threshold, an optimum, or a fitted value. Ideas 128-130 queued. Result: `research/backtests/2026-09-05_vol-threshold-sweep-on-ewall_B.result.md`, `.memo.md`.
- 2026-09-05 (lane B, idea 129 absolute-bars-disqualify-the-best-insurance) — **ANSWERED; KILL of the claim that PROTOCOL rule 4b's CAGR floor is a risk bar.** Corpus = 3 panels (u56, broad, small) x 3 books (V1u, TOP20, EWall) x 17 published instruments x 2 cost rungs = 306 arm-rows on idea 94's imported simulator, plus a 342-row static-gross ladder control; 3 of 3 reproduction checks exact (run() vs engine.backtest 0.00e+00; idea 94's published EWall+vol60-dg u56@10bps 11.587%/1.133/-16.884% vs 11.6/1.133/-16.9; re-parameterised bars 0/306 mismatches vs H.margins). Two tuned parameters, both bars of 4b itself (phi in {0,0.5,0.6,0.7,0.8,0.9,1.0}, delta in {0.5,0.6,0.7}); all 21 grid points reported. (1) **The floor is the SOLE cause of KILL for 48% of every arm that clears 4b's other four bars** — 27 of 56 corpus-wide, 11 of 23 on the (Sharpe, MaxDD) Pareto frontier — and all 11 Pareto-best victims pass 4a. Indicatively 288 of 1289 published leaderboard rows (22.3%) and 5 of its 6 Pareto-best rows sit in the same class. Idea 95's `theta=0.40/dg` was not an accident. (2) **The floor's victims are out-of-sample BETTER, not worse.** Screened on the IS window alone and read once OOS, floor-only rejects (n=9) score OOS Sharpe 1.160 vs 1.056 admitted (+0.104, Welch t +3.22) and OOS MaxDD -13.4% vs -21.5% (8.1pp shallower, t +8.97), losing only on CAGR (9.1% vs 13.4%, t -7.12) — the axis the floor screens on. Paired by cell: 3/4 on Sharpe, 4/4 on drawdown. (3) **Raising the floor costs drawdown and buys nothing.** phi 0 -> 0.70 at delta=0.60 removes 27 of 56 admissions and moves mean admitted OOS Sharpe by +0.002 while making mean admitted OOS drawdown 1.9pp deeper (-16.6% -> -18.5%); the floor and the drawdown cap pull against each other, and delta is by far the stronger dial (3/29/57 passes at delta 0.50/0.60/0.70). (4) **Rule 8 (three selectors pre-registered before any OOS read): the floor is INERT in selection.** S0 (no screen), S1 (IS 4b screen with the floor) and S2 (floor deleted) pick the identical arm in all 7 of 18 cells where the screens pick — 0 of 18 picks changed — and S1/S2 empty the same 11 cells; S1 OOS 12.7%/1.022/-21.1% vs SPY 0.882/-33.7%, ungated control 0.762, RULES v1 0.451 (beats SPY 5/7, v1 7/7, own control 2/7). The screen's apparent gain over S0 is entirely the cells where it declines to pick, not better picking. This reproduces idea 109 (which moved the pick in 8/44 and never for the better) on a disjoint arm family. (5) **The floor is NOT broken and must not be deleted:** on the static-gross ladder it is the sole KILL in 97 of 342 rows and bites only at gross <= 0.80, exactly its designed job. Its defect is that it cannot distinguish a de-grossed lever from a better-shaped defensive book — the victims sit at 0.57 mean gross yet carry higher OOS Sharpe than the admitted set at 0.74. So the floor binds maximally at ADOPTION and not at all in SELECTION; that asymmetry is the finding. **Memo asks the Sunday review for a PROTOCOL rule-4b reporting clause only — an arm clearing the halves bars, the OOS bar and the drawdown cap and failing ONLY the CAGR floor is recorded `4b-defensive`, not KILL, and must state its CAGR shortfall in pp and its mean gross. No bar is moved (rule 7 stands), no RULES change, and the standing 4b candidate `EWall + vol60-dg` is untouched.** Caveats carried: group B is 9 correlated arm-rows in 4 cells, all EWall + a slow trend gate (so the class may be one construction in disguise — filed as idea 133); survivorship (idea 54) runs against the floor's defence; idea 128's shallow IS window biases the screen toward over-admission; idea 38's calendar-day index unfixed on u56/broad; every number t+1 only (idea 126). Follow-ups filed: 131 (replace the CAGR floor with an explicit minimum-gross bar), 132 (why the IS 4b screen changes no pick), 133 (is the defensive class one book in disguise). Script `research/backtests/2026-09-05_cagr-floor-calibration_B.py`, memo `.memo.md`, result `.result.md`.

- 2026-09-05 (cloud, idea 127 four-way-gate-spread-is-a-weighting-convention) — **ANSWERED; KILL of the QUEUE's premise. The four-way gate spread's MAGNITUDE is the ranking signal, not the weighting convention. Rules unchanged; a PROTOCOL reporting clause proposed.** The two rows the queue compares differ in TWO things, not one: ideas 38/56 rank on raw 12-1 momentum and weight literally (`(rank<=n)*g/n`, cash for missing slots), idea 121 ranks on the RULES v1 composite — which already contains a 0.5/1.0 trend factor and a /sqrt(vol20) scaler, i.e. internalises the gates being priced — and renormalises to g. Both reproduce here before anything new was read (38/56 max|diff| **0.0034**, 121 **0.0000**; engine-equivalence 6.9e-18/6.9e-18/1.4e-17; idea 38's calendar-index warning re-checked, 0 weekend rows on all three panels). 1440 arm-rows = 3 panels x 3 signals x 4 gates x 3 conventions x (R10/R20/R40 + EWall) x 2 gross x 2 cost rungs, all reported; exactly two tuned parameters (n, g). **(1) The attribution is decisive**: at a FIXED mom signal the matched spread is **0.362** against the literal **0.355** (+0.007), so the convention explains **-3%** of the published 0.356->0.080 gap and the signal explains **103%** (-0.287), split about half the composite blend (0.362->0.225) and half the vol scaler (0.225->0.079) — idea 81's finding, now on the gate rows. **(2) The convention is exposure TIMING, not level.** For a ranked book the two conventions hold the identical names, so literal = matched x f_t; splitting off a static-gross control gives |timing| median 0.0043 vs |level| median 0.0000 over 480 arms (timing dominates 86%). It is nil on the ranked small books (f=0.99) and large on equal-weight books, where the gate really de-grosses: small EWall+both f=0.419 (0.209 inside SPY drawdowns >10%), literal costs 2.0pp of CAGR and buys **28.1pp of MaxDD** vs matched. **(3) The census answers the queue's question in the opposite direction.** 0 of 44 published gate-claim cells depend on the literal convention (no sign flip, none halves) — 'vol60 is the larger destroyer' is -0.272 literal / -0.285 matched, ideas 49/51's equal-weight gate cost -8.5pp / -6.5pp CAGR. But in **8 of 44** cells the MATCHED value is more than twice the literal one (max **7.03x**), all on u56: d_both -0.020 lit vs **-0.091** mat at n=40, -0.011 vs -0.075 for EWall, and on u56 OOS the literal convention **inverts the ordering** (tau -0.67, every gated book beating ungated, vs 0.00 matched). A u56 gate row quoted literally is a statement about cash, not about the gate. **(4) Rule 8**: 216 cells, (n,g) on IS Sharpe only, OOS read once — 102 beat SPY's 0.882, 171 beat RULES v1, 17 pass 4b, 0 pass 4a; the convention is worth <=0.036 of mean OOS Sharpe on any panel. 0 of 480 small-panel rows pass 4b (idea 121 reproduced). **(5)** Exactly one arm passes 4b in all four (u56/broad x 10/25 bps) cells — `EWall / vol60 / literal / g=0.75`, the standing incumbent `EWall+vol60-dg`, confirmed by a run that was not looking for it. All five pre-registered predictions HELD. Survivorship restated: three current-constituent panels, no level here is an achievable return.
- 2026-09-05 (lane C, idea 123 relative-priceability-floor) — **ANSWERED; the relative floor `dMaxDD >= 10% of the control's |MaxDD|` is proposed as a REPORT-ONLY PROTOCOL clause (exact wording in `2026-09-05_relative-priceability-floor_C.memo.md`) and is a KILL as a selector.** Reproduction first: idea 94's engine-equivalence max|diff| 0.0, idea 97's committed `pricelist.csv` 288/288 rows to 4e-16 and its `grid.csv` 306/306 arm-points bit-identical including both KEEP flags. (1) The re-tabulation: of the 202 published full-window prices, REL(0.10) unprices **53 (26.2%)** — u56 6/70, broad 16/68, small 31/64; by book V1u 42.7% / TOP20 30.1% / EWall 18.2% (the same concentrated-book address idea 122 found for the sign test); by tier the **per-name stop loses 21 of its 23 published rows (91.3%)**, the gate tier 32.7%, the book-level DD rule 4.6%. All five φ reported (0.02/0.05/0.10/0.20 remove 8.9/15.3/26.2/50.5%). (2) The case FOR the clause: the rows it removes keep their IS→OOS price **sign only 49.1% of the time (median |Δrate| 2.701) against 88.4% (0.891) for the rows it keeps** — it separates measurements from coin flips, which idea 94's absolute 0.10 pp floor cannot. (3) Two queue premises REFUTED. A negative price is NOT a shallow-denominator artefact off the small panel: 48 of 76 negatives removed (63.2% against an 80% bar); idea 119's own `small/V1u` cell is 11 of 11, but u56 is 1 of 9 full and 5 of 16 OOS with survivors buying 7–29% of the control's drawdown, and ρ(|rate|, denominator size) is +0.392 u56 / +0.143 broad / −0.412 small. And the floor does not rescue idea 97's tier sentence — it **weakens** it: clause-rows true C1 33/54→24/54 (every loss a V1u cell; small-panel C1 2/6→0/6, median small gate price 0.350→0.551 against a 0.279 lever), C2 unchanged 38/54, exact 4-tier order 20/54→16/54, and C3's 50/54→52/52 is true only because the stop becomes unpriceable and idea 97's convention ranks an unpriceable tier last. (4) Rule 8 (eligibility on 2009/2011–2016, 2017–2026 untouched): Srel(0.10) changes 2 of 18 picks and loses **0.019 of mean OOS Sharpe (0.710 vs idea 94's S1 0.729)** with regret 0.441→0.602; φ=0.02/0.05 change nothing; φ=0.20 leaves 4 cells with no eligible arm (0.625). No selector beats the no-instrument control (0.762) or SPY (0.882/15.45%/−33.7%); RULES v1 OOS Sharpe 0.747/0.576/0.581. Same report-only verdict idea 122 reached on the sign test, by an independent screen. (5) KEEP paths: all 306 arm-points carry idea 97's flags unchanged (97 pass 4a, 29 pass 4b, every one an already-published gated `TOP20`/`EWall` arm) — **no new KEEP candidate**; this run tests a publication rule, not a book. Survivorship: current-constituent panels, worst on the small list, so its gate prices are flattered; the calendar-day index (idea 38) is unfixed for u56/broad and cancels inside every cell.


- 2026-09-05 (cloud, idea 133 is-the-defensive-class-one-book-in-disguise) — **ANSWERED; the `4b-defensive` class is NOT one book in disguise, and it is not an exposure band either. PROTOCOL should name the CLASS, not the book. Rules unchanged.** Idea 129's census machinery (`bars_win`, `margins_at`, `fails`, `pareto_front`) and idea 94's simulator are IMPORTED, so this is literally the same census on a wider corpus: 3 panels x 9 books (EWall, V1u, R5/R10/R20/R40, S3-25/S3-50/S4-50) at MATCHED gross 0.75 x idea 94's 17 overlays x 2 cost rungs = **816 arm-rows**, plus a 240-row static-gross ladder control; two tuned parameters (n, sleeve fraction f), all values reported. Checks before any new number: engine-equivalence **0.000e+00** on all 24 ungated books, and all **306** of idea 129's rows reproduce arm-for-arm (max|diff| Sharpe **2.2e-16**). **(0) The premise was already an approximation of its own source**: of idea 129's 11 Pareto-best floor-only KILLs, 10 are EWall+gate+dg and the 11th is `broad/EWall/25bps/ddctl-8/.5/recover` at 0.661 gross; the wider floor-only set is 26/27 EWall but only 16 dg, with 2 rw and one TOP20. **(1) On the widened corpus the class is 138 of 816 rows across 6 books and 61 distinct (book, arm) constructions — 112 of them NOT EWall, 122 not 'EWall+gate+dg', 28 matched-gross.** **(2) The existence proof needs no overlay at all**: `S3-50/control` and `S4-50/control` — the ranked top-20 book blended 50/50 with a momentum x risk-parity sleeve of (TLT, GLD, UUP[, DBC]), at full matched gross 0.750 — are 4b-defensive on BOTH large-cap panels: u56 10.1%/**1.276**/-12.2% (OOS 1.241) and 10.4%/1.259/-13.1%, broad 9.9%/1.108/-13.1%, each killed by a CAGR shortfall of **0.3-0.8 pp/yr** while holding about a third of SPY's drawdown (SPY 15.2%/0.889/-33.7%). **(3) The Pareto-best members are the sleeve, not EWall**: of 17 class members on their cell's (Sharpe, MaxDD) frontier, S3-50 10 / EWall 4 / S4-50 3; best is u56/S3-50/ebud-0.10 at 9.9%/**1.291**/-11.1%, OOS 1.277. Idea 129's 11 EWall rows survive as the subset a corpus with only one diversified book could see. **(4) The '~53% gross' reading dies**: the static-gross ladder puts 88 of 240 rows into the class spanning ALL EIGHT books at gross 0.297-0.750, so low gross is SUFFICIENT to enter the class but NOT NECESSARY — 51 of 138 real members sit at full gross >= 0.74. The class is defined by the floor, not by a construction. **(5) Rule 8 (S0/S1/S2 fixed before any OOS read): the floor is no longer inert.** S1 != S2 in **3 of 6** cells, breaking idea 129's 0-of-18; mean OOS 11.6%/0.939/-21.8% (S0), 12.4%/1.111/-17.3% (S1), 10.1%/**1.124**/**-13.5%** (S2) — deleting the floor buys +0.013 of OOS Sharpe and 3.8pp of OOS drawdown for 2.3pp of OOS CAGR, and 3 of 4 S2 picks are themselves defensive. Both screens admit NOTHING on the small panel. **(6) No new KEEP**: 88 of 816 rows pass 4b but only two constructions pass in all four (u56/broad x 10/25 bps) cells — the incumbent `EWall+vol60-dg` and `EWall+band3-rw` — so six new books produced no new cross-cell passer. Predictions: P1 HELD, P2 **FAILED** (members at full gross), P3 **FAILED** (sleeve majority), P4 HELD, P5 HELD. Proposed clause: a `4b-defensive` record must state its CAGR shortfall in pp AND its mean realised gross, because a full-gross diversified book and a 0.52-gross trend book are different objects. Survivorship restated: three current-constituent panels; sleeve statements are two-panel statements; no level here is an achievable return.
- 2026-09-05 (lane B, idea 133 is-the-defensive-class-one-book-in-disguise) — **INDEPENDENT SECOND RUN. Lane B and the cloud lane ran idea 133 concurrently without seeing each other's work and reached the SAME verdict on disjoint corpora (2244 rows / 8 books / 3 gross modes here, 816 rows / 9 books / matched-0.75 there), including the same two cross-cell 4b survivors (`EWall/vol60-dg`, `EWall/band3-rw`) and the same finding that the class's Pareto front is sleeve books rather than EWall. Treat the two as a replication, not a duplicate. ANSWERED; KILL of the premise. Idea 129's `4b-defensive` class is a CLASS, not a book, and the construction idea 129 named is a mediocre member of it. Rules unchanged; no new KEEP candidate; the standing 4b candidate `EWall + vol60-dg` reproduces exactly and is untouched.** Idea 94's simulator and idea 102's sleeve construction were IMPORTED, not re-implemented, and five reproduction checks ran before anything new was read: `book_targets` vs `H.targets` **0.000e+00** over 3 books x 6 gates x 2 conventions, `H.run` vs `engine.backtest` **0.000e+00**, idea 94's published `EWall+vol60-dg` u56@10bps **11.587%/1.133/-16.884%**, and idea 129's **entire 306-row grid re-run inside this corpus at 306/306 matched, max|d Sharpe| 2.2e-16, 0/306 `floor_only` and 0/306 `pass4b` mismatches**. Corpus 2244 rows = 3 panels (u56, broad, small) x 8 books (V1u, TOP5/10/20/40, EWall, SLV25, SLV50) x 17 arms x 2 cost rungs x 3 gross modes, all reported; exactly two tuned parameters (n in {5,10,20,40}, f in {0.25,0.50}), with gross mode a pre-registered reported convention (native / mean gross forced to 0.53 / forced to 0.75), achieved 0.529-0.530 and 0.742-0.751. **(1) H_one is refuted decisively.** The class has **531 members across SIX of eight books**; **37 (7.0%) are `EWall + slow trend gate + de-gross` and 494 (93.0%) are not**; 431 of 531 also pass 4a. **(2) The claim idea 129 actually made — about the (Sharpe, MaxDD) Pareto front — inverts.** Within-cell (idea 129's convention) 118 of 138 floor-only Pareto rows are not EWall; across ALL books within (panel, cost, gross), the frontier the question needs, the front is **47 rows of which `EWall+slow-gate-dg` holds 2, ranking 27th and 28th by Sharpe**. The top 26 are macro-sleeve books: `broad/SLV50/ebud-0.10` 8.7%/**1.292**/-13.6% (OOS **1.311**), `broad/SLV50/vol60-dg` 7.6%/1.277/-10.6% (OOS **1.338**), `u56/SLV25/band3-dg` 7.2%/1.253/-9.6% (OOS **1.369**) — all at native gross 0.59-0.74, i.e. not de-grossed books at all. Class means by book at native gross: SLV50 OOS 1.255, SLV25 1.164, TOP40 1.154, **EWall 1.112**, TOP20 1.034; corpus-wide sleeve members OOS 1.208 (n=325) vs EWall 1.102 (n=96) vs ranked 1.066 (n=110). **(3) Gross does real work but is not the whole story.** Forcing 0.53 mean gross moves TOP20 from 1 member to 34 and TOP40 from 10 to 52 (class 139 -> 278), so de-grossing a ranked book MANUFACTURES membership — idea 129's own warning, now measured. But `V1u` and `TOP5` join at **no** gross level (0 of 276 rows each; they fail the halves and OOS bars too) and `SLV25`/`SLV50` are members at **every** level (73.5/97.1/76.5% and 76.5/76.5/77.9% at native/m53/m75), while class members average MaxDD -13.3% at Sharpe 1.12 against non-members' -25.2% at 0.76. **(4) Rule 8 (four selectors fixed in writing before any OOS read; 18 cells pooling all books): the class is prospectively SELECTABLE, and its picks are not one construction.** S3 — argmax IS Sharpe among IS-window `4b-defensive` arms — gives OOS **9.1%/1.233/-14.3%** and the best mean OOS rank (**15.9 of 136**), against S0 no-screen 12.5%/0.958/-23.7% (41.6), idea 129's S1 floor-kept 13.0%/1.069/-20.9% (52.9) and S2 floor-deleted 10.3%/1.192/-16.6% (26.0); S3 beats SPY 12/12, RULES v1 12/12 and its own control 11/12 where it picks. SPY OOS 15.45%/0.882/-33.72%, RULES v1 OOS 4.86%/0.451/-25.30%, ungated EWall control OOS 0.952. **S3's 12 picks: SLV50 x7, SLV25 x3, TOP20 x1, TOP40 x1, EWall x0.** All six small-panel cells are empty for S1/S2/S3; the small panel contributes **0 of 531** class members at any gross level. **(5) Both KEEP paths on all 2244 rows: 4a 1053, 4b 144.** The only arms passing 4b in all four (u56/broad x 10/25 bps) cells at native gross are `EWall/band3-rw` and `EWall/vol60-dg`, **both already in idea 129's grid** — this run confirms them and discovers nothing new.
