The best sleeve arms are not candidates: they miss the CAGR floor by 2.0-5.5 pp/yr, which is what puts them in the class. **Proposed to the Sunday review (no file modified):** adopt idea 129's PROTOCOL rule-4b reporting clause with its "may be one construction in disguise" caveat **deleted as tested and false**, and with **mean gross AND book construction** made mandatory on every `4b-defensive` row (exact wording in the memo); no bar is moved, rule 7 stands, RULES v1 and the standing candidate are untouched. **Caveats carried:** the sleeve books inherit every open question about the sleeve (idea 100 is a PARK, idea 102 found GLD carries 53% of its return, ideas 105/106 open) and nothing here argues for adopting one; class members earn 7-9% CAGR against SPY's 15.4% and are defensive sleeves, not capital-worthy books; survivorship (idea 54) runs TOWARD this finding since absent delistings inflate the fully-invested controls most; idea 128's shallow IS window biases every IS screen toward over-admission; idea 38's calendar-day index cancels inside each cell; idea 126 — t+1 only; a matched-gross row is a different instrument from its native row and is never quoted as one. Script `research/backtests/2026-09-05_defensive-class-census_B.py`, result `.result.md`, memo `.memo.md`, grid `.grid.csv` (2244 rows), walk-forward `.walkforward.csv` (72 rows), console `.console.txt`.
- 2026-09-05 (lane C, idea 128 shallow-window-prices-are-not-prices) — **ANSWERED; DOUBLE KILL, no RULES change and no new KEEP candidate.** Idea 94's harness imported unchanged (engine-equivalence **0.000e+00** on both panels) and idea 122's published table reproduces to the row: 192 arm-rows, 138 priceable, dMaxDD_IS ≤ 0 in **40**, dMaxDD_OOS ≤ 0 in **0**, 4a **54**/192, 4b **29**/192. **(1) The queue's premise is a TAUTOLOGY, not a measurement.** `published` conditions on dMaxDD_full > 0.10 pp, and **103 of 104 book troughs (and SPY's) lie inside 2017-2026**, so `dMaxDD_full == dMaxDD_OOS` **exactly in 132 of the 138 published rows** (corr 0.998, against 0.755 for the IS half). Filtering on the full-sample denominator *is* filtering on the OOS denominator; the 0-of-138 is arithmetic. Unconditionally at 10 bps over all 96 arm-rows: dMaxDD > 0 in **58 IS / 71 OOS / 71 full** — the real gap is 60% vs 74%, and "the 2009-2016 window cannot measure a drawdown instrument at all" should be withdrawn. **(2) The 90% depth threshold DOES NOT EXIST.** 1,640 rolling windows (L ∈ {2,3,4,5,6} y × monthly starts × 2 universes), each scored by its own SPY MaxDD against the share of that panel's 51 arms with dMaxDD > 0. The relation is positive — pooled L=4 slope **+0.0051/pp, R² 0.13, deciles 0.63/0.60/0.54/0.51/0.63/0.67/0.57/0.70/0.68/0.69 over depth 9.7→33.7 pp — but its significance is an overlap illusion: **t +7.07 on all 328 overlapping windows and t +0.04 on the 7 disjoint ones** (t_disj > 2 in 1 of 15 fits, n=3). And it never gets there: **max frac over all 1,640 windows is 0.833**, 0 of 328 L=4 windows reach 0.90, 1.5% reach 0.80, and the **empirical crossing is undefined at 45 of 45 grid points**; the fitted L=4/p\*=0.90 crossing is **77.5 pp** of SPY drawdown pooled (u56 57.2, broad 144.8), deeper than 2008's −55%, and 44.9–65.3 pp even at p\*=0.80. **Length does not substitute for depth** (matched 28–35 pp band: 0.661 → 0.708 as L goes 2 → 6 y); rule 8's own 9.7-year OOS half scores 0.750/0.729 and its 8-year IS half 0.583/0.625. Per book at 22.1 pp depth the fitted frac is **V1u 0.451 / EWall 0.682 / TOP20 0.720** — the 5-name book is unpriceable at every depth, idea 122's own address for its cost- and panel-axis failures. **(3) Rule 8** (selection on 2009–2016 only, 2017–2026 untouched): S0 hold-the-control **0.937 OOS Sharpe / 12.04% / −22.5%**, S1 idea 94's selector on the full IS half **0.885 / 9.92% / −19.0%**, S2 the same selector on the deepest IS sub-window **0.885 / 9.16% / −18.3%** at L=4 (0.838–0.885 across L) — against SPY **0.882 / 15.45% / −33.7%** and RULES v1 **0.747 / 7.73% / −13.8%**. The depth clause changes **13 of 30** picks and buys nothing; no selector beats doing nothing. The mechanism: the deepest IS sub-window has depth **22.062 pp at every L from 2 to 6 years**, identical to the whole IS half, because SPY's 2009–2016 maximum is the post-warm-up tail of the GFC — inside rule 8's IS window a deeper ruler cannot be bought at any length, which also bounds what idea 111's re-derived split date can achieve. **(4) KEEP paths:** all 192 arm-points carry idea 122's flags unchanged (4a 54, 4b 29, every passer an already-published gated `TOP20`/`EWall` arm); this run tests a publication rule, not a book. Proposed PROTOCOL rule-4 clause is **negative** (window depth does not rescue a drawdown denominator; never quote an IS price as if it were the OOS measurement) — exact wording in `research/backtests/2026-09-05_shallow-window-prices-are-not-prices_C.memo.md`. Survivorship: current-constituent panels, so levels are optimistic and a survivorship-free panel would deepen every window — moving the crossing, not the direction. Index: with BTC/ETH excluded both panels load on a **trading-day** index (3,679 one-day / 846 three-day gaps), so idea 38's calendar-day defect does not bite this run. NOTE: the queue contains **two ideas numbered 128** (the other is `threshold-plateaus-are-the-general-case`, still Open) — needs renumbering by whoever owns the file. Result: `research/backtests/2026-09-05_shallow-window-prices-are-not-prices_C.result.md`.

- 2026-09-05 — Research cloud (idea 134, sleeve-f-that-clears-the-floor): **ANSWERED — YES, the floor is one dial away, but PARK not KEEP.** Idea 133's harness and idea 94's simulator were imported rather than re-implemented: engine-equivalence `max|diff| = 0.0` on all 13 ungated books, and this run's 136 f=0.50 rows reproduce idea 133's arm-for-arm to 2.2e-16 before anything else was read. The premise audit corrects the queue in two places: `SLV50` is not a book name (the f=0.50 sleeve books are `S3-50`/`S4-50`), and the '2.0-3.0 pp/yr' floor shortfall is the tail — over idea 133's 101 sleeve class-members it runs -4.10/-1.29/-0.06 pp/yr (min/median/max). 2 panels x 13 books x 17 arms x 2 cost rungs = **884 arm-rows plus a 260-row static-gross ladder, all reported**; TWO tuned parameters (sleeve fraction f, 6 values all run; sleeve asset set S3 vs S4, both run). **(1) The answer is yes.** 4b passes by f on broad@10bps are 0/0/5/9/16/22/4 for f=0.00/.05/.10/.15/.20/.25/.50 and on u56@10bps 9/16/23/27/29/28/3; 250 of the 680 swept `f<=0.25` sleeve rows pass, 272 of 884 overall. The count peaks at **f=0.15-0.20**, not at the bottom of the sweep (prediction P2 FAILED). **(2) What f buys is an exchange rate.** The mean CAGR-floor margin falls monotonically +1.89 -> +0.08 -> -1.88 pp/yr and the mean drawdown-cap margin rises monotonically -1.11 -> +3.52 -> +7.84 pp as f goes 0.00 -> 0.25 -> 0.50: about **1 pp of CAGR margin per 2.5 pp of drawdown margin** over 0.00-0.25. Below f=0.10 the cap binds, above f=0.25 the floor does (fail_CAGR 11 -> 129 rows). **(3) The ladder control splits the panels, and this is the run's real finding.** On u56 f is just the exposure dial in disguise — `R20` alone passes 4b at m=0.80. On **broad@10bps the ladder can never pass**: `R20` fails the CAGR floor at m=0.60 and the drawdown cap at every m from 0.80 to 1.40, with Sharpe flat at 0.973-0.978 across the whole ladder (idea 66 again), while a sleeve at f>=0.10 does pass. The sleeve **moves the (CAGR, MaxDD) frontier**; gross only slides along it — the first instrument the project has found that is not a point on the gross ladder (new idea 139). **(4) The wall.** broad@25bps admits **0 of 442 arm-rows and 0 of 130 ladder points** at any f; exactly one row clears the halves, the OOS bar and the cap and fails only the floor (`S4-50/band3-rw`, by 0.99 pp/yr), every other failing a Sharpe bar. Idea 82's ~7.5-10.5 bps breakeven reappears as a hard edge (new idea 137). **(5) Best member** `S4-25/band3-rw` (0.75 x ranked top-20 + 0.25 x momentum-vote x risk-parity over TLT/GLD/DBC/UUP, 3% band, matched gross) passes 4b on 3 of 4 cells — u56@10 13.0%/1.192/-16.9% (halves 1.214/1.181, OOS 1.235), u56@25 11.6%/1.070/-17.1%, broad@10 13.3%/1.055/-17.1% (also 4a) — and dies broad@25 on H2 0.791 and OOS 0.861, not on the floor. 35 (book, arm) pairs pass 3 of 4 cells; **0 pass 4**. **(6) Rule 8**: parameters chosen on 2009-2016, OOS 2017-2026 read once. S1 beats the ungated control, SPY (OOS 0.882) and RULES v1 in 4 of 4 cells (OOS Sharpe 1.047/0.891/1.202/1.086) but mean OOS Sharpe is S0 1.065 / S1 1.057 / S2 1.065 — **the IS 4b screen is worth -0.008**, and S1 != S2 in 4 of 4 cells against idea 129's 0 of 18. **PARK**: cost-fragile on the panel that matters, blocked on ideas 105/106 for any 'macro sleeve' RULES wording, and biased in its own favour by survivorship (absent delistings inflate the equity leg more than the ETF sleeve, i.e. exactly in the direction that produced this answer). Rules unchanged. Memo carries the exact wording the Sunday review would need if 105/106 clear it. New ideas 137, 138, 139.

- 2026-09-05 — Research cloud (idea 132, why-the-IS-4b-screen-changes-no-pick): **ANSWERED — explanation (A), and a KILL of PROTOCOL rule 8's IS 4b screen as a SELECTOR. It survives only as an ABSTENTION rule. Rules unchanged, no new book KEEP.** Idea 129's corpus was re-derived rather than read: **306 of 306 rows reproduce to max|diff| 2.2e-16** across CAGR/Sharpe/MaxDD/IS and OOS metrics/gross, with engine-equivalence 0.0 on all three panels, before any new number was looked at; the screen under test is literally the code that produced the result under test. TWO tuned parameters (the IS selector statistic, 4 values all run: IS Sharpe, IS Calmar, IS MaxDD, IS CAGR; and the screen, 3 values: none / 4b-with-floor / 4b-floor-deleted) = **216 picks, all reported**. **(1) The premise reproduces**: IS-Sharpe/S1 picks in 7 of 18 cells, declines in 11, and moves the arm in 0. **(2) (A) wins, decisively.** Across all selectors and both screens there are **12 moved picks and all 12 come from non-Sharpe selectors**: moves-where-it-picks are IS-Sharpe 0, IS-Calmar 0, IS-CAGR 3, IS-MaxDD 5; the unscreened argmax is already admissible in **100%** of non-empty cells for IS-Sharpe and IS-Calmar against 57% for IS-CAGR and 29% for IS-MaxDD. The refinement the queue did not anticipate is that the immunity is **not Sharpe's alone but the risk-adjusted-return selectors' as a class** (new idea 141). **(3) What the screen removes is a cell, not an arm.** Median admitted per cell is **0 of 17** and the set is non-empty in only **7 of 18** cells: it admits nothing in all 6 small-panel cells, all 4 V1u cells and u56/TOP20@25bps, and inside the 7 it does admit it admits 1-12 arms and always the one the selector wanted. **(4) The apparent OOS gain is abstention, and the number is exactly zero.** On the SAME 216 picks, S1 - S0 in mean OOS Sharpe reads **+0.327** the way idea 129 read it (averaged over the cells the screen entered), **+0.055** with an explicit fallback to the cell's ungated control in the 11 cells it declines, and **+0.000** paired on the 7 cells where every screen picks — exactly zero for IS-Sharpe and IS-Calmar because it is the same arm and therefore the same return series (K_Calmar +0.311/+0.028/+0.000, K_MaxDD +0.412/+0.129/-0.012, K_CAGR +0.274/-0.012/-0.001). **(5) The CAGR floor is inert**: S1 and S2 pick the identical arm in every cell for 3 of the 4 selectors. **(6) Rule 8 was read once** on 2017-2026: 53 distinct picked arms, **18 pass 4a, 7 pass 4b full-sample and the same 7 pass 4b re-measured on the OOS window alone**, every one a pre-existing leaderboard book; picks beat SPY (OOS 0.882) in 6 of 18 cells and RULES v1 in 12 of 18. **(7) Reported as found, not acted on:** the incumbent IS-Sharpe is the **worst** of the four selectors on the paired cells (1.022 vs IS-MaxDD 1.052, IS-CAGR 1.047, IS-Calmar 1.040) — 7 cells cannot order four selectors, which is new idea 142. **Proposed to PROTOCOL rule 8 as a REPORTING clause** (no selector change, no book change): every screened walk-forward must report admitted/total arms per cell, whether the unscreened argmax was already admissible, and the screen's OOS advantage paired or under an explicit fallback — an advantage averaged only over the cells a screen chose to enter must not be quoted. Caveats carried: survivorship on all three current-constituent panels (idea 54), idea 128's shallow IS window (which biases the screen toward admitting too much, i.e. against this run's own P3, which held anyway), t+1 execution only (idea 126). New ideas 140, 141, 142.
- 2026-09-05 (research sprint lane B, idea 131) — **KILL of the gross-level swap.** Idea 129 closed by diagnosing 4b's CAGR floor as "really a gross-level filter"; tested directly on the same 306-row corpus and 342-row ladder, that is false. **No gamma does both jobs (0 of 34 grid points):** admitting all 27 floor-only victims (11/11 Pareto-best) needs gamma<=0.51, where 23 static-gross ladder points come in against the CAGR floor's 10; emptying the ladder needs gamma>=0.68, where none of the 11 survive. The families overlap on mean gross (victims 0.519-0.750, core-admissible ladder 0.075-0.673) and the axis is perverse inside the overlap (0.53->0.54 discards 6 Pareto books and 0 ladder points). Spearman(gross, CAGR) is +0.245 on real books but +0.691 on the ladder, i.e. the substitutability holds only where the two are the same dial. Rule 8: the gross bar is a third inert screen — S3==S1==S2==S0 at 1.022 OOS Sharpe paired, 0 of 7 picks moved at every gamma<=0.65. Two things survive: gamma in [0.57,0.61] weakly dominates the published floor on all three of the floor's own criteria, and a post-hoc diagnostic (cv of daily gross: victims 0.268 vs ladder 0.014, 11/11 Pareto-best above every ladder point) separates where the level cannot — queued as idea 143, deliberately not adopted in the run that found it. Rules unchanged; a negative PROTOCOL reporting clause is proposed for Sunday review. Memo: `research/backtests/2026-09-05_gross-as-the-missing-third-bar_B.memo.md`.

- 2026-09-05 (lane B, idea 93 absorbing-state-audit-of-every-state-dependent-rule) — **ANSWERED: the absorbing pathology is CONFIRMED and made exact, its DIAGNOSIS IS CORRECTED, and the fix idea 93 proposed is a KILL as an improvement.** 3 books (RULES v1 as live, idea 2's `CAND20`, idea 72's `EWall`) x 2 universes x 2 cost rungs x two instrument families: `DDCTL` (idea 22/40's book-level drawdown control, 5 reset arms x D in {4,6,8,10,12,15}% x k in {0,0.25,0.50,0.75} + control = 1452 points) and `STOP` (idea 9's per-name trailing stop x 4 re-entry arms x idea 75's 2 arming arms x s in {10,15,20,25}% + control = 396 points), plus a 228-point static-gross ladder — **all reported**. Exactly two tuned parameters (D, k; s alone in STOP); reset/re-entry/arming are ARMS and every rule-8 selection is confined inside one arm. Harness sanity: both state-machine controls reproduce `engine.backtest` at max|diff| **0.0e+00** in all 12 cells and reproduce the live book and idea 2's standing candidate to the decimal. **(1) The absorbing state is exact, not a tendency:** at k=0.00 with equity-conditioned re-entry (`high` or `recover`), **140 of 140 arms that ever armed never released**, mean capture **724 rebalances** (max 919 = the entire remaining sample) — the cut is what freezes the equity the release condition reads, so the rule fires once and the book is in cash for life. The 4 exceptions never armed. At k>0 episode length scales as ~1/k (`high` 724/218/109/69 at k=0.00/0.25/0.50/0.75). **(2) The diagnosis in idea 22's wording is WRONG and this run says so on its own pre-registered prediction.** I predicted a per-name stop reading the NAME's price would be safe because the name keeps trading; it is not. Release rate / mean block: `free` 0.999 / 2.6 days, `spy200` 0.999 / 35.3, **`nhigh` 0.951 / 250.9** (8.4 names permanently out), **`bookhigh` 0.863 / 285.4**. Both **high-water-mark** arms are near-absorbing and one non-HWM arm is endogenous — so the operative property is *a re-entry condition demanding a state variable regain a running maximum*, not "a function of the book's own equity". Idea 22's wording exonerates `nhigh`, which is the project's second most absorbing rule. **(3) The fix removes the pathology and buys nothing:** exogenous release absorbs **0 of 288** in all three arms, but `spy200`'s mean dSharpe is **-0.2633** against `high`'s **-0.2636** — Sharpe-identical to what it cures — and it makes MaxDD **deeper** in **140 of 288** arms (mean gain -0.0325) because it re-levers as soon as SPY reclaims its 200d, mid-drawdown, at 19.8x/yr turnover vs the control's 15.5x. On idea 74's axis every family is dominated by the plain static-gross lever (0.537 pp/pp) in 67-91% of arms. **(4) Both KEEP paths: 0 of 1824 treated arms pass 4a and 4b together, and 0 of 12 cells has an arm that converts a 4b failure into a 4b pass.** All 69 4b arm-passes sit in the single cell whose control already passes; 20 of the 48 `DDCTL` passes never fired at all and all 28 that fired have negative dSharpe (best -0.0028); in broad/EWall@10bps the control passes and 0 of 152 arms do. 4a's 598 passes are de-grossing against a low-return live book (mean gross 0.629 vs 0.743; only 45 of 598 beat their own control). **(5) Rule 8:** 78 walk-forward selections, params on 2009-2016 only, OOS 2017-2026 read once — S1 moves materially in 65 and **beats its control in 15**, mean OOS Sharpe **-0.171** (DDCTL -0.318, beats SPY 6 of 30; STOP -0.033, beats SPY 24 of 48); the 4b-aware selector picks **NOTHING in 59 of 78**. Idea 75's conditional arming is mildly positive (+0.0328 mean over 192 paired arms) but concentrated on the absorbing arms (`bookhigh` +0.073, `nhigh` +0.045, non-absorbing +0.007) — it helps by firing a broken rule less often. **Two PROTOCOL clauses proposed** in `research/backtests/2026-09-05_absorbing-state-audit_B.memo.md`: prohibit high-water-mark re-entry outright and require every state-dependent arm to report armings/releases/escape rate/terminal state; and forbid quoting de-absorption as an improvement. **No RULES change, no new KEEP, no book proposed.** Footnote for the corpus: idea 9's published stop numbers should be re-read against §4 of the result before being quoted as a stop's numbers rather than a partial-cash book's.
- 2026-09-05 — Research lane C, idea 145 `weak-dominance-bands-as-the-calibration-output`: **ANSWERED — publish the band, as an INDIFFERENCE interval; the idea's own premise is KILLED.** Corpus reproduced exactly from ideas 129/131 (306 arm-rows + 342 ladder rows; four reproduction checks PASS, including one that caught an inverted drawdown comparison in an earlier version of this run). Each of 4b's five bar coefficients swept ONE AT A TIME with the other four pinned, endpoints computed analytically (each bar is monotone in its own coefficient, so 'unbounded' is a proof, not a scan artefact); all grid points reported. Bands: H1 1.00 in [0.9974,1.0453], H2 1.00 in [0.9829,1.0213], OOS 1.00 in **(-inf,1.0520] (unbounded)**, MaxDD cap 0.60 in **[0.5979,0.6012] (width 0.0033)**, CAGR floor 0.70 in [0.6965,0.7020] (width 0.0054) — **three orders of magnitude apart, published point interior in 5 of 5**. (a) The premise dies: **phi has no strictly dominating value in its own units** — idea 131's gamma in [0.57,0.61] was a property of SWAPPING THE STATISTIC (CAGR -> mean gross), not of re-calibrating a coefficient, and sweeping phi the three criteria are strictly opposed at every step. (b) **The drawdown cap, not the CAGR floor, is 4b's largest sole cause of KILL**: 47 rows vs 27, 61.8% vs 48.2% of arms clearing the bar's own other four — ideas 129/131/135 are all framed on the smaller instrument. (c) **The OOS Sharpe bar excludes nothing on this corpus**: 0 sole victims and 0 sole ladder exclusions, and its indifference band is unbounded below, i.e. deleting it changes neither the admitted set nor the leakage (reported, NOT recommended as a deletion on one corpus). (d) Bands are separable only in a neighbourhood: phi's band is identical for every delta>=0.60 and delta's for every phi<=0.80, and outside that both blow up because the joint screen has emptied — so **a wide band can mean an inert bar, and width is meaningless without the sole-victim count** (idea 128's trap, reached from the other side). (e) The dominance test only orders a bar when its criteria are co-monotone: on the Sharpe bars the ladder control excludes nothing, and H2 comes out dominated in BOTH directions, i.e. undetermined. Rule 8: 4 of 4 IS-derived bands contain the published point and overlap the full-sample band (DD and CAGR identical to 3dp); **all 12 in-band coefficient sets move 0 of 18 picks** and leave OOS at 12.7%/1.022/-21.1% (SPY 15.45%/0.882/-33.7%; RULES v1 4.77%/0.480/-19.7%; ungated control 1.051) — an argument FOR quoting the band, since the point implies precision the OOS record does not contain. NOBARS moves 11 picks but is identical paired on the 7 entering cells, so the screen is abstention rather than selection for a fifth time, and its picks beat the ungated control only 2/7. Both KEEP paths at every band edge: 4a 97/306, 4b 29/306, both 6/306 — unchanged; only ladder leakage moves (10 -> 9 at three tighter edges). Proposed 4b and rule-8 wording in the memo. Rules unchanged, no book proposed, no KEEP candidate.

- 2026-09-05 (research sprint lane C, idea 153 does-book-share-of-the-panel-price-a-tilt) — **ANSWERED: book share prices a cross-sectional tilt; the key's own t-statistic does not. Idea 81's central puzzle dissolves. Rules unchanged; one KEEP-candidate goes to the Sunday review as a SCOPING clause, not a new book.** Idea 94's window/halves/4a machinery, idea 129's panel and 4b-bar machinery and idea 80's Fama-MacBeth were IMPORTED, and all five pre-registered reproduction checks hold before any new number was read: idea 81's INV-vs-NONE name overlaps come back at **69.4% (u56)** and **42.5% (broad)** to the published digit, its committed grid row reproduces to 5 dp (12.65974%/1.09214/-18.30835%, halves 1.08828/1.10155), and idea 80's bivariate vol20 slopes at **+0.00450 (t +3.90)** and **+0.00294 (t +3.19)**. Corpus: 3 panels x 7 target book shares m x 3 tilts x 2 cost rungs = **126 books**, plus a 63-book gross-normalised control (2 tuned parameters, all 21 grid points per cell reported). **(1) The measurement.** Regressing the tilt's realised magnitude on how much of the eligible panel the book holds: |dSharpe| on name overlap gives b **-0.428 (t -6.60), R2 0.429** on m<=0.53 against **R2 0.028** for the panel's Fama-MacBeth t; on the full grid 0.516 vs 0.010; gross-normalised 0.485 vs 0.009. Within-panel Spearman -0.83 / -0.94 / -0.46. **(2) The puzzle.** dCAGR(POS-NONE) at matched n=20 is +0.49%/yr on u56 vs +2.89% on broad (**5.9x**); at matched share m=0.20 it is **+2.83% vs +2.75% (1.0x)**. Cross-panel overlap spread: **0.425 at matched n, 0.045 at matched share**. Matched-n comparisons across panels of different breadth have been comparing different experiments. **(3) The by-product, zero free parameters.** The standing 4b KEEP holds 0.51-0.53 of u56's eligible list; idea 44 killed the same NAME COUNT on broad (4b H2 0.811 vs SPY 0.834). Carrying the SHARE instead gives broad n=47/49: **11.6-11.7% CAGR / 1.02 Sharpe / -19.0% MaxDD, halves 1.12/0.93, OOS Sharpe 1.03 — 4b PASS on both large-cap panels at 10 bps.** Dead at 25 bps (u56 H1, broad H2+CAGR — idea 137's wall) and dead at every share on the small panel (all five bars — idea 136, third confirm). **(4) The vol scaler, killed a fourth time.** INV (RULES v1's own /sqrt(vol20)) passes 4b **0 of 42**; in the rule-8 walk-forward, allowing a tilt gives mean OOS Sharpe **0.753 vs 0.781** for the no-tilt control (RULES v1 0.451, SPY 0.882). **(5) Stated against the candidate:** an IS-Sharpe chooser picks m=0.05-0.10 and never the m that passes 4b, so the share SWEEP is not walk-forward-selectable — only the inherited-share transfer is, which is why the memo proposes it as a scoping clause on the existing KEEP rather than a new rule. Exact RULES wording, the 25 bps and survivorship conditions and three follow-up ideas (155-157) are in `research/backtests/2026-09-05_does-book-share-price-a-tilt_C.memo.md`; full numbers in the `.result.md` and the grid/overlap/regression/matched/transfer/walkforward CSVs.

- 2026-09-05 (lane C, idea 174 the-sharpe-vs-4b-margin-sign-flip) — **ANSWERED; KILL of the idea's own hypothesis. PROTOCOL rule 4b's CAGR floor and drawdown cap ARE jointly satisfiable on this data and the incumbent (0.70, 0.60) sits comfortably inside the non-empty region: 4b is a screen, not a wall. Rules unchanged; no new KEEP; one REPORT-ONLY PROTOCOL clause proposed.** Reproduction was asserted before any new number was read: `fast_backtest` vs `products/backtester/engine.backtest` at **2.776e-17**, idea 171's **530 of 530** committed GROSS-dial ladder rows re-derived at **2.220e-16**, and idea 171's headline premise re-counted on its own committed file (**CAGR 1223 of 1908, exact**). The two swept parameters are the idea itself — PHI (16 points) x DELTA (20 points) = **320 grid cells, all reported** — and the sweep costs zero extra backtests because pass/fail at any cell is a pure function of `c = CAGR/CAGR_SPY` and `d = |MaxDD|/|MaxDD_SPY|`, computed once per point on a 1590-point corpus (idea 171's 53 books x 10 gross x 3 sleeve, 10 bps, t+1, weekly). **At the incumbent: 173 points clear both coefficients (31 of 53 distinct books) and 151 clear all five bars of 4b; `phi_max(0.60) = 0.9569`, i.e. +0.2569 of headroom before the pair empties.** The three Sharpe bars close only 13 of the 320 cells that the two coefficients leave open. **The pair reduces to ONE number:** because `c` and `d` scale together on the gross ladder, a point satisfies both iff its efficiency ratio `k = c/d >= phi/delta = 1.1667`; median k is 1.376 and **51 of 53 books** clear it. Idea 164 (cloud, same day) derived the identical constant from the other side — "non-empty iff `rho/s <= delta/gamma = 0.857`", and 1/1.1667 = 0.8571 — two independent derivations agreeing to 4 decimals. **Idea 171's premise reproduces and is re-read, which is the run's real content:** the CAGR floor IS the most-violated bar here too (1280 of 1590 violations, sole binding for 1021, against H2 243 / DD 153 / H1 127 / OOS 116), but that is a fact about the LADDER the project sweeps, not about the bar — mean `c ~= 0.856 x gross`, so the floor mechanically needs `g >~ 0.82` and 8 of the 10 gross rungs sit below it (4b pass rate **0.0% at every g <= 0.60**, 10.7-23.9% at g >= 0.70). `Spearman(c, d) = +0.9340` full sample and +0.9166 OOS: return and drawdown are bought together, which is the measured mechanism behind idea 171's Sharpe-vs-margin sign flip. Rule 8, both forms: the region is a **stable object** (IS-window and OOS-window maps agree on emptiness in 306 of 320 cells, `Spearman(n_IS, n_OOS) = +0.9942`), but the (phi, delta) screen **is not a selector** — the IS-chosen pick returns OOS 8.03%/0.9804/-12.93% against a do-nothing control's 5.36%/**1.0553**/-8.96% (**-0.0749**), beating the control in only **3 of 240** non-empty cells; that is the fifth consecutive instance after ideas 110/132/151/166/171 and the screen must not be added to rule 8. Both KEEP paths at the incumbent, all 1590 points: 4a 1430, 4b 151, both 142, passers B136 141 / U56 10 / **SMALL484 zero** (consistent with idea 170); 4a's near-universal pass is de-grossing beating the low-return live book's drawdown, which is PROTOCOL's own stated reason for adding 4b. Per idea 144 every 4b passer is an idea-171 book re-grossed, so **no new KEEP candidate is proposed**. The memo proposes REPORT-ONLY PROTOCOL clause 11 (publish `k` alongside every 4b CAGR-floor or DD-cap failure, and state whether `k >= 1.167`) so that "fails at the gross it was priced at" stops being reported as "fails the bar". Pre-registered predictions scored 6 hits and **1 miss** — P3 predicted a Sharpe bar would bind at the incumbent; the CAGR floor binds, 1280 to 243. Caveat carried, not buried: survivorship (idea 54) biases every `c` up and every `d` down, so the region looks MORE reachable than it is — "satisfiable" is the conservative direction and "a wall" would have been the strong claim. Script `research/backtests/2026-09-05_the-sharpe-vs-4b-margin-sign-flip_C.py`; artefacts `.console.txt`, `.points.csv`, `.region.csv`, `.frontier.csv`, `.walkforward.csv`, `.keep.csv`, `.result.md`, `.memo.md`.
- 2026-09-05 (cloud, idea 173 is-the-ladder-endpoint-a-general-selector-artefact) — **SPLIT: KILL of the QUEUE's generalisation, KEEP of the worry for three of five dials. Rules unchanged; one 4b by-product PARKed.** 234 engine runs -> 468 ladder points (3 panels x 3 signals x 5 dials x 2 cost rungs, the 25 bps rung derived exactly from the 0 bps run and the engine's own turnover series), 90 ladder instances, every point written to `.grid.csv`; exactly ONE tuned parameter per row, with the anchor (n=20, g=0.75, W, max_vol=0.60, p=0.5) deliberately **interior in all five ladders** (rank 3/7, 3/5, 2/4, 3/5, 3/5). **(1) The endpoint signature is not a property of ladders.** Re-pricing a RANDOM pick on the same 468 points against three reference constants: RANDOM beats an **INTERIOR** constant in **0.32** of the 90 instances, a **LOW-end** constant in **0.74**, a HIGH-end one in 0.44. Idea 171's "RANDOM also beats the constant" reproduces only in the low-end column — which is exactly where its f=0 sleeve constant sat. The artefact is (monotone ladder) x (incumbent parked at the far end); the ladder alone manufactures nothing. **(2) The worry is real for three dials.** Spearman(rank, across-book MEAN OOS Sharpe) is **+1.00 for COUNT** (n=5..80: 0.599/0.676/0.720/0.818/0.863), **+1.00 for VOLCAP** (0.30..none: 0.569->0.784) and **-1.00 for VOLPOW** (p=0..1: 0.782->0.684), with the OOS oracle at an endpoint in 0.67/0.72/0.72 against a 0.40 null. On those dials "the argmax is X" reports where the grid stopped. **(3) GROSS is a different and arguably worse pathology: it is FLAT.** Mean OOS Sharpe moves **0.003** over a 3x gross span (0.720 -> 0.721); its IS argmax buys **+0.0002 at t = 0.42**; 67% of cells have |rho| >= 0.9 but in inconsistent directions (mean rho -0.028). GROSS carries **46 of the project's 104 textual `argmax` claims** (COUNT 19, SLEEVE 10, CADENCE 9, VOLCAP 8, VOLPOW 7; 66 of 104 concern a dial re-run here). **(4) CADENCE is the one dial with selectable structure**: oracle **interior in 18 of 18** against a 0.50 null, median IS capture of the oracle **1.000**, IS beats RANDOM in 0.833, RANDOM's own delta **-0.0513 at t -5.33**, monthly the mean-best point (0.831 vs W 0.720, Q 0.649, D 0.476) — independent confirmation of idea 175 on a different corpus and the evidence idea 107 asked for. **(5) Rule 8** (dial chosen <= 2016-12-31, read once on 2017-2026, 90 instances): IS pick 10.30%/**0.7483**/-26.13% vs the do-nothing anchor 8.81%/0.7205/-22.80%, RULES v1 4.39%/0.4229/-26.24%, SPY 15.45%/**0.8820**/-33.72%; selection buys +0.028 and only VOLPOW reaches t = 2.27 (uncorrected, 5 tests). 4a 28/90 (anchor 35), 4b full 18/90 (15), 4b OOS-window 17/90 (15), 15 of the 17 on u56. **(6) By-product, PARK:** u56 R6 top-20 EW g=0.75 **MONTHLY** clears 4b full-sample and OOS-window at both cost rungs (13.61%/1.1557/-18.81%, H1 1.2279 / H2 1.1017; OOS 14.56%/1.1695; 4.8x/yr turnover) but is 1 of 90 unpriced selections and **fails the universe change** — broad 15.95%/1.1298/**-24.51%**, 4.3pp over 4b's drawdown cap. Memo written with exact RULES wording; ideas 182/183/184 queued. Predictions P1, P2, P4 and P5 all FAILED as written and are reported as failures. Survivorship caveat carried on all three panels.
- 2026-09-05 (cloud, idea 181 does-a-null-column-change-any-published-verdict) — **ANSWERED; the proposed null-key clause splits in two: REPORT-ONLY is worth having, SELECTION GATE is a KILL, and VERDICT movement is exactly zero. Rules unchanged; a report-only PROTOCOL clause 11 proposed with its price attached.** 453 engine runs -> 906 rows: 3 panels x (5 real keys VOL/MOM/R6/R3/PRICE + **20 matched null draws**) x 2 directions x 3 tilt strengths + an untilted control per panel, 2 cost rungs derived exactly from the 0 bps run and the engine's own turnover series; exactly two tuned parameters (m, direction); every point in `.grid.csv`. The null draws are verified null rather than assumed: realised monthly rank IC mean +0.0002 / +0.0006 / +0.0007 on u56 / broad / small with |t| > 2 in 2/20, 1/20 and 0/20 draws. **(1) Q2, the question the idea actually asks: the clause moves ZERO verdicts.** Over 3600 real-vs-null swaps in matched cells the KEEP/KILL verdict is identical in **84.3% (4a) / 88.8% (4b full) / 87.7% (4b OOS)**, and **a zero-information key clears 4b MORE often than a real one — 19.9% of 720 null arms against 18.3% of 180 real arms** (4a 33.6% vs 34.4%, 4b-OOS 31.7% vs 28.9%). A KEEP path is decided by the bars and the base book, not by whether the key carries information; the 33 real-key 4b passes are 28 u56, 5 broad, 0 small and are spread over every key and both directions. **(2) Q1, the clause as a REPORTING column, is not bookkeeping.** Only **59 of 180** real-key arms (32.8%) clear `|dSharpe| > max of 20 matched nulls`: PRICE 27/36 (mean |d| 0.2473, null percentile 0.944), VOL 10/36, R6 9/36, R3 8/36, MOM 5/36, and every POS direction except PRICE clears 0 of 3 in every cell. Of **33 real-key 4b passes only 1 (3.0%)** clears the clause, and of 52 4b-OOS passes only 3 (5.8%) — so the clause would flag 32 of 33 tilt-based 4b passes as magnitudes indistinguishable from noise while changing none of them. **(3) Q3: KILL of the clause as a SELECTION GATE.** Rule 8 with the tilt chosen <= 2016-12-31 and read once on 2017-2026: S0 do-nothing 10.12% / 0.7736 / -23.25%, S1 IS-argmax 16.48% / **1.1823** / -22.38%, S2 clause-gated 9.99% / 0.7612 / -24.88%; paired (S1-S0) **+0.4086 winning 6/6** — the first selector in this project to beat the control, after ideas 110/132/151/166/171/174 all found the opposite — while (S2-S0) is **-0.0124 winning 2/6** with S2's pick differing from S1's in 6 of 6. Requiring a tilt to beat its null band removes the arm the selector wanted. **(4) P3 failed in the useful direction: the live `/sqrt(vol20)` scaler is not noise, it is measurably harmful.** VOL/NEG's dSharpe is negative in **all 12** large-cap rows (-0.0600 to -0.3860, monotone in m) and CLEARS the null band in 4/6 u56 and **6/6 broad** rows; ninth independent delete-the-scaler reading and the first with significance attached. On small it clears 0/6 with mixed sign. **(5) By-product, PARKed with a survivorship warning, NOT proposed:** all six S1 picks are PRICE/NEG. It clears 4b in 6/6 u56 rows (u56@10bps m=0.50: 13.59% / 1.1680 / -18.15%, H1 1.1805 / H2 1.1588, OOS 14.55% / 1.2160) but 0/6 on broad (drawdown -21.4%..-22.5% against the -20.23% cap) and 0/6 on small (H1 0.535-0.821 against SPY's 0.891). Share price is a split-history artefact on the large-cap panels and on the small panel a low-price tilt points at exactly the beaten-down cohort a current-constituent panel over-represents — small@10bps m=1.0 returns 20.59% OOS at Sharpe 1.19 from a panel containing no delistings at all. Ideas 185/186 queued. **(6) Census:** 76 of 3315 LEADERBOARD rows rest on a keyed tilt's realised magnitude (2.3%), dominated by sleeve-separation, entry-budget and drawdown-control rows whose construction is not a keyed tilt and which this run therefore does not claim to have re-priced; idea 186 covers them. P1, P2 and P5 held; P3 and P4 failed and are reported as failures. Survivorship caveat carried on all three panels.

- 2026-09-05 (cloud, idea 175 does-cadence-skill-survive-a-second-corpus) — **ANSWERED, SPLIT: the CADENCE dial survives, its SELECTOR does not, and idea 107 should write a constant rather than fit one. Rules unchanged; no new KEEP; a memo with exact RULES wording goes to Sunday review as evidence, not as a proposal.** Idea 171's paired protocol was re-run byte-identically on a corpus DISJOINT from its own — **115 books** (SMALL439 after dropping the 44 `max_1d_move >= 1.0` tickers, U56, ETF36, plus 112 sub-panels drawn from those three; no B136 anywhere) — on a **7-point ladder D/2D/W/2W/M/6W/Q** instead of 4, 805 runs, 10 bps, t+1, IS <= 2016-12-31 and the 2017-2026 window read once. Three reproduction controls asserted before any new number: `cad_mask` == `engine.rebalance_mask` at all four engine-supported points, `fast_backtest` == `engine.backtest` to **1.4e-16** on returns and 1.1e-15 on turnover, and CAND-20 weights identical (**0.0e+00**) to idea 78/171's `weights_cand` on all three fixed panels. **(1) Skill replicates in sign, not in size.** Pooled SEL-SHARPE is **+0.0388 OOS Sharpe (t +3.26, 84W/30L, sign p < 1e-5)** against idea 171's +0.0642, and **capture of the oracle falls 67.1% -> 23.8%** (oracle +0.1629). It is not one finding across families: **ETF +0.0646 (t +5.19, capture 40.3%), SMALL +0.0473 (t +2.27 but sign p 0.111, n.s., 28.8%), U56 +0.0006 (t +0.02, capture 0.3%)**. Books inside a family are correlated draws, so the pooled t overstates — the same criticism idea 175 levelled at idea 171, now applying to this run too. **(2) The two questions the idea asks: Q1 YES, Q2 NO.** Monthly is still the IS pick in **91 of 115 books (79.1%)** — 65.3% SMALL, 81.8% U56, 97.0% ETF — but it is the **OOS argmax in only 12.2%**; the modal OOS argmax is **6W (60.9% overall, 97.0% on both U56 and ETF)**, and M leads OOS only on the small family (28.6%). The selector reproduces idea 171's *pick* and misses the *optimum by one grid step*, which is where the capture went. Mean OOS Sharpe ladder (ALL 115): D 0.621 / 2D 0.662 / W 0.680 / 2W 0.689 / M 0.756 / **6W 0.780** / Q 0.539 — humped with an interior argmax (P2 hit), but 6W is one point from the ladder edge, so **idea 183's anchor-position caveat applies to this run's own 6W reading** and is queued as idea 187 rather than asserted. **(3) The decision number for idea 107, and the run's real content: the pre-registered CONSTANT beats the fit that mostly picks it.** Every cadence priced as a constant against the incumbent W, no selector anywhere: **M vs W = +0.0761 pooled (t +6.71), SMALL +0.0978 (t +3.91), U56 +0.0469 (t +7.24), ETF +0.0730 (t +6.82)** — positive and significant on all three families and roughly **2x SEL-SHARPE's +0.0388**. 6W is larger on the large-cap families (U56 +0.1640, ETF +0.1600) but n.s. on small (+0.0163, t +0.81) and Q is a cliff there (U56 -0.2472, ETF -0.2914), so **M is the only ladder point positive and significant on all three**. Mechanism for the U56 null, decomposed post-hoc and labelled as such: SEL-SHARPE's **27 M picks earn +0.0531 each (+1.433 total) and its 4 Q picks lose -0.3368 each (-1.347)** — the entire family result is the selector's Q tail, and a pre-registered constant cannot land on Q. This is the sixth consecutive project instance of an IS selector failing to earn its complexity (ideas 110/132/151/166/171/174), and the first that names the alternative precisely: read the mode once and write it into RULES (queued as idea 189, generalising the test to all five of idea 171's dials). **(4) Rule 8 walk-forward**, cadence chosen on the IS window only and 2017-2026 read once, mean OOS Sharpe over the 115 books: CONST-W 0.680 -> SEL-SHARPE 0.719 -> **constant-M 0.756** -> ORACLE 0.843 (RULES v1 u56 OOS 7.73%/0.747/-13.83% and small 6.35%/0.492/-36.12%; SPY OOS 15.45%/0.882/-33.72%). **(5) Both KEEP paths on all 805 ladder rows: 4a 195, 4b 59 — every 4b pass is in the U56 family; SMALL is 0 of 343 (and only 2 of 343 clear 4a) and ETF is 0 of 231**, a tenth reproduction of idea 136. Fixed-panel 4b passes are U56 @ 2D (12.01%/1.0557/-17.50%, 15.1x/yr), @ W (12.86%/1.1075/-18.21%, 9.5x/yr) and @ M (**14.76%/1.2081/-19.58%, halves 1.219/1.206, OOS 16.71%/1.2866/-19.58%, 4.3x/yr**) — the last reproduces idea 171's memo'd by-product to 3 dp. Per idea 144 all three are **re-cadencings of idea 2's existing book, not new KEEPs**, and none is proposed. Most-violated 4b bar over failing rows: CAGR 703, H1 624, H2 496, OOS 478, DD 304. **(6) Predictions: 5 of 7 hit; P4 and P6 MISSED and are reported as failures** — monthly IS the small-panel IS pick (65.3%, I predicted it would not be), and RANDOM was not negative on every family (U56 +0.0025). Caveats carried, not buried: current-constituent survivorship on all three panels (idea 54) — the paired comparison is unaffected, every level is not, and the small panel's numbers here are from a list containing no delistings; idea 38's calendar-day index makes a 'bar' a calendar day on U56/ETF after 2014-09-17, so D and 2D there rebalance on some non-trading days; t+1 execution only (idea 126). Memo `research/backtests/2026-09-05_does-cadence-skill-survive-a-second-corpus_cloud.memo.md` carries the exact RULES wording for a monthly cadence clause; **RULES.md, scan.py, bot.py and baseline.py untouched.** Ideas 187/188/189 queued. Script `research/backtests/2026-09-05_does-cadence-skill-survive-a-second-corpus_cloud.py`; artefacts `.console.txt`, `.ladder.csv`, `.choices.csv`, `.paired.csv`, `.constants.csv`, `.walkforward.csv`, `.keep.csv`.

- 2026-09-05 (cloud, idea 186 the-null-column-for-instruments-that-are-not-keyed-tilts) — **ANSWERED: the matched null for an OVERLAY is defined, validated and priced. PROTOCOL clause 11 extends to overlays as a REPORT-ONLY column carrying a stated turnover caveat; as a SELECTION GATE it is a KILL. Rules unchanged; no new book; no KEEP candidate.** Idea 181 built clause 11's null for a KEYED TILT and explicitly left the sleeve, entry-budget and drawdown-control constructions un-priced. **The definition this run supplies:** an overlay is a pair (s, A) — an ON indicator evaluated at the book's rebalance dates plus an action applied while on — and its matched null is the SAME action on a **circular rotation** of s. Rotation is the right null because it preserves the on-share and the episode structure exactly and destroys only *when* the overlay fires; a permutation shatters episodes and a re-drawn state variable changes the construction rather than the timing. Corpus: 3 panels (U56, BROAD136, SMALL439 — the 483-name sub-$2B panel with the 44 `max_1d_move >= 1.0` tickers dropped) x 3 families (DDCTL drawdown control, BUDGET entry/turnover budget, SLEEVE conditional TLT/GLD/UUP) x 3 thresholds x 2 depths x 2 cost rungs x (1 real + 20 rotations) = **2268 rows, 108 real**; base book fixed at idea 2's top-20 EW composite, no vol scaler, gross 0.75, weekly, t+1; both cost rungs derived exactly from a single 0 bps run via the engine's own turnover series. Four reproduction controls asserted before any new number: `fast_backtest` == `engine.backtest` (2.8e-17), the cost identity (2.8e-17), CAND-20 weights == idea 78/171's `weights_cand` (**0.0e+00**), and the null itself validated — **on-share preserved in 2160 of 2160 draws and the circular switch count in 2160 of 2160**. DDCTL uses a TRAILING 252-day high rather than a running high-water mark, because idea 93 established that HWM re-entry is absorbing. **(1) The null is exact where it matters and NOT exact everywhere, and the run says which.** Realised turnover matches to **1.25% mean (DDCTL) and 0.82% (SLEEVE)** but only **25.4% mean and 213.8% max for BUDGET-skip**, because suppressing a rebalance genuinely changes trading. Idea 186's phrasing — "the same on-share AND turnover" — is therefore achievable for overlays that scale or reallocate weights and **not** for one that suppresses a trade, and the proposed clause publishes the gap instead of hiding it. **(2) As a REPORTING column it is informative rather than bookkeeping: 17 of 108 real overlays clear (15.7% on Sharpe, 9.3% on drawdown), about half idea 181's 32.8% for keyed tilts, and it separates the three constructions cleanly — DDCTL 15/36 (42%), BUDGET 2/36 (6%), SLEEVE 0 of 36 (0%).** The reading is sharper than the count: **all 15 clearing DDCTL points have NEGATIVE dSharpe, -0.19 to -0.52** — the drawdown control's effect is distinguishable from random timing and reliably harmful, an independent confirmation of idea 93 on a different construction — while **the conditional sleeve's entire effect (dSharpe -0.188..+0.032) sits inside a null band whose max averages 0.176**, i.e. an equally-frequent randomly-timed sleeve delivers the same thing, including the drawdown improvement that makes the sleeve attractive (u56 ma=200/f=0.5: MaxDD -14.07% against the base's -18.21% and OOS Sharpe 1.2333 against 1.1775, and it does not clear). **527 of the committed LEADERBOARD's 3330 data rows mention a sleeve**; 54 mention a drawdown control or trailing stop and 11 an entry/turnover budget. **(3) The clause moves no verdicts** — idea 181's Q2, asked of overlays: the 4b verdict is identical in **91.5%** of the 2160 real-vs-null swaps and 4a in 83.4%, and a **randomly-timed overlay clears 4b MORE often than the real one (20.7% of null rows against 16.7% of real rows; 4a 25.5% vs 22.2%)**, the same direction idea 181 found for tilts. **Every one of the 18 real overlay rows that clears 4b is inside its own null band** (17 on u56, 1 on broad), so the clause would flag 18 of 18 overlay-based 4b passes as noise while changing none of them. **(4) Rule 8, the overlay point chosen on <= 2016-12-31 and 2017-2026 read once, 18 cells: a KILL of the fit and of the gate.** S0 do-nothing 0.7766 mean OOS Sharpe; S1 IS-Sharpe argmax 0.7334, paired **-0.0432 (t -2.00), winning 6 of 18**; S2 the same argmax gated on the clause read on the IS window 0.7405, paired **-0.0361 (t -2.62), winning 0 of 18**, abstaining in 11 cells and agreeing with S1 in only 3. That is the seventh consecutive project result (ideas 110/132/151/166/171/174/175) that an IS-fitted selector loses to doing nothing, and the second — after idea 181 — that the null clause fails as a gate. **(5) Both KEEP paths on all 2268 rows: real 4a 24/108 and 4b 18/108; SMALL439 is 0 of 36 on both paths at both cost rungs** (eleventh reproduction of idea 136). Per idea 144 an overlaid book is the same book with an instrument on it, so nothing here is proposed. 4 of 108 cells are inert by construction — u56 DDCTL at D=0.15 never fires and its dSharpe is exactly 0.0 — and are reported as such rather than counted as evidence. **(6) All 7 pre-registered predictions HIT**, including P3 (SLEEVE clears least) and P4 (DDCTL clears most), which were written before the sign of DDCTL's effect was known. Caveats carried, not buried: current-constituent survivorship on all three panels (idea 54) is inherited identically by real and null draws so the comparison is unaffected and the levels are not; only J distinct rotations exist and neighbouring offsets are correlated, so the clause's nominal one-sided 4.8% size is approximate; a rotation cannot move the sample's crises, which makes the clause a WEAKER test the higher an overlay's on-share (BUDGET fires on 53-93% of dates and clears 2/36; DDCTL fires on 4.6-40.5% and clears 15/36) — which is why on-share must be published beside it (queued as idea 191); idea 38's calendar-day index and idea 126's t+1-only execution carry over. Memo `research/backtests/2026-09-05_the-null-column-for-instruments-that-are-not-keyed-tilts_cloud.memo.md` carries the proposed clause 11b wording; **RULES.md, scan.py, bot.py and baseline.py untouched.** Ideas 190/191/192 queued. Script `research/backtests/2026-09-05_the-null-column-for-instruments-that-are-not-keyed-tilts_cloud.py`; artefacts `.console.txt`, `.grid.csv`, `.null.csv`, `.clause.csv`, `.walkforward.csv`, `.keep.csv`.
- 2026-09-05 (cloud, idea 177 publish-the-failing-bar-not-the-required-gross) — **ANSWERED, and it is a rare YES: the 4b FAILING-BAR string is the first proposed LEADERBOARD column in this project that carries real decision content. REPORT-ONLY clause 5b proposed; not a KEEP, no book promoted, RULES untouched.** Idea 165 spent ~2000 backtests on `g_req` and re-labelled ≤2.3% of CAGR-floor KILLs; this column costs nothing — every backtest already computes it. Idea 165's corpus REBUILT NOT READ (3 panels x 7 keys x 9 shares = 189 genuine weight paths x 2 cost rungs = 378 books, weekly, t+1, gross 0.75 `norm`) plus 4 repair instruments (GUP raise gross / GDN cut gross / WIDE more names / SLOW slower cadence) x 2 dials x 189 paths = **1701 genuine backtests in 2056 s**, no series anywhere produced by rescaling another (idea 165 showed gross is not an exact lever under engine.py). Exactly 2 tuned params (naming rule x 3, dial strength x 2), all 6 grid points printed; panels, keys, shares, cost rungs and the 4 instruments are carried/reported axes. **Reproduction 2/2 EXACT before any new number was read:** the cost identity (25 bps rung derived as `r0 − turnover·bps/1e4` from one 0 bps run) at **0.000e+00** against genuine re-runs, and 378 of 378 rows against idea 165's published .grid.csv (n 0.0, CAGR 9.71e-17, Sharpe/H1/H2/OOS_Sharpe 2.22e-16, MaxDD 8.33e-17). **(1) The column is not degenerate: 55 of 378 books pass full-sample 4b and of the 323 failures 84.8% fail ≥2 bars** (136 fail all five), over 19 distinct strings; the three naming rules disagree sharply (CANON names H1 in 238 and OOS in 0; TIGHT names H2 in 166; TIGHTZ names DD in 107), so 'first-named' is a real parameter and not a formatting choice. **(2) THE RESULT: at the MILD repair dial the corpus-modal bar1→instrument map scores 0.885 against a 0.623 base rate — +26.2pp at permutation p<0.0005 — under ALL THREE naming rules (n=61 fixable books); on all 323 failing books TIGHTZ scores 0.616 vs 0.368, +24.8pp, p<0.0005.** The map is stable and mechanically sensible: `CAGR→SLOW, DD→GDN, H1→SLOW, H2→WIDE`. The bar-clearing table proves the mechanism rather than inferring it: P(instrument clears the bar | the book fails it) is CAGR 0.305 GUP / 0.329 SLOW / **0.000 GDN**, DD 0.287 GDN / 0.005 SLOW, H1 0.441 SLOW / **0.000 GDN** — the five bars are NOT five readings of one quantity, each names a different lever and the wrong lever clears it essentially never. **(3) The boundary, reported not buried: at the STRONG dial the map collapses to 'WIDE everywhere' with lift +0.0pp and p 1.000** — a large enough change to any dial dominates every book regardless of what failed — so the column is diagnostic only for SMALL changes and is never a gate. **(4) Rule 8 (361 cells, bar1 and the map fitted on IS ≤2016-12-31, 2017–2026 read ONCE): the project's eight-run do-nothing streak BREAKS — but to a pre-registered CONSTANT, not to a selector.** S2 ('always rebalance monthly') +0.1257 mean OOS Sharpe (t +18.56, 303W/58L; 0.8113/12.19%/−28.74% vs S0's 0.6856/9.58%/−26.17%) beats S1 the IS-fitted map +0.1153 (t +16.96) at EVERY naming rule and EVERY dial, exactly as ideas 175/189 predict, and S3 random is +0.0328. **The Sharpe gain is bought with drawdown and BOTH KEEP counts FALL: OOS 4a 107→69 and 4b 67→51** — on the bars that decide real capital, doing nothing still wins. At the STRONG dial every arm loses S0 (S2 −0.0301, t −4.01, OOS 4b **0 of 361**). Benchmarks over the same window: SPY OOS 15.45%/0.8820/−33.72%; RULES v1 @10bps u56 7.73%/0.7471/−13.83%, broad 5.94%/0.5763/−21.19%, small 7.92%/0.5807/−32.84%. **Predictions P1 HIT, P2 HIT, P3 MISS (reading (A) DIAGNOSTIC survives), P4 SPLIT, P5 MISS, P6 MISS — 2 of 6, reported as they fell.** Survivorship carried: all three panels are current constituents and the small panel is the sub-$2B screen with the 44 `max_1d_move ≥ 1.0` tickers dropped, so every CAGR here is optimistic and no level is achievable. Script `research/backtests/2026-09-05_publish-the-failing-bar-not-the-required-gross_cloud.py`; `.result.md` alongside. Ideas 193/194 unaffected; **RULES.md, scan.py, bot.py and baseline.py untouched.**
- 2026-09-05 (cloud, idea 194 abstention-is-the-only-thing-that-ever-helps) — **ANSWERED, and idea 194's hypothesis is HALF RIGHT IN THE STRONGEST POSSIBLE WAY: abstention is EXACTLY a dilution toward doing nothing and NEVER a source of gain. As a KEEP path or a rule-8 arm every gate is a KILL. Report-only rule-8 clause 8b proposed; no new book; RULES untouched.** 378-book corpus (3 panels x 7 keys x 9 shares x 2 cost rungs, weekly, t+1, gross 0.75 `norm`) rebuilt by IMPORTING idea 177's `build_panels()`/`build_base()`, so the corpus under test is literally the code that produced the corpus in the run beside it. CELL = (panel, cost rung, share) = **54 cells**; MENU = the 7 keys; CONTROL = NONE (the composite with no cross-sectional tilt — the natural 'apply no key'); CANDIDATES = the other 6 including idea 159's RND scramble. Exactly 2 tuned params (abstention threshold τ ∈ {−inf, 0.00, 0.05, 0.10, 0.20} on the gate's IS-Sharpe margin over the control, and the IS selector ∈ {IS_SHARPE, IS_CALMAR, IS_MAXDD, IS_CAGR, IS_4B}) = **25 gates, every grid point printed**, plus S0 do-nothing and RANDOM as controls. **Reproduction 2/2 EXACT:** corpus vs idea 165's published .grid.csv (n 0.0, CAGR 9.71e-17, Sharpe/H1/H2/OOS_Sharpe 2.22e-16, MaxDD 8.33e-17), and **[c] the ABSTAIN-ALWAYS identity at 0.000e+00** over all 54 cells. **(1) THE FIRST ANSWER IS ARITHMETIC, NOT A MEASUREMENT.** In this project's own convention a gate that admits nothing HOLDS the cell's control book, and S0 IS that control book, so on an abstained cell the paired difference is identically zero: a pure abstain-always arm is not an approximation of do-nothing, **it IS do-nothing**. Hence `gain(G) = a·0 + (1−a)·c` where a is the abstention rate and c the mean choice effect on picked cells — **and the identity holds to 3.47e-18 across all 25 gates**. 'How much of the gate's gain is explained by its abstention rate alone' therefore has an exact answer: **none of it as a source, all of it as a scaling.** **(2) Abstention is pure dilution — 5 of 5.** Per selector, sign(slope of gain in a) = −sign(c₀) in every one: IS_MAXDD c₀ −0.1258 → slope **+0.1450, R² 0.961**; IS_CAGR c₀ +0.0145 → slope −0.0277, R² 0.810; IS_SHARPE c₀ +0.0099 → slope −0.0191, R² 0.599; IS_CALMAR and IS_4B the same sign at low R². **But POOLED across the 25 gates the R² of gain on a alone is 0.002** — the rate explains nothing across gates because c is not constant, so idea 194's literal wording is FALSE and the identity is what is true. **(3) Is the abstention CHOICE skilful? Matched-rate random-abstention null (abstain in the same NUMBER of cells, chosen at random, 2000 draws): 5 of 19 testable gates escape — and the DIRECTION is the finding.** All three ABOVE-band gates are IS_MAXDD, the worst selector in the run: its gate claws −0.1258 up to −0.0087 (p 0.000 / 0.008 / 0.017) and **never above zero**. Both BELOW-band gates are the DECENT selectors at τ=0.20 — IS_SHARPE gain −0.0104 vs null [−0.0084,+0.0116] p 0.020, IS_CAGR −0.0119 vs [−0.0078,+0.0133] p 0.005 — i.e. at a high threshold the gate declines in exactly the cells its selector would have won. **A gate never manufactures a gain; at best it undoes its own selector's damage, approaching do-nothing from below.** **(4) Rule 8 (everything fitted ≤2016-12-31, 2017–2026 read ONCE, 54 cells, 27 arms): the NINTH consecutive do-nothing win.** S0 mean OOS Sharpe **0.7195** (10.31% CAGR, −25.71% MaxDD); best of all 27 arms IS_CAGR ungated 0.7340 (**+0.0145, t +1.09**, 33W/21L) — insignificant; worst IS_MAXDD ungated 0.5937 (−0.1258, t −6.67, 9W/45L); RANDOM 0.6812 (−0.0383, t −1.91). Every one of the 25 gates lies within [−0.0219, +0.0145] of S0. Both KEEP paths on the OOS window: **S0 4a 17/54 and 4b 12/54**, max over all arms 4b 15/54 — **no arm is promoted**. Benchmarks: SPY OOS 15.45%/0.8820/−33.72%; RULES v1 @10bps u56 7.73%/0.7471/−13.83%, broad 5.94%/0.5763/−21.19%, small 7.92%/0.5807/−32.84%. **(5) The record back-fill** (idea 194's 'for every published selector in the record'): 74 `*.walkforward.csv` scanned, **only 18 carry a recoverable abstention schema**, 74 published selector arms recovered — mean abstention rate 0.364, 50 abstain at all and 3 abstain always; OOS gain vs each file's own control mean +0.0349 but **median −0.0294 with only 24 of 74 positive**, and among the 50 abstainers `gain = +0.0669 − 0.0451·a` at **R² 0.005** — the same null as the fresh experiment. That 56 of 74 files record no abstention signal at all is itself the argument for the column. **Predictions P1 HIT, P2 HIT, P3 MISS (informatively — the escapes point the other way), P4 MISS, P5 HIT, P6 HIT.** Proposed report-only rule-8 wording **8b**: a rule-8 arm that declines to pick holds the control, so abstain-always is identically do-nothing; report every gate's ABSTENTION RATE beside its OOS gain and read the gain as (1−a)·c; a gate may only be claimed to add value if it beats a MATCHED-RATE RANDOM-ABSTENTION null at the same a. Survivorship carried (current-constituent panels; small = sub-$2B screen less the 44 `max_1d_move ≥ 1.0` tickers, joined never-selectable SPY) and the 54 cells share three price panels so every t-stat is optimistic — the permutation null is the inference this run leans on. Script `research/backtests/2026-09-05_abstention-is-the-only-thing-that-ever-helps_cloud.py`; `.result.md` alongside. **RULES.md, scan.py, bot.py and baseline.py untouched.**
- 2026-09-05 (lane C, idea 208 audit-every-committed-null-for-a-salted-seed) — **ANSWERED, and the answer is worse than the question.** The census is small and the fix is one line: an AST audit of all 166 committed `.py` (165 parse) finds **5 `hash()` sites, 4 of them seeds, in 2 scripts** — idea 191's `the-on-share-column_cloud.py` (lines 435, 580) and `the-share-at-which-ranking-stops-paying_B.py` (536, 581). The site that matters reaches the RNG through a **locally defined helper's `seed` parameter** (`rotations(J, N_NULL, SEED + hash((pan.name, fam, thr)) % 10_000)`), so a `default_rng(` grep misses the corpus's single largest exposure; a two-child-process probe confirms a str-bearing hash differs per process while `hash(3)` and `hash((1,2,3))` do not. **Exposure: 612 of 10,472 published draw-derived cells (5.8%) and 540 of 4,628 clause verdicts (11.7%).** By-product: 0 of 34 randomness-using scripts draw unseeded, so this is the only hole of its kind. **The re-draw is exact, not sampled:** idea 191 draws 20 of the J−1 = 974 circular rotations, so enumerating the WHOLE population (30 U56 configs × 975 × 2 cost rungs = **29,250 genuine backtests**) makes the draw law closed-form, `P(clears) = C(K,20)/C(974,20)`. Idea 191's 60 U56 real rows reproduce first at **max |Δ| 1.4e-15** through its own code. Then: **0 of 60 published cells clear with probability 1, 10 with probability 0, and 50/60 = 83.3% are UNDETERMINED**; E[# clearing] 12.04 ± 1.61 against 10 published; **expected verdict flips vs the published draw 6.00/60 = 10.0%**, worst cell P(flip) 0.864; two independent 20-draw seeds disagree on **8.7%** of cells — the exact counterpart of idea 201's sampled 17.8%. The band's own 5–95% draw range is **98.5% of the median band**, and the undetermined zone is 0.3070 wide on a mean |dSharpe| of 0.0862 — **356% of the effect being tested**; only 10 of 60 cells have P outside [0.1, 0.9]. **So a correct seed buys reproducibility, not a verdict: the 20-draw band is the disease and the salt is the symptom.** Rule 8 prices it: pick on ≤2016 within family by largest IS margin, read 2017→ once, under 200 independent seed regimes — **23.7% of (cell, seed) draws pick a different overlay**, within-cell OOS Sharpe range up to **0.3832**, best seed 1.177 / worst **0.826** (OOS CAGR 14.7% vs **7.2%**) against the do-nothing control **1.177 / 14.5% / −18.2%**, SPY 0.882 / 15.5% / −33.7%, RULES v1 0.399 / 3.8% / −14.2%. **0 of 6 cells beat do-nothing (best cell mean dOOS −0.0026, pooled −0.0738, best single seed +0.0000) — the twelfth consecutive do-nothing win.** KEEP paths on the 60 real rows: 4a 7/60, 4b 27/60, of which **0 clear their own null with probability 1**, 18 undetermined, 9 never, and **8 are the inert control book** (on-share 0, idea 200's defect reproduced from an independent direction) — no 4b pass on this grid is defensible as an overlay effect. No new book proposed; PROTOCOL.md and RULES.md untouched (rule 6). **Proposals: (5) forbid `hash()` of a str or of a tuple containing one as a seed — use an int literal, `zlib.crc32(key.encode())` or an int-built `SeedSequence`, and print seeds; (11b) publish `clears` only beside `P(clears)` or as UNDETERMINED, and where the null population is enumerable, enumerate it (975 backtests per configuration here) instead of drawing a band.** 5 of 5 pre-registered predictions hit. Caveats carried: survivorship (U56 current constituents, levels biased upward), correlated neighbouring rotations (the enumeration is exact for the draw law, not for the clause's power), BROAD136/SMALL439 not re-drawn, the MaxDD mean range ratio is a divide-by-zero artefact of the 8 inert cells (median 67.9% is the usable figure).

- 2026-09-05 (cloud, idea 207 COMPANION RUN — clause-11b-draw-count-by-enumeration) — **A second cloud lane answered idea 207 the same day from a 400-rotation sampled pool (`2026-09-05_how-many-draws-does-clause-11b-need_cloud`, commit f709548) while this enumeration was in flight; both claimed the idea while it was open. This entry is the companion, not a replacement: the same 90 configurations with the rotation population ENUMERATED IN FULL — 84,600 genuine backtests -> 169,020 null rows on all three panels, 1687 s on 4 cores — so every statement is a closed-form property of a known finite population rather than an estimate. Rules unchanged; no book; no KEEP.** Reproduction before any new number: `fast_backtest` == `engine.backtest` at 1.4e-17/2.1e-17/2.8e-17, CAND-20 weights **0.000e+00** on all three panels, RULES v1 on U56 0.66418 exact, idea 191's **180 of 180** published real rows at **9.7e-17**, and idea 208's 60 U56 cells identical on 176 of 180 (K,N) pairs — the 4 disputes being cells whose real effect is 0 to machine precision with 169-352 tied rotations and a largest disputed gap of **2.220e-16**, a tie-breaking convention rather than a different population. **(1)** Using every rotation in turn as a pseudo-real (169,020 per row), the incumbent max-of-n rule's measured size is **1/(n+1) EXACTLY** — 0.04762 / 0.01961 / 0.00990 / 0.00498 to five decimals — so the sibling run's inference from band drift is confirmed in closed form: n is the test's alpha, not its precision. **(2) Under the incumbent form more draws are strictly WORSE**: expected disagreement with the enumerated truth rises **0.0742 -> 0.1009 -> 0.1392 -> 0.1739** on Sharpe and the undetermined zone WIDENS (0.1031 -> 0.1574 at eps = 0.10). **(3) At a fixed level** — the permutation p-value `(#{|d_null| >= |d_real|} + 1)/(n+1) <= alpha`, the exact-inference form of the sibling's Q95 proposal and identical to the incumbent max-of-20 at alpha = 0.05 — flip vs truth falls **0.0742 -> 0.0516 -> 0.0312 -> 0.0238**, two-seed disagreement 0.1003 -> 0.0313, and the zone falls from **104.0% to 20.6% of the mean |dSharpe| (0.0991)**. **(4) Two corrections to the sibling run, both in the direction of MORE draws.** Its caveat states J ~ 670/600; the enumeration counts **J-1 = 974 / 974 / 869**, so a 400-draw pool is 41-46% of the population, not 60-67%. And its 'K=200 doubles the cost for no measured gain' rests on ONE disjoint block pair per configuration (its own P5 miss): under the exact law n=200 is 24% better than n=100 on flip rate and 36% better on zone width, so **100 is a floor, not an optimum**, and the complete population — 2.4x a 400-draw pool, 4.9x a 200-draw band — is exact. A single 20-draw realisation misses its own clause's expectation by 2-6pp (sibling's realised 15.6%/6.1% against the exact 17.7%/11.9%), which is the instability both runs were commissioned to measure showing up in the measurement itself. **(5) Independent cross-checks that agree exactly: 4a 37/180, 4b 28/180, SMALL439 0/60, S0 do-nothing 10.22%/0.7766/-23.53%, and 0 of 8 clause-gated rule-8 settings beating do-nothing** (best MAXN/200 -0.0131 t -14.0, level-alpha/200 -0.0946, ungated -0.0560; SPY OOS 15.45%/0.8820/-33.72%) — the **thirteenth consecutive do-nothing win**, with MAXN's apparent improvement in n exposed as idea 194's dilution identity: its abstention rate climbs 0.531 -> 0.851, so it improves only by acting less. Of the 28 4b passes, **0 clear their own null with probability 1 at n <= 50** and 22-23 can never clear. **By-product: ties are structural** — mean **129.2 of 974** rotations tie the real effect exactly on drawdown (84.2 on Sharpe, 95.8 on IS-Sharpe), 75 of 180 cells affected, which is the resolution floor of any clause built on that statistic and is not reported anywhere in the corpus. Full numbers, all 24 grid points and the reconciliation table in `research/backtests/2026-09-05_clause-11b-draw-count-by-enumeration_cloud.result.md`; PROTOCOL.md, RULES.md, scan.py, bot.py and baseline.py untouched (rule 6). 3 of 5 predictions hit — P3 and P5 both missed in the direction of the level-alpha form being BETTER than predicted, and idea 208's '83.3% undetermined' headline is shown to be U56-and-band-specific (25.0% on the three-panel corpus).

- 2026-09-06 (cloud, idea 188 COMPANION RUN — can-a-panel-property-choose-the-cadence) — **A sibling cloud lane answered idea 188 on 2026-09-05 while this run was in flight (the queue read as open when this lane claimed it at 00:00 UTC). This is the companion, not a replacement: it measures the same two properties independently, agrees with both, and then takes the step idea 77's clause actually requires — turning each property into a rule-8 SELECTOR. That step is a KILL. Rules unchanged; no book; neither KEEP path claimed.** Idea 175's corpus, cadence mask, backtest and KEEP evaluators imported verbatim; **its published 805-row ladder reproduces 805/805 at 2.2e-16** and its 28-cell constants table at 8.3e-17, including the split under test (SMALL M +0.0978 / 6W +0.0163; U56 6W +0.1640; ETF 6W +0.1600). **(1) Signal decay is not merely absent as a mechanism, it points the other way.** Cross-sectional rank IC of the composite against forward return, eligible names only, sampled every 21 bars so windows do not overlap: on U56 the IC RISES monotonically **+0.0639@21d -> +0.1481@126d (t 2.76 -> 6.59)** and on ETF +0.0470 -> +0.1291, while on SMALL it is **+0.0045 at h=21 with |t| <= 1.55 at every horizon on the grid**. The large-cap signal does not decay over any horizon tested; the small panel has no cross-sectional signal to decay. The IR-optimal horizon `argmax IC(h)/sqrt(h)` lands at h=5d on all three fixed panels, so the statistic that ought to select the cadence selects the ladder's fastest point everywhere and cannot produce the split. **(2) Holding-episode length (idea 76's measure, made cadence-free on daily top-20 membership) orders the families the right way — SMALL 18.8d < U56 31.3d < ETF 38.8d (fixed panels 9.0 / 17.2 / 22.9) — but is not a clause:** alone it explains R2 0.173 of the M-minus-6W gap, with h* 0.204, against **0.445 for the family dummies alone**, and adding the dummy on top of both properties still lifts R2 to 0.452. Spearman(gap, episode_days) is -0.386 pooled but +0.072 / +0.141 / -0.008 within ETF / SMALL / U56 — the correlation is entirely between-family, the signature of a re-labelling. Turnover explains 1.5%, confirming idea 175's aside. **(3) Rule 8, the run's real content: writing the constant down beats every fitted panel property.** With each property computed on IS <= 2016-12-31 and 2017-2026 read once: ORACLE 0.8427 / const **6W 0.7797** / const **M 0.7559** / EPISODE 0.7384 / SEL-SHARPE 0.7186 / DECAY-IR 0.6966 / const W 0.6798 / DECAY-IC 0.6325 / DECAY-HALF 0.5688; **all six DECAY settings and the EPISODE rule lose to M (best -0.0175, t -1.32, winning 38% of books) and all seven lose to 6W** (SPY OOS Sharpe 0.8820; RULES v1 7.73%/0.7471 on U56 and 6.35%/0.4923 on SMALL). Third reading of 'the constant beats the fit' after ideas 175 and 189, and the first where the fitted object is a panel property rather than a performance selector. **(4) One correction to the sibling run, on a number that changes its reading:** its IC t-statistics are computed on fully overlapping h-day windows sampled every bar, which inflates t by about sqrt(h). Its SMALL439 row reads t 5.22/5.76/3.58 at h=5/10/21; the same IC point estimate at h=21 (**+0.0064 here vs its +0.0070**) carries **t 0.75** non-overlapping, and 3.58/sqrt(21) = 0.78 accounts for the whole difference. So 'SMALL peaks at h=10 and is dead by h=30' over-reads a null — it was never significant — while the large-cap significance survives the correction and the CONTRAST both runs report is real. **(5) KEEP paths across all 805 (book, cadence) points: 4a 195/805, 4b 59/805, and every one of the 59 is U56** (M 18, 2D 12, W 10, 6W 10, D 5, 2W 4, Q 0) with ETF 0/231 and SMALL 0/343 — idea 136's small-panel wall again, and Q passes neither path anywhere. **Conclusion for idea 77: the universe clause cannot be written on this evidence.** Idea 175's split is real and reproduces to 1e-16, no measurable property recovers it, and a single global constant must therefore trade the families off — 6W is the better pooled choice (+0.0238 over M, 73% of books) but the worse choice on the one family where M was significant. Implementation note recorded for anyone reusing idea 76's measure: run-lengths must be encoded PER NAME; a 2-D diff plus a flat nonzero splices runs across names and inflates the mean by two orders of magnitude (3974d instead of 9.0d on SMALL439) — this run's first pass had that defect and the committed version flattens column-major with a False pad per column. Full numbers in `research/backtests/2026-09-06_can-a-panel-property-choose-the-cadence_cloud.result.md`; PROTOCOL.md, RULES.md, scan.py, bot.py and baseline.py untouched (rule 6). 2 of 5 predictions hit; P5 was wrong in the most useful direction and is the run's central result.
- 2026-09-06 (lane B, idea 187 is-6W-a-grid-edge-or-a-real-optimum) — **KILL. The idea's dichotomy is false on both horns: 6W is neither a grid edge nor a real optimum, because CADENCE LENGTH IS NOT THE VARIABLE — BLOCK PHASE IS. No RULES change, no KEEP claimed; RULES.md, scan.py, bot.py and baseline.py untouched.** Idea 175's ladder was extended from 7 points to 13 (D/2D/W/2W/M/6W/**7W/8W/2M/10W**/Q/**16W/2Q**) on its 115 books verbatim, 1495 runs, and the 7 shared points reproduce its committed ladder.csv on **all 805 rows at 7.105e-15** with 0 verdict-string and 0 worst-bar mismatches, so old and new points are comparable bar for bar. **THE ARGMAX MOVES (6W -> 2M pooled) AND IS INTERIOR IN 4/4 FAMILY ROWS**, which refutes plain truncation (0 of 7 slower-than-6W points beat 6W on ALL/U56/ETF) — **but the ladder is JAGGED, not humped**, and that is the finding: pooled OOS Sharpe runs 6W **0.780** -> 7W **0.606** -> 8W 0.651 -> 2M **0.826** -> 10W 0.693, so neighbours 1.2 rebalances/yr apart differ by 0.17 of Sharpe; **Q (4.0/yr) is the WORST point on the entire ladder (0.539) while 2Q at HALF Q's frequency beats it (0.646)**; and 6W-minus-7W on U56 is **-0.3108 (t -29.65)**, 1.9x the whole 6W-minus-W gap idea 175 published (+0.1640). No signal-decay mechanism can produce that shape. **A LABELLED POST-HOC CONTROL NAMES THE MECHANISM:** holding cadence FIXED and sweeping the block phase — which weeks the book lands on, fixed by an arbitrary sample-start date and not a tradable choice — gives a mean-OOS-Sharpe spread at **6W alone** of **0.1518 (t 5.41) ALL / 0.2618 (t 11.32) SMALL / 0.3957 (t 34.12) U56 / 0.3862 (t 34.89) ETF**, i.e. **2.13 / 16.05 / 2.41 / 2.41 times the published 6W-minus-W cadence effect**, and on U56/ETF it exceeds the spread across the entire slow zone. **Q returns EXACTLY 0.0000 phase spread on all four families as the negative control** (a k=1 calendar point has one phase by construction), so the non-zero spreads are phase and not sweep noise. **The ladder's 6W is phase 0 of 6 and ranks 1/6 on ALL, U56 AND ETF — the luckiest of six arbitrary alignments — and beats the weekly incumbent at only 2 of its own 6 phases** (the pooled argmax 2M at 1 of 2). **RULE 8 THEN KILLS THE SELECTOR:** SEL-SHARPE falls from idea 175's +0.0388 (t +3.26, capture 23.8%) to **-0.0124 (t -0.68), capture -5.6%, BEHIND the RANDOM control**, losing outright on U56 (**-0.1377, t -6.57, sign p 0.0046**) and with SEL==ORACLE agreement of **5.2% pooled and 0.0% on U56**; the IS pick scatters across M/8W/16W/2Q because the IS argmax chases the same phase noise from a different draw. **Idea 107's evidence base — cadence as the one dial of five with selector skill — was a 4-point-grid artefact, and no cadence constant is writable into RULES.** Both KEEP paths on all 1495 rows: 4a 272, 4b 90, **every 4b pass on U56** (SMALL 0/637, ETF 0/429), 31 of 90 on the six new points, most-violated bar CAGR:1287; the fixed-panel 4b passes (U56 @ 2M/M/W/2D) are the SAME BOOK re-cadenced (idea 144) and **U56 @ 2M is explicitly NOT a candidate** — a 1-of-13 grid pick on the one panel where 4b ever passes, at phase 0 of 2, which loses to weekly at its other phase. Idea 188's family split survives as a sign but not as a constant (SMALL's argmax moves M -> 2M; 5 of 7 slower-than-6W points beat 6W there, best 2M +0.2199 t +10.56). Predictions P1/P2/P3/P4/P7 HIT, P5/P6 MISS — both toward the phase reading. **Proposed for Sunday review, a PROTOCOL clause and not a RULES change: clause 12 — any comparison of two multi-unit-block cadences (every point except D, W, M, Q, which have exactly one phase) must report the effect against that cadence's own phase spread; an advantage smaller than its phase spread is an alignment draw and may not be written into RULES.** Three follow-ups queued (220 back-fill the phase spread over every published cadence claim; 221 is phase sensitivity a predictable book property; 222 the phase-averaged ladder as the honest estimator). The incumbent weekly cadence stays — not because W won, but because nothing here identifies a replacement.
