The sub-panel 4b count peaks at q=0.90 (24 of 72) above q=1.00 (19). **The by-product, recorded not promoted:** `q = 0.90` (equal-weight the top 90% of the eligible set) passes 4b on **U56 at 0/5/10/15 bps** (11.30% / **1.0808** / -16.79%, halves 1.114/1.054, OOS 1.1263, 9.4x/yr) and on **B136 at 0/5/10 bps** (11.14% / 1.0325 / -18.37%, halves 1.148/0.925, OOS 1.0267) — cross-universe at PROTOCOL's own 10 bps, one rung further than q=1.00 on U56 — and dies at 20/15 bps on the **CAGR floor**, so it does NOT clear idea 82's proposed 25 bps bar. Per **idea 144 a re-dialled book is the same book**, so it is idea 72's `EWall` with its worst decile trimmed and goes to review as report-only scoping wording (`.memo.md`), never as a new book. 5/6 predictions hit; the MISS (P2, a negative slope of gross premium in q) is the headline. Caveats: current-constituent survivorship (idea 54) runs AGAINST the arm this run favours — q=1.00 holds exactly the beaten-down cohort a delisting-aware panel would kill — so the true argmax q is if anything lower and "drop the ranking" is the conclusion this bias does not manufacture; the small panel is secondary throughout (inverted gate) and is the only panel where selectivity survives 30 bps; costs are flat linear bps on turnover, not spread-and-impact (idea 126); the 72 sub-panels share one parent so their spread is a sampling band; at q=1.00 the premium is 0 by construction. Follow-ups queued: 227 (census the coverage-vs-ranking split in every published EWall claim), 228 (does the q=0.90 trim hold as a constant on a third corpus and on other dials), 229 (re-quote idea 78's gross-spread family in both units, since the sign depends on which is quoted).
- 2026-09-06 (cloud, idea 155 COMPANION RUN — where-selectivity-and-cost-cross) — **A sibling lane (`2026-09-06_where-selectivity-and-cost-cross_B`, commit e787f48) answered idea 155 the same day while this cloud lane was in flight; both claimed it while it read as open. This entry is the companion, not a replacement, and the two runs agree where it matters, from independent implementations.** Agreements: the argmax at 10 bps is **0.90 on U56 in both runs, with an identical premium of +0.0316**; rule 8's IS window picks **q = 0.15 (U56) and 0.10 (B136) in both runs** and loses to doing nothing; and both find idea 78's premise inverted within a panel — Spearman(q, gross premium) **+0.615 / +0.853** here against lane B's **+0.430 / +0.899** — so "the two curves of opposite sign" is a cross-panel artefact in both readings. Differences, all explained by grid and convention rather than by result: lane B's B136 argmax is **0.95** because its q grid carries that point (this run's grid stops at 0.90); this run screens the small panel (SMALL439, max_1d_move >= 1.0 dropped) where lane B uses idea 78's SMALL484, and **both get argmax 0.30 at every rung**; lane B reads a crossing on the small panel between 20 and 25 bps, which this run's breakeven table renders as >30 bps for every q >= 0.20 on the screened panel. **Lane B contributes one finding this run did not have and it amends this run's memo: the argmax's LOCATION is construction-dependent** (U56 moves to 0.55 under a constant-count-at-equal-cash form, B136 to 1.00), so **q = 0.90 must be quoted as "the top decile", not as a constant**; only the direction is robust across all four constructions. This run contributes the cost-rung monotonicity over 72 seeded sub-panels (mean argmax 0.840 -> 0.955 across 0-30 bps, 20.8% -> 76.4% of draws at exactly 1.00), the breakeven curve c*(q) on five panels, and the coverage-vs-ranking confound in idea 78's `weights_ewall` (426 of 4699 B136 days). No RULES change in either lane; no book promoted.

- 2026-09-06 (lane C, idea 256 every-published-reach-is-a-ladder-edge-until-proven-otherwise) — **ANSWERED, SPLIT: the record's argmaxes ARE grid ends, and widening them is not worth money. No RULES change, no KEEP claimed; RULES.md, scan.py, bot.py and baseline.py untouched.** CENSUS (computed, not asserted): 723 committed CSV artefacts -> 715 after content-hash de-duplication -> **22,443 fully-crossed cells** over 61 scripts and 21 dial names. **87.4% of published argmaxes sit at a grid END** against the 2/L null a random argmax would give (34.4%), excess **+52.9pp**; unit-weighted (script x dial x outcome family, so one 1485-row artefact cannot outvote thirty small ones) 82.8% vs 39.8%, and 127 of 234 units are at an edge in EVERY cell they publish. The split that matters: **66.9% of cells are MONOTONE** (the ladder never turned over, so the published number is the ladder end and nothing else), 20.5% turn but still peak at an end, **12.6% have an interior argmax**. Not a cost-rung artefact — dropping cost/cost_bps/bps still leaves 0.845 against a 0.298 null. The queue's actual question: of the 90 edge grids, **73.3%** have ever been swept wider by another script on the same dial name, **42.2%** by a LATER one, and **26.7% have never been widened by anybody**. EXPERIMENT (rule 8, one tuned parameter — the dial level, chosen on IS <= 2016, 2017-2026 read once): three families on the constant-gross NORM book over u56/broad136/small484 at 10 and 25 bps, 228 grid points, 30 cells. The record-grid pick is at an end in **27/30**, widening moves it out in **13/30**, and the widened pick is itself at the new end in **14/30** — widening relocates the edge about as often as it resolves it. Its price: OOS Sharpe (wide - narrow) mean **+0.0178, t +0.91, 8/30 wins**; `n` pays (+0.181, 6/6 moved, u56 20->56 lifts OOS Sharpe 0.993->1.113), `max_vol` costs (-0.046), `gross` is inert (its whole extended ladder spans 0.034 of OOS Sharpe, and its top end is unwidenable by PROTOCOL rule 2 anyway). KEEP paths on everything: **4b 0/30 for both the narrow and the wide picks**, 4a 7/30 and 4/30. The one finding that survives for capital: **3 of 228 points pass 4b and all three lie outside the record's own grids** (u56 n=20 no vol gate 10.7%/0.993/-19.5%, OOS 1.033; broad136 n=136 EWall 10.7%/1.025/-17.7%, OOS 1.019) — inside the record grids, 0 of 90 points pass — but each is a known record object (EWall; ideas 38/49/232), the IS chooser selects none of them, and no KEEP is claimed. Proposed for Sunday as REPORT-ONLY: print `edge=lo|hi|interior` and the outcome spread across the whole ladder beside every published argmax, so an edge worth 0.03 (`gross`) is legible as noise and an edge worth 0.88 (`n`) as an unfinished sweep.
- 2026-09-06 (cloud, idea 261 back-fill-the-zero-bps-rung-under-every-gross-matched-arm-difference) — **SPLIT: KILL of the queue's premise, CONFIRM of the contamination at roughly half idea 260's rate. Rules unchanged; one report-only clause proposed.** Three tiers. **TIER L (live, exact):** 45 pre-registered gross-matched one-dial arm pairs re-run from scratch on U56 / B136 / SMALL439 (44 tickers with `max_1d_move >= 1.0` dropped first), every arm targeted at 0.75 gross — max realised gross gap across a pair **0.0096** — 16 arms x 3 panels x 3 rungs = **144 points, every one printed and written to `.grid.csv`**. Each book is run ONCE at 0 bps and the other rungs derived as `r(c) = r(0) - turnover*c/1e4`; the identity against a live `backtest()` call is **0.000e+00** on all three panels. Result: **14 of 45 pairs (31.1%) are majority-cost on Sharpe** (`|D(10) - D(0)| > 0.5|D(10)|`), 6 on CAGR, 2 sign flips each — **but 9 of the 45 have `|dSharpe@10| < 0.02`**, i.e. no publishable magnitude to contaminate, and excluding those the rate is **5 of 36 (13.9%)** with one sign flip. The five real instances are named in the leaderboard; the flip is `B136 EWall_M - EWall` **+0.0433 -> -0.0018**. **TIER V (estimator):** the turnover back-fill `Sharpe(0) ~ Sharpe(c) + (c/1e4)*TO/Vol` and `CAGR(0) ~ CAGR(c) + (c/1e4)*TO` is validated against tier L's exact truth on all 96 live points — mean error **-0.00011 Sharpe (max 0.00137)** and **-0.00127 CAGR (max 0.00391)** at 10 bps, 95th-pct |err| 0.00049 / 0.00263. It is a third-decimal operation, so any single-rung leaderboard row carrying a turnover column (**84 of 96 grids do**) is re-readable at 0 bps with no re-run. **TIER R (the record):** all 107 committed `research/backtests/*.grid.csv` scanned, 96 usable. **The queue's premise is wrong** — only **26 of 96 (27.1%)** carry one cost rung or none, **70 carry two or more**, so 5,214 of the 6,183 recoverable gross-matched one-dial differences have an EXACT 0-bps twin obtained by extrapolating through two rungs actually run, with no model at all. Of the **4,858** pairs outside the estimator's error band, **1,162 (23.9%) are majority-cost and 272 (5.6%) change sign** between 0 bps and their published rung; the exact tier alone gives 26.6% / 267. **The exposure is a Sharpe problem, not a CAGR problem:** 814 of 2,116 Sharpe differences (**38.5%**, 8.9% sign flips) against 348 of 2,742 CAGR differences (**12.7%**, 3.0%) — because a Sharpe difference is small relative to the same absolute cost term. **What predicts it:** the two arms' turnover ratio, cleanly — **>= 2x: 54.5% majority-cost; < 2x: 23.5%** — and it concentrates in ONE dial, **CADENCE** (median cost share 0.508, 5/9 majority-cost, both sign flips), against COUNT 0.129, RANKKEY 0.220, GATE200 0.200, VOLCAP 0.251. That validates the 2x-turnover trigger idea 263 proposes and localises the record's exposure to the cadence claims rather than to the count and gate argmaxes that carry most of it. **Rule 8 is the limit on the finding:** `PICK@0` and `PICK@10` differ only in the rung the IS window is scored at and **pick the same arm in 3 of 3 panels** (U56 `FWD20_novolcap`, B136 `FWD20_M`, SMALL439 `FWDVS20`), OOS Sharpe delta **+0.0000** — so the contamination corrupts published MAGNITUDES, not the arm a selector would choose, and the correct remedy is a reporting column, not a decision rule. Do-nothing `ANCHOR` OOS 0.827 > `PICK` 0.818 > `EWALL` 0.807, SPY 0.882, RULES v1 0.605 (the 17th selection-loses instance). **KEEP paths:** 4a 35/144, 4b 29/144 (U56 5/16 and B136 4/16 at 10 bps, SMALL439 **0/16** at every rung); every 4b passer is a known object (EWall, FWD20/40/60, and idea 101's monthly arms), with `U56/FWD20_M` clearing 4b at all three rungs including 25 bps (15.3% / 1.213 / -19.5%, OOS 1.307, 4.8x/yr turnover) but failing on B136 and SMALL439. **No new KEEP-candidate, no memo, no RULES change.** Proposed report-only clause (for Sunday review, not applied here): any published difference between two arms whose turnovers differ by >= 2x must print its 0-bps twin beside the quoted rung — which is idea 263, now with a validated trigger and a validated back-fill. SURVIVORSHIP: all three panels are current constituents; the headline is a ratio of a difference to a difference so a common additive premium largely cancels, but the 4a/4b counts are upper bounds.
